# Differences from the Phase 6 plan

This is the implementation decision log for the active Phase 6 work. It is not a completion report. Audio sample similarity sorting and the XO/Atlas research are excluded from this work. Entries are chronological; see the [current scope audit](#current-scope-audit--2026-10-26) for remaining work rather than treating early checkpoint lists as current status.

## Indexing admission is stricter

Indexing starts with no enrolled roots. Browser visibility and permission to index are separate decisions. Explicit enrollment cannot override the protected-content policy, credential-folder exclusions, or exclusions for repository internals and generated content.

The implementation additionally denies system directory trees and private application configuration (`.config`), alongside the plan's credential locations. Credential/key files are excluded completely from the search inventory rather than merely withholding their embeddings. This avoids persisting secret-related paths and prevents the folder button from creating an index in locations with broad credential exposure. Specific content folders remain eligible, including ordinary folders inside a source-code repository; its `.git` subtree is excluded.

Enrollment and content reads inspect the pinned object's filesystem classification and resolved location, so an ordinary-looking symlink cannot bypass a denial. Root identity and location are retained and revalidated. Core's ordinary-content guard remains authoritative; the search policy adds narrower restrictions without weakening the core guard.

## Standalone model helper and optional Vulkan backend

The model helper adapts only Recall's reviewed MIT C inference shim, through a small standard-library Python JSON-lines process. It does not depend on Recall's crawler, schema, Rust engine, or application lifecycle. Model installation is explicit and checksum-verified at the pinned GGUF revision. Ordinary search startup does not download models or enroll roots.

Prepared media reaches the helper as bounded encoded bytes rather than source pathnames. Guarded readers and external decoders must prepare those bytes in the search coordinator. The helper validates image dimensions and requires bounded 16 kHz mono PCM16 audio. Overlong text/audio is rejected instead of inheriting Recall's silent truncation. The protocol reports finite normalized 768-dimensional float32 output; persistent vector encoding remains a separate index decision.

Vulkan is available as an optional backend in addition to CUDA/CPU. The reused Recall llama artifact has CPU/Vulkan support only. A subsequent workstation check confirmed that the CUDA compiler is installed at `/opt/cuda/bin/nvcc` (CUDA 13.4); the earlier assertion that the compiler was absent was incorrect. After the user's reboot, NVIDIA GPU access works, with loaded and user-space driver versions matching at 615.78.08 and the updated kernel running. The native CUDA/Vulkan/CPU runtime is now built and installed. All three real backend smoke checks passed text/image/audio embeddings, finite normalized 768-dimensional output, a simple relevance comparison and token-budget rejection: `/tmp/clip-search-{cuda,vulkan,cpu}-smoke-20261018.json`. Elapsed checks were 2.126/14.240/4.366 seconds respectively; these are single compatibility runs, not representative warm throughput or retrieval benchmarks. The implementation selects CUDA, then Vulkan, then CPU through fresh owned helpers, checks the backend actually returned, and reports it in index status. Backend changes invalidate the runtime build cache without by themselves invalidating embedding pipeline identity. Installation stages libraries before replacing an idle runtime, with locking and rollback. CPU compute is bounded to four threads.

## Generic question answering and applicability

User clarification: temporary columns accept arbitrary questions, not a specialized statement-extraction workflow. Bank statements are one illustrative test case. Embeddings retrieve evidence; they do not return exact values. Use one additional local generative model for both applicability and answering, rather than adding a separate judge model. Following the user's NuExtract3 suggestion, the primary evaluation compares NuExtract3 Q8_0 with base Qwen3.5-4B Q8_0. NuExtract3 is an Apache-2.0 Qwen3.5-4B fine-tune with author-provided GGUF weights and structured-task framing. Its internal benchmark is promising but does not establish our generic question/applicability behavior. The semantic plan records this distinction and the validation gate.

Applicability belongs to each question/file pair and retains undetermined internally. Not-applicable cells display `-`, including in boolean columns; false, zero, missing answers, incomplete evidence and unsupported media remain distinct. A low similarity score or an unsupported extension is not proof of non-applicability. Type validation and evidence checks happen outside the model.

A bounded text-answer helper is now implemented against the same pinned llama.cpp libraries. It uses non-thinking constrained JSON generation, tokenizes question/content without allowing special chat-role tokens, checks evidence quotations against supplied source text, rejects contradictory states, validates plain decimal/date/boolean types, and converts unsupported absence claims under partial coverage to unknown. Model artifacts have pinned sizes/checksums and are explicitly downloaded. Initial Vulkan synthetic checks: NuExtract3 passed 10/11, base Qwen passed 11/11; NuExtract3 mislabeled competing recipe temperatures as not found rather than ambiguous. The initial prompt also exposed confusion between a false boolean and non-applicability; explicit generic instructions corrected that tested case. These are small text compatibility checks, not acceptance or proof that either model is best.

Expanded comparison: NuExtract3 passed 18/20 in 61.867 seconds; base Qwen passed 20/20 in 72.065 seconds on the same RTX 3090 Vulkan runtime. Reports: `/tmp/clip-answer-nuextract3-smoke-20261018c.json` and `/tmp/clip-answer-qwen35-smoke-20261018b.json`. NuExtract3's additional failure treated embedded malicious instructions as conflicting evidence and abstained as ambiguous; neither failure accepted an incorrect value. The fixtures include generic recipes, agreements, technical prose, event counts, literature interpretation, invoices, care instructions, negative readings, partial dates and prompt injection. Provisional text-worker default remains base Qwen; NuExtract3 remains a development candidate for visual/layout evaluation. No visual advantage, general accuracy percentage or warm throughput follows from these short sequential text runs. A separate CUDA run passed 20/20 in 60.846 seconds: `/tmp/clip-answer-qwen35-cuda-smoke-20261018.json`. Eleven download-free Python contract/provisioning tests pass. Phase 6 remains incomplete.

The Rust coordinator now executes the text-answer worker, and `q` creates up to four per-folder session columns without filtering away other rows or creating a semantic index. Repeated identical questions focus the existing column. Columns expose header sorting, title editing, retry, refresh and removal; a horizontal scroller preserves usable name/metadata widths. Visible rows across columns receive priority, with one cancellable file request at a time. Source revisions and folder objects guard publication/cache reuse, and worker/model/prompt pipeline changes invalidate session answers. Navigation/studio entry cancels pending read work while retaining completed results in memory. Closing search preserves columns; closing the window discards them.

The application validates evidence and types again before accepting helper replies. Decimal sorting uses sign and canonical digit runs rather than floating point, keeps exact currency/unit groups separate in both directions, and preserves prior display order for equal answers. Date precision groups remain separate; offset timestamps compare as instants. Valid values precede pending/missing/error states in either direction. Applicability retains applicable/non-applicable/undetermined independently from answer state; non-applicable renders `-`, while false/zero/empty remain values. Source quotations and explanations are available on hover.

This is the initial readable-text/PDF path, with a 24 KiB extraction budget and 64 source passages per file before the 8,192-token answer-context limit. Partial coverage yields unknown rather than a sortable value or definitive absence. Unsupported images/audio and PDFs lacking usable text are disclosed; vision/OCR answers and larger-document aggregation remain work. The coordinator currently shares one model process between embedding and answering roles, replacing its weights on a role change and pausing background indexing briefly between answer jobs. This bounds GPU residency but may reload weights when interactive searches interrupt a batch; separate residency requires later measured scheduling work.

Private dark UI acceptance passed numeric/boolean answers, numeric header reversal, retaining columns after closing search, folder return and no implicit index: `/tmp/clip-answer-columns-acceptance-20261018c/report.json`. A strengthened light run exposed selection loss on Enter: GDB showed the background response had `fake_primary_click=true` and `clicked=false` when focus returned from query entry (`/tmp/clip-answer-columns-debug-20261018c/files.log`). The list background now requires a physical primary click, and search submission consumes Enter before returning focus. Final strengthened light UI verification passed (`/tmp/clip-answer-columns-debug-20261018e/report.json`), including preserved selection; the debugger observed no selection-clear call. The first attempted fix touched the tree's similarly named focus response; that edit was reverted and the actual list background corrected.

An audit also found that the initial embedding formatter included mutable filenames as titles, contrary to the plan. It now uses `title: none`; the embedding pipeline identifier advances to `search-v2` so old vectors are not mixed with the corrected input policy. Canonical input-hash inference reuse is now implemented; see the reuse checkpoint below. Full-source digest reuse remains outstanding.

## Implementation progress

This is a work ledger, not a reduction of the requested scope:

- Implemented: shared command parser; exact size range boundaries and aliases; modified/created filters; metadata/type/glob exclusions; anchored regex; local name filtering and ranking; ordinary column sort commands; Ctrl+F/leading-colon entry; browser selection/scroll restoration.
- Connected: a bounded background coordinator owns the index and model. The address-bar Index button is red before enrollment, yellow during building or incomplete/error states, and green after the root's accepted work completes. Enrollment goes through a disposable guarded reader and is persisted only after admission. Configured roots start reconciliation on reopening; defaults enroll nothing and create no index.
- Connected: explicit recursive/indexed name queries and literal content queries execute through bounded reader processes. Current plain text is UTF-8; PDF pages use existing extraction and visual fallback. Images produce bounded PNG input, and audio produces bounded mono 16 kHz PCM16 WAV windows. Unsupported formats keep name-only records or an explicit failure/coverage state. Office containers, video extraction, other encodings, and complete format coverage remain work.
- Connected: text/image/audio inference and float16 persistent vectors. Semantic queries apply metadata/scope restrictions before file candidate ranking, keep each file's best matching segment, cap candidates at 1,000, and verify returned sources in bounded reader batches. Changed/unavailable files are omitted with a count. Relevance order survives ordinary browser rebuilds; explicit sorts still replace it. Foreground queries have independent cancellation tokens, so closing or superseding a query stops its read-only work without cancelling accepted indexing enrollment.
- Implemented: separate hard-link paths/root memberships, generation-based inventory, transactional FTS/vector publication, DB revision/pipeline rejection, empty-default configuration, and single-writer ownership. Retirement requires complete enumeration plus a final bounded absence check; confirmed missing directories retire their descendants. Failed enumeration retains inventory. Derived search storage is excluded from its own scan.
- Connected: initial typed session answer columns and the bounded local text-answer worker, with evidence inspection, applicability, exact numeric/date/boolean ordering, cancellation and header actions. Larger-document aggregation, visual answers, memory/coverage budgets and broader question-quality evaluation remain work. The strengthened light UI acceptance now passes, including selection preservation.
- Still required: settings UI for configured always-search locations; precise token-aware segmentation and small-source full-digest reuse; full text/document/video support and evidence snippets; retrieval calibration and result explanations/pagination (hybrid fusion is connected in the later checkpoint); mount/session-aware reconciliation and broader remote/offline recovery acceptance; the remaining answer-column coverage and quality gates above; full settings/maintenance/backup controls; complete performance/coverage and end-to-end gates.

Conservative byte-bounded text chunks are an interim implementation, not a decision to replace the planned tokenizer-aware segmentation. The original best-segment cosine execution path has since been replaced by the hybrid ranker documented below; calibration remains outstanding. Image hash execution and initial text `q` execution are connected; neither establishes complete Phase 6 coverage.

## Owned reader helpers

Metadata enumeration and source preparation run in the application binary's shared internal worker dispatch. Reader requests/replies and source/media sizes are bounded. A reader pins and revalidates the root and source, rejects symlink traversal and nested-mount descent, and passes owned descriptor-backed inputs to existing external tools. Search enumeration does not perform the ordinary browser's TypeScript payload sniff before admission.

Read-only helpers use an opt-in private process group through core's existing command runner. Timeout/cancellation terminates decoder descendants before draining pipes, including a descendant that outlives its helper. Audio probes/decodes whitelist local protocols and ordinary audio demuxers; disguised playlist inputs cannot fetch network resources or unrelated playlist members. Existing command callers retain their prior lifecycle behavior. The shared CLI dispatch was simplified to keep argument validation centralized as the new worker was added.

## Verification so far

The focused search suite passes 37 tests, covering parser/ranges, protected source admission and aliases, compact vectors, inventory/transactions, bounded model transport, Unicode text extraction, image/audio preparation, image ordering, backend fallback and cancellation, relevance-order preservation and cancellation, plus rejection of a disguised audio playlist without opening its network URL. Eleven Python answer-contract/runtime-installation tests also pass. Core's process-group test verifies that timed-out helpers and helpers that exit ahead of descendants do not leave inherited pipes blocked. The prior configuration test verifies no default roots and preservation of unrelated settings.

A real private Weston/Niri acceptance run passed content filtering, recursive filename scope, closing/restoring the listing, empty-default behavior, folder enrollment, three persisted file records/vectors, and semantic ranking of a cat document above a bank statement. A second run additionally changed the cat document after indexing and verified that its stale semantic match was omitted. Both dark and light runs passed; evidence is in `/tmp/clip-search-acceptance-20261018d/report.json` and `/tmp/clip-search-acceptance-20261018e/report.json` and their screenshots. The latest dark run, `/tmp/clip-search-acceptance-20261018g/report.json`, also verifies closing search through the visible Close button after correcting its layout. Those tests use isolated config/data and synthetic files; they do not enroll user folders. Tesseract needed its already-installed English data selected through `TESSDATA_PREFIX`.

These checks do not establish full Phase 6 completion. The broader file-manager suite passed 571 tests, with one intentionally ignored test, before the progressive-navigation increment below. The full workspace and final UI/performance gates remain outstanding. Initial real model smoke checks independently cover text/image/audio compatibility, finite normalized 768-dimensional output and oversized-query rejection on CPU; they are not retrieval-quality benchmarks.

Architecture comparisons found new large worker functions during integration; source admission, preparation, index publication, candidate ranking and popup controls were separated by responsibility. The old worker CLI's complexity fell from 17 to 6. The remaining new clone warning is a six-line standard PCM WAV header sequence also present in an audio test fixture; the other new clone group is repeated storage/configuration test setup. Existing gallery/image-strip clone warnings reflect adding the creation-time field to their pre-existing test entries. No newly excessive-complexity functions remain in the latest comparison. Overall duplication remains below the starting baseline. The native shim retains its third-party source/license provenance.

## Image-order checkpoint (2026-10-18)

Image dHash and selected-reference Hamming-distance sorting are implemented through a lightweight shared thumbnail/hash path, without semantic enrollment or full-file SHA-256 work. The session displays a sortable dHash/Distance column, retains the captured reference as selection changes, places pending/failed values last in either direction, prioritizes visible images, and bounds analysis to two reader jobs. Reference verification also runs periodically while active. Hash readers suspend/cancel for studio entry and resume when returning to the browser.

The focused search suite includes hash field parsing, unsigned ordering, distance ordering, natural-name ties, unavailable values, reference replacement, and obsolete cached-input rejection. The corrected full private Weston/Niri acceptance run passed: `/tmp/clip-image-order-acceptance-20261018c/report.json`. It checks distance display, header reversal, retained reference after selection changes, reference invalidation, raw-hash order and restoration, without creating an embedding database. Exact all-one hash bits are verified through the same reader protocol because OCR cannot reliably distinguish repeated hexadecimal `f` characters. The first rerun lacked Tesseract's language-data path; the passing run explicitly sets the installed `TESSDATA_PREFIX`. Full Phase 6 acceptance remains outstanding.


## Progressive local navigation checkpoint (2026-10-18)

Ordinary typing now ranks exact, prefix and substring matches before relaxed extension matching (`foo.md` → `foo_2.md`), ordered name fragments (`a_ foo` → `a_foo_b.md`), then conservative typo matches. Typo correction permits one insertion/deletion/substitution/adjacent transposition, requires at least four query characters, and compares at most 128 Unicode scalar values. Fragment scoring uses the same ordered-character approach as the existing PDF contents finder, with compact matches preferred. Equal scores retain display order. The transient hint names the fallback stage; typing still selects/reveals without filtering or opening files. Explicit command filename queries remain literal, and regex/filter semantics are unchanged. Nine targeted navigation tests pass, including real egui event handling, focus isolation, timeout/editing, priority and no implicit index/model creation. Automatic recursive escalation and configurable always-search locations remain outstanding; this checkpoint does not claim that work is complete.

The strengthened light Q/A UI run passed after the Enter/background-click correction: `/tmp/clip-answer-columns-debug-20261018e/report.json`. The latest stricter 21-case CUDA answer comparison passes 20/21 for Qwen and 18/21 for NuExtract3. A literal-absence question remains a quality failure: document text claiming that its source changed can lead the model to abstain despite an authoritative complete extraction flag. The embedding→answer→embedding UI run has not yet passed that case. Earlier 20-case smoke results above remain historical evidence, not a claim that current generic answering is fully validated.

Architecture comparison after local navigation introduces no additional flagged excessive-complexity functions or clone groups relative to the preceding checkpoint. The overall Phase 6 comparison still reports the previously documented clone warnings and lower overall duplication than baseline.

Progressive-navigation regression verification: `cargo test -p clip-workbench --lib file_manager:: -- --test-threads=1` passed 573 tests with one intentionally ignored test (`/tmp/clip-file-manager-tests-20261018-progressive.log`). Formatting and `git diff --check` pass.


## Automatic recursive navigation (2026-10-18)

After all local progressive name stages fail, ordinary typing waits 350 ms and hands the literal query to an editable search field. The UI explicitly labels automatic scope and searches the current subtree plus optional `file_manager.search.always_search` locations. That configuration is empty by default and independent of semantic enrollment. Edits cancel and invalidate the previous request immediately, then debounce a new submission by 250 ms. Returning to a locally matching name switches back to current-folder results. Automatic results select the highest-ranked entry for ordinary activation; Escape restores the prior listing/selection. Explicit command scopes do not gain this widening, progressive matching or extra roots. Automatic text is passed as structured literal query data, so colons/semicolons cannot become commands accidentally.

The traversal reuses guarded disposable readers, never enrolls a root or generates embeddings, rejects protected paths and symlink/nested-mount traversal, deduplicates overlapping paths, and bounds work to 100,000 entries, 10,000 directories and 64 directory levels. Automatic scans also stop admitting directories after 30 seconds; an individual in-flight bounded reader can extend that elapsed time. Skipped roots/reads and depth/budget limits remain visible as incomplete/partial coverage. All accepted results are ranked before returning, including budget-limited results. One unavailable configured location does not erase matches from other locations.

The first private integration run caught focus moving back to the list on the first live-edit character. Live debounced submissions now retain editor focus; explicit Search/Enter still returns it to the list. The regression scenario checks the complete edited query, not only the resulting filename. Background traversal and content matching now share a dedicated `search/walk.rs` owner, keeping model/index lifecycle separate. Architecture comparison reports no newly excessive-complexity functions after that split; the pre-existing checkpoint's clone warnings remain, with overall duplication below baseline. Full Phase 6 remains incomplete.

Private dark UI acceptance passed for automatic recursive navigation, live edits, overlapping/configured roots, protected/unavailable roots, symlink loops, restoration and explicit-scope isolation: `/tmp/clip-navigation-acceptance-20261018b/report.json`. The configured-location screenshot discloses two incomplete roots (missing and protected); no embedding database was created.

Final recursive-navigation regression check passed 576 file-manager tests with one intentionally ignored test (`/tmp/clip-recursive-file-manager-tests-final.log`). The debug file-manager build, Rust formatting check, Python acceptance-script compilation and `git diff --check` pass.


## Canonical embedding reuse and root availability (2026-10-18)

Prepared text/image/audio inputs now reuse existing vectors only when modality, exact SHA-256 canonical input and model pipeline match. A metadata change or rename still goes through bounded preparation and final source verification, but identical prepared content avoids inference. Copies can reuse vectors across object identities. This does not prove whole-file equality or replace the planned small-source full-digest optimization.

Schema version 2 records each segment's original pipeline separately from the mutable file row and adds an indexed reuse lookup. The transactional v1 migration preserves vectors, backfills only ready rows whose pipeline is known, and leaves pending rows' original pipeline unknown instead of guessing from overwritten file metadata. This prevents a pending reindex under a new pipeline from relabeling old vectors as reusable new-model output. Pipeline/backend distinctions remain as described above.

Reconciliation defers deleting orphan file/vector rows until its admitted work batch drains. This allows a move's source directory to finish before its destination is observed without losing expensive vectors. Orphan paths are removed promptly; unreferenced file/vector rows are removed at batch completion. Explicit root removal still prunes immediately. This adds no historical vector cache or unbounded duplicate copies. The Index status popup shows generated/reused inference counts for the running process.

Enrollment no longer overwrites a previously enrolled directory's device/inode identity at the same path. A replaced/remounted source is marked offline and excluded from current root coverage, while retained inventory/vectors remain available for recovery; choosing a replacement requires removing the prior enrollment. Startup publishes configured existing roots before probing them, so a failed probe does not hide their removal/recovery controls. Empty defaults still create no database or source scan. Device/inode checks do not yet establish the full mount-session lifetime contract; that remains required along with event-driven and periodic reconciliation.

The private CUDA acceptance run `/tmp/clip-reuse-acceptance-20261018c/report.json` passed cross-directory move reuse, copy reuse, new inference after changed text, orphan cleanup after deletion, and rejection/offline marking of a replacement root. It used isolated synthetic data/config; the authorized real corpus has not yet been used for the final coverage/performance gate. The first run stopped because full-frame OCR omitted the visible Rebuild label's coordinates; the runner now clicks its verified position on the private fixed output. Store tests additionally cover migration provenance, modality/hash/pipeline separation and source-before-destination retirement. Architecture comparison adds no excessive-complexity functions or clone groups beyond the earlier checkpoint; overall duplication is 5.84%, below the 6.04% baseline.


## Live incremental index monitoring (2026-10-18)

A bounded inotify instance now monitors admitted ordinary directories. Watch installation runs in the existing disposable guarded reader, against a retained directory descriptor and expected root identity; the coordinator inherits its notification handle through core's owned command mechanism. It does not open source payloads or perform filesystem lookups on the UI thread. Existing guarded content/output command arguments share the same descriptor-ownership implementation; seven core process tests pass.

Ordinary events invalidate the affected parent membership observations immediately when drained, debounce for one second, and queue a nonrecursive parent reconciliation. Previously known child directories retain their inventory; newly discovered directories are crawled. Full root scans and delta jobs never overlap for the same root. Both parents of a file move are processed in one admitted batch, retaining canonical-vector reuse. Queues are bounded; 8,192 watch registrations are admitted. Lost/self-moved watches, unmounts, queue overflow, malformed notification packets and read/transport failures invalidate uncertain coverage and request full reconciliation. If registration acknowledgement is lost, retiring the entire instance releases unknown registrations before rebuilding coverage. Removing an enrolled root retires its watch ownership without removing other roots' shared registrations.

Periodic full reconciliation currently runs every five minutes for all roots, including local ones. This is a conservative departure from the plan's 30-minute local / five-minute remote split while filesystem/session classification is unfinished; it avoids guessing that a remote/unknown filesystem has reliable client notifications. These are scheduling targets between bounded coordinator jobs, not guaranteed end-to-end freshness deadlines. Periodic checks cannot be postponed indefinitely by continuous ordinary events. Failed roots retry after 30, 60, 120 and then at most 300 seconds. Pausing prevents new background work while notification invalidation still marks coverage stale. Startup restores configured expected root identities even when a root is offline, allowing later bounded retries. Unverified memberships keep a root partial rather than falsely ready.

The private CUDA run `/tmp/clip-monitor-acceptance-20261018a/report.json` passed automatic edits, a cross-directory move with vector reuse, indexing a newly created subdirectory, deletion cleanup and automatic offline marking of a replacement root, with no manual rebuilds. Its database evidence (`monitor-results.json`) proves the first edit advanced only Alpha's directory generation from 1 to 2; the root, Quiet and Zulu remained at generation 1. Quiet remained unchanged through subsequent operations. This uses isolated synthetic fixtures, not user-root enrollment.

The broader file-manager suite passed 583 tests with one intentionally ignored test (`/tmp/clip-monitor-file-manager-tests.log`). After the final lost-registration guard, all 46 focused search tests pass (`/tmp/clip-monitor-search-tests-final.log`). They cover debounce, no overlapping root work, overflow/malformed packet recovery, periodic scheduling despite repeated events, retry timing and delta invalidation retaining unrelated directories. Architecture comparison reports no new excessive-complexity functions or additional clone groups relative to the preceding checkpoint; overall duplication is 5.83% against 6.04% initially. Full mount-session/source-lifetime validation, remote disconnect/reconnect acceptance, Show offline results and broader Phase 6 coverage/performance gates remain unfinished.


## Bounded document extraction (2026-10-18)

Search now extracts DOCX body/header/footer/note/comment text, ODT paragraphs with explicit whitespace, PPTX text in presentation order plus notes, cached XLSX values with sheet/cell locations, and HTML text without executing scripts or loading links. Plain text additionally supports BOM-marked UTF-16/UTF-32; ambiguous legacy encodings are still rejected. Code/archive/3D/MIDI formats remain name-only. ZIP entries are read in memory, never unpacked to paths; external relationships and XML entity declarations are not followed. Macro and formula execution are absent.

The extraction helper is standard-library Python and uses the existing pinned-descriptor reader/command lifecycle. It has a 768 MiB address-space limit, 20/25-second CPU limits and a 30-second outer command timeout; sources are limited to 64 MiB, extracted text to 8 MiB, XML parts to 8 MiB/32 MiB total, 4,096 ZIP members, 512 output parts, XML depth 128 and 250,000 elements per part. These are conservative initial limits. Embedded visual content or unsupported representation details produce explicit partial coverage. XLSX always remains partial until styles/Excel serial dates and related fidelity are handled; cached values are useful for literal/semantic search but are not claimed as complete Q/A evidence. RTF, legacy Office formats, richer encodings and remaining media/document coverage are unfinished.

The existing shared spreadsheet loader was reviewed, but it materializes whole worksheet ranges before applying a search budget. The search helper therefore extracts stored XML cell values directly, with explicit coverage limits. This is a deliberate bounded extraction mechanism; it does not replace the shared spreadsheet viewer/editor.

Schema version 3 adds a separate extraction-policy revision to file observations. Older ready/name-only files are prepared again when extractors change, while their exact canonical input/model-pipeline vectors remain reusable. This avoids both permanently skipping formerly unsupported formats and invalidating expensive identical vectors solely because extraction capabilities improved. Migrations preserve existing records. Model helper resolution is shared with document extraction and remains independent of the launch directory; provisioning retains the document helper with runtime support scripts.

Private real-CUDA acceptance passed all seven new format/encoding fixtures through literal search and embedding indexing/retrieval: `/tmp/clip-document-acceptance-20261018a/report.json`. It verifies literal search creates no database, external entity text is not imported, script code is absent from extracted text, code stays name-only, and slide/sheet locations reach stored segments. Nineteen Python contracts pass, as do 584 file-manager tests with one intentionally ignored test (`/tmp/clip-document-file-manager-tests.log`). Architecture comparison reports no new excessive-complexity functions; duplication remains below baseline. Full Phase 6 and its quality/performance gates remain unfinished.


## Hybrid retrieval and bounded presentation (2026-10-18)

Semantic commands now combine filename matches, extracted-text FTS5 BM25, and separate text/image/audio best-segment lists. Exact full basenames remain first (case-sensitive before case-folded). Literal Unicode lexical terms use AND with a final-token prefix, falling back to OR only when there are no lexical matches within the same hard filters. User input cannot introduce raw FTS operators. Each component keeps at most 500 eligible files; scope, hidden-file visibility and metadata predicates run before these limits. Per-path hidden status comes from the path rather than shared hard-link object metadata.

Fusion uses the planned one-based reciprocal ranks: lexical weight 2, total semantic weight 1 divided across searched modalities, offset 60. Segment counts do not accumulate relevance. Missing modality floors default to cosine 0.25; finite per-modality overrides live in configuration separately from embedding provenance. Equal ranks use natural filenames and lossless paths. Source verification still omits changed/unavailable candidates before publication. This replaces the historical cosine-only/1,000-candidate checkpoint above. Calibration, matching snippets/locations and lexical-first results while a model loads remain unfinished.

Schema version 4 rebuilds only the FTS index to include Unicode combining-mark categories; it preserves vectors and requires no embedding regeneration. The transactional legacy migration is tested with retained text and original vector provenance. The [SQLite FTS5 tokenizer documentation](https://www.sqlite.org/fts5.html#unicode61_tokenizer) defines the category configuration. Candidate sets are bounded with a keyed ordered set, including replacement of a file's previous best segment, rather than an unbounded map or stale heap entries.

Private CUDA UI acceptance passed exact filename priority for a name-only source file, a rare technical identifier found through FTS, and strict extension filtering: `/tmp/clip-hybrid-acceptance-20261018a/report.json`. The subsequent complete file-manager suite passed 592 tests with one intentionally ignored test (`/tmp/clip-hybrid-file-manager-tests-final.log`), including migration retention and hard-link visibility. These are compatibility/correctness checks, not completion of retrieval-quality or performance gates.

Semantic presentation now starts with 100 results and provides explicit Load more in batches of 100 within the retrieved window. Explicit sorts and answer-column sorts run over the entire available candidate set before pagination; ordinary filename/metadata/content lists remain unpaged. The status distinguishes displayed count from available matching candidates and retains the retrieval-cap disclosure. Closing or submitting a new search resets pagination. The pagination regression test verifies 100/200/all pages, whole-window size sorting and unpaged ordinary searches. The focused search suite passes 56 tests; the full file-manager suite passes 593 with one intentionally ignored test (`/tmp/clip-pagination-file-manager-tests.log`). Nineteen Python helper contracts and the empty-default configuration test pass. Formatting, the debug build and diff whitespace checks pass.

Private CUDA UI acceptance also passes the rendered Load more action (100 of 102 → 102 of 102), reset on the next submission, descending natural-name sort before pagination, exact filename priority and hard filters: `/tmp/clip-hybrid-pagination-acceptance-20261018b/report.json`. The first fixture run failed because adding 102 names pushed its old initial-screen expectations below the visible area; the corrected runner checks a visible fixture without scrolling the user's compositor. The latest architecture comparison flags no new excessive-complexity functions or pagination clone groups; its overall regression classification continues to include the previously documented fixture/image-strip clone warnings, while overall duplication remains below baseline. Full Phase 6 remains incomplete.


## Tokenizer-aware passages (2026-10-18)

The interim 1,536-byte nonoverlapping chunks are replaced by passages bounded by the actual loaded EmbeddingGemma tokenizer: at most 768 tokens including `title: none | text:` framing and special tokens, and 8 KiB of source UTF-8. Paragraph/sentence boundaries in the latter half of a candidate passage are preferred; long paragraphs hard-split. Subsequent passages retain up to 128 tokenizer tokens and at most half the prior passage as overlap, guaranteeing forward progress. Binary boundary search is conservative because BPE counts need not be monotone; every selected boundary is counted directly. The original extracted text is never decoded back from tokens or normalized to form a passage.

The existing owned model helper handles a bounded tokenization-only request, with its normal cancellation/deadline mechanism. The coordinator retains one prepared text part and processes one passage per work turn instead of allocating every chunk in advance. Persisted segment locations include the exact UTF-8 byte range within their extracted part/page. These are extracted-text offsets, not PDF/archive-container file offsets. Content-derived title extraction remains unavailable; the existing neutral title avoids filename-dependent vectors.

Extraction revision 2 causes unchanged previously prepared files to be segmented again at reconciliation. The canonical model-input pipeline stays unchanged: exact old input hashes can still reuse valid vectors, while newly bounded/overlapping passages require inference. Segmentation is extraction provenance rather than a reason to invalidate compatible image/audio vectors or identical text inputs. The database schema remains version 4.

Twenty-three Python contracts pass, including multilingual exact byte coverage, bounded overlap, sentence boundaries, hard splits and explicit invalid-input rejection. The real CUDA smoke run (`/tmp/clip-passages-cuda-smoke.json`) passes text/image/audio normalized vectors and tokenizer budgeting: its multilingual passage has 767 tokens and 128 overlap tokens. An initial smoke fixture exceeded the new 8-KiB request limit and was explicitly rejected; the corrected fixture fits the documented protocol. Private CUDA UI acceptance passes with English and multilingual documents larger than an 8-KiB request window: `/tmp/clip-passages-acceptance-20261018b/report.json`. Its `passages.json` and database assertions verify exact persisted source slices, complete byte coverage, advancing overlap, extraction revision 2 and subsequent semantic retrieval. The first fixture run expected more than four vectors from two documents that correctly produced two passages each; the corrected fixtures are longer and cross the request-window boundary. The focused Rust search suite passes 56 tests and the broader file-manager suite passes 593 with one intentionally ignored test (`/tmp/clip-passages-file-manager-tests.log`). Debug build, formatting, Python compilation and whitespace checks pass. Architecture comparison adds no flagged complexity/clone issues for this increment; the earlier fixture/image-strip warnings remain. Generic Q/A aggregation, retrieval-quality calibration and full Phase 6 acceptance remain outstanding.


## Search preferences and configuration editing (2026-10-18)

Edit → Search settings now exposes persistent background pause, eligible hidden-file indexing, last-used model residency, automatic filename-search locations and per-modality semantic thresholds. It also lists enrolled roots with refresh/removal controls; enrollment remains the explicit folder Index action. Saving preferences with empty enrollment does not create a database, scan directories or load a model. Preferences are validated and written by the coordinator before publication to the UI; commands preserve the coordinator's authoritative enrolled roots rather than accepting a stale editor copy. A hidden-indexing change queues full guarded reconciliation of enrolled roots; pause stops background jobs while foreground search remains available. Paused queued work no longer prevents the normal five-minute idle model release.

Automatic locations are limited to 32 absolute reversible browser addresses, with protected paths rejected and runtime guarded admission retained. Missing ordinary locations can be saved and later report incomplete search coverage. Threshold changes do not invalidate vectors. Preferences use explicit Save, allowing edits/cancellation without immediate side effects. Root exclusions, indexing quotas, backup/restore and global Clear index are still outstanding settings/maintenance work.

The shared configuration writer now uses the already resolved `toml_edit` 0.22 parser/editor instead of line replacement. This handles multiline arrays and inline nested tables while preserving unrelated comments/formatting and enrollment. Multiple preference edits are prepared and validated together before writing; malformed parent values fail instead of overwriting unrelated data. This is not yet a new cross-process configuration transaction/locking protocol. Eight configuration tests pass, including existing appearance/audio persistence and the new multiline/inline-table preferences case. The focused search suite passes 57 tests, including protected-location and threshold validation. The broader file-manager suite passes 594 tests with one intentionally ignored test (`/tmp/clip-search-settings-file-manager-tests.log`); the 23 Python model/extraction contracts, formatting, build and whitespace checks pass. Shared browser modal routing now covers settings, naming and transfer windows consistently, including shortcut forwarding and focus restoration. Architecture comparison introduces no newly excessive-complexity functions for this increment; earlier fixture/image-strip clone warnings remain and overall duplication is below baseline.

Private dark UI acceptance passes `/proc` rejection, preference save/reopen/resume, authoritative config checks and automatic filename fallback into a newly configured ordinary location, all without creating an embedding database: `/tmp/clip-search-settings-acceptance-20261018e/report.json`. Earlier runs exposed and corrected the private runner's pointer parking on the File menu, a clipped initial root summary and OCR matching dimmed background labels instead of foreground controls. The helper now parks on the actual private title bar; the window has an appropriate initial height, and this fixed-output acceptance uses verified button coordinates where borders defeat OCR. The equivalent light-theme run also passes: `/tmp/clip-search-settings-light-20261018a/report.json`. This does not claim root-exclusion, quota or backup/restore controls are complete.


## Index capacity admission (2026-10-18–19)

Persistent `[file_manager.search.limits]` settings default to 10,240 MiB and 1,000,000 vectors. Search settings exposes both as advanced storage controls; UI/config validation admits 16..1,048,576 MiB and 1..100,000,000 vectors. These limits apply to generated search data, independently of model weights. Existing configured defaults remain opt-in and empty.

The single index owner checks capacity before background jobs, before new text/media inference and before final publication. Projected vector counts credit the previous vectors of the file being replaced. The cached committed-vector count is initialized from SQLite, updated only after successful publication, and refreshed after orphan/root cleanup; it is not a COUNT over the entire vector table on every passage. Byte accounting reads the private pinned directory's database/WAL/SHM sizes and reserves space for pending segments, prepared media and FTS/WAL write amplification. The reserve is a conservative soft-admission estimate (eightfold pending payload plus 1 MiB), not a hard guarantee of SQLite's final allocated size. Allocation reuse/free pages are not reported as reclaimed filesystem capacity.

A rejected admission retains the current work item and its pending segments, pauses further background scanning/inference, and publishes a separate capacity reason that foreground searches cannot clear. Existing compatible results remain searchable; changed/unverified results keep their usual freshness exclusions. Saving preferences or explicitly refreshing/removing an indexed root retries admission. Increasing a limit can continue queued work without beginning a fresh crawl. Background SQLite FULL/IOERR/READONLY failures also pause further admission with a disk-space/retry explanation; the failed root retains error/retry reconciliation rather than claiming the interrupted item completed. Notifications can still invalidate stale membership while indexing is paused. The ordinary five-minute idle model release applies during a capacity pause.

The focused search suite passes 59 tests, including vector replacement/removal/reopen accounting, WAL bytes, pending-text write reserves and injected SQLite FULL detection. Configuration tests cover the new default limits. The architecture check initially flagged coordinator complexity; single-passage construction now has its own focused owner, reusing existing tokenizer, input hash and vector cache mechanisms. The refined comparison has no newly excessive-complexity function for this increment; prior fixture/image-strip clone warnings remain. The private real-CUDA run `/tmp/clip-capacity-acceptance-20261018b/report.json` passes pause at one vector, searching the completed file while paused, changing the persisted vector limit through the settings control, and finishing both files after raising it to two. The root stays at generation 1, proving queued continuation rather than a new full crawl. The first run revealed a stale throttled status count when capacity stopped work; pausing now forces a fresh root snapshot. The runner was also corrected to identify the actual vector control rather than a root's vector-count label and to keep scroll injection within its documented bounds. Eight core configuration tests and 23 Python contracts pass; the broader file-manager suite passes 596 tests with one intentionally ignored test. The final repeat after the snapshot correction passed 596 tests. Formatting/build/whitespace checks pass. Backup/restore, Clear index, editable root exclusions and the full performance/quality gates remain outstanding.


Disk-full follow-up (2026-10-19): the stronger replacement test proved that SQLite transaction cleanup can overwrite the connection's last error code. The store now records typed FULL/IOERR/READONLY failures before cleanup across its queries and mutations, including pruning helpers. A failed replacement preserves the old text/vector and committed-vector counter; its storage-failure indication survives rollback and later status reads. Coordinator checks cover command/monitor failures as well as indexing jobs. Explicit retry/admission starts a fresh storage attempt instead of carrying a handled failure indefinitely. The focused 59-test search suite passed after error recording; the broader suite after coordinator integration passed 596 tests with one intentionally ignored test (`/tmp/clip-capacity-storage-file-manager-tests.log`).


Directory-inventory admission follow-up (2026-10-19): a directory's bounded reader response is now reserved as a whole before any file/path/membership rows are written and before the model loads. Its payload estimate includes serialized entry metadata, path/index overhead and the same conservative write-amplification reserve. Denied inventory retains its directory job and generation; it never marks that directory complete or publishes a partial inventory as complete. Bounded watch/metadata reading remains independent of content admission. The read/validation/admission stage is shared in the coordinator's `read_inventory` owner, keeping scan iteration and job scheduling separate.

The private 5,000-file/no-model test passes: `/tmp/clip-capacity-inventory-acceptance-20261019a/report.json`. Under a 16-MiB budget it stops with a storage-limit explanation, zero file rows/vectors and no runtime/model directory, with actual DB/WAL/SHM below the budget. The final CUDA vector-limit run also passes after storage-error recording and inventory admission: `/tmp/clip-capacity-final-acceptance-20261019a/report.json`. The focused 59-test search suite passes after separating admitted inventory reads. Architecture comparison reports no new excessive-complexity function after that split; existing fixture/image-strip clone warnings remain. Final debug build and the full file-manager regression run pass: 596 tests with one intentionally ignored test (`/tmp/clip-capacity-admission-file-manager-tests.log`). The subsequent storage-failure retry test also passes, including preservation across status reads and explicit reset for a new attempt. Formatting, 23 Python helper contracts, script compilation and whitespace checks pass. Full Phase 6 acceptance remains outstanding.


## Explicit backup and clear maintenance (2026-10-19)

Search settings now offers an explicit unused backup destination and a confirmed Clear generated index action. Backup uses the [SQLite online backup API](https://www.sqlite.org/backup.html), including source pages still in WAL, and produces a self-contained schema-v4 SQLite file with no model weights. Preparation copies 128 pages per step with cancellation/deadline checks between steps. Output stages beside the destination through a retained ordinary-directory handle, syncs before installation and installs with no overwrite; failed/cancelled preparation removes its temporary file/sidecars. It reports success once installation commits rather than masking an already-created backup with a later cleanup error. Backups are explicit user files, not an accumulating automatic history.

Clear removes generated path/membership/directory/file/segment/FTS records transactionally while retaining root enrollment identities and model files. It then attempts WAL checkpointing and compaction; a reader delaying reclamation or post-commit compaction failure is a warning on a successful clear. The vector-count cache changes only after the deletion commits. Source files and user backups are retained. Unlike an immediate automatic rebuild, Clear also persists Pause indexing, so clearing does not immediately spend time regenerating all vectors. Expected roots are guarded/requeued while paused; explicit Resume can start that queued rebuild. This is a deliberate UX choice. Successful clear retires old semantic-result lists and restores their browser state without discarding an open settings editor; unrelated ordinary filename/content searches remain independent of the embedding index.

Maintenance admission marks the operation busy before the worker can dispatch it, defers application close through the existing file-operation guard, rejects duplicate maintenance and releases the busy flag on failed queue admission. Accepted ordinary browser operations continue ticking. The single coordinator serializes backup/clear with its index operations; this does not add a second resident model or SQLite writer.

The focused suite passes 64 tests and the broader file-manager suite passes 601 with one intentionally ignored test (`/tmp/clip-maintenance-file-manager-tests.log`). New tests cover WAL snapshot content/integrity, private backup permissions, no overwrite, cancelled staging cleanup, clear preserving identities/models/backups, delayed reclamation under a reader, and accepted/rejected maintenance lifecycle. The architecture check initially flagged the enlarged settings method; maintenance controls now have their own state owner and no newly excessive-complexity function remains. Existing fixture/image-strip clone warnings persist, with total duplication below baseline. The debug build, 23 Python contracts and whitespace checks pass. The private dark UI run passes: `/tmp/clip-maintenance-acceptance-20261019a/report.json`. It checks a readable backup, source/backup/model/root preservation through a confirmed clear, persistent pause, retirement of semantic search and reindexing only after explicit Resume. A separate last-maintenance outcome now survives background progress and other search commands, and rejected queue admission preserves the previous outcome. The light-theme run also passes: `/tmp/clip-maintenance-light-20261019b/report.json`; its first run clicked a history label instead of the backup field, and the corrected runner uses the unique destination label. The final regression repeat after the persistent-outcome change passes 601 tests. Restore/import validation is still outstanding; no restore control is exposed yet. Full Phase 6 remains incomplete.


## Configured enrollment remains authoritative (2026-10-19)

Maintenance review found that retained SQLite root rows could otherwise remain eligible after their configuration entries were removed. On opening the cache, the coordinator now disables unconfigured roots and invalidates their membership observations without deleting their vectors. Disabled cached rows supply neither semantic candidates, active source roots nor indexed traversal scopes. Clear preserves that disabled state. A fresh explicit Index action can reuse a matching cached lifetime, or replace a disabled mismatched lifetime after guarded admission; automatic reconciliation still rejects replacements of active/offline enrolled roots. Inactive generated work is not silently promoted back into enrollment by persisting another root.

Indexed on-demand searches now pass the enrolled `SourceRoot` identity to the existing bounded traversal reader rather than probing a pathname into a fresh root. Unavailable roots are omitted. The private paused-reconciliation replacement test passes: `/tmp/clip-indexed-root-identity-20261019a/report.json`. The original root remains available in the database with its old inode/generation, yet indexed content search returns zero matches and one incomplete read for its replacement, proving the expected identity was retained rather than relying on an already-offline flag.

A new regression test reopens a populated cache, removes configured eligibility, confirms there are no matches/active scopes while vectors remain, and verifies Clear cannot reactivate those roots. Enrollment tests also distinguish a fresh explicit enrollment from automatic replacement. The focused search suite passes 65 tests. Empty semantic scope now returns an enrollment explanation before loading a model or opening/creating an index. With empty enrollment, Search settings checks only cache-file metadata on its worker to offer backup/clear for inactive cached work, so those operations remain accessible even without currently enrolled folders; empty defaults still create no index or source scan. The settings renderer uses one owned view snapshot, avoiding a shared lock during UI interaction.

The final debug build and 65 focused search tests pass. The default-empty-scope UI test and broader regression repeat after enrollment-state integration are recorded below. Reconfiguring a disabled cached root exposes only checking intent until fresh admission; failed probes cannot promote an unconfigured cached root out of disabled state. Restore validation and import remain outstanding along with the other previously documented quality/performance and remaining Phase 6 work.


The default-empty-scope acceptance exposed an existing Ctrl+F transition issue: when focus remained in automatic search, Ctrl+F did not reset it to explicit command mode, so a pasted colon command stayed literal. Ctrl+F now targets the list or its own search field, and still avoids unrelated editors/popups. A real egui-key-event regression verifies the mode transition, preserved draft, editor isolation and no service execution merely from focusing search. Automatic typing still treats colons as data unless the user explicitly enters command mode. The focused suite now passes 66 tests; the final default-scope UI and broader regression checks pass.


Final verification for this increment: 603 file-manager tests pass with one intentionally ignored test (`/tmp/clip-maintenance-enrollment-file-manager-tests.log`), along with 66 focused search tests, 23 Python helper contracts, Rust formatting and diff whitespace checks. The corrected private default-scope run passes `/tmp/clip-empty-enrollment-settings-20261019b/report.json`, including automatic extra-location search followed by an explicit semantic command with no enrollment; no index file is created and no model is installed/loaded. The debug file-manager build passes. This checkpoint covers backup/clear, lifecycle, configured-root scoping and shortcut transitions; it does not establish restore/import, full scope-policy retirement, generic Q/A quality, remaining format/video coverage or the final performance/real-corpus gates.


## Restore/import checkpoint — implementation under verification

Search settings now exposes a confirmed Restore action, available even with no enrolled folders. Preparation runs in the existing disposable reader with owned input/output descriptors, a five-minute wall deadline, CPU/address-space limits, SQLite parser/value limits, row/vector caps and the configured storage budget. The input must be a completed native schema-4 snapshot, not a live WAL main file. The guarded input is copied through its held descriptor to private staging before SQLite opens an immutable/read-only snapshot; SQLite resolves descriptor symlinks itself, so opening the original descriptor label directly would not preserve the intended path guarantee. The original identity is checked again after preparation. Restore constructs our schema in a private temporary file, streams only the known ordinary-table columns, checks file metadata/identities, paths, foreign keys and finite nonzero f16 vectors, and rebuilds FTS from segment text. Supplied triggers, views, indexes and FTS records are not copied. Generated columns are rejected. Source files and model weights are untouched.

Deviation from the closed-file-swap description: after canonical preparation, a valid active cache is replaced through SQLite's backup transaction on the existing owned connection, retaining the directory/ownership lock and SQLite's rollback/commit semantics. Preparation failures do not pause or modify the active cache; immediately before application, Pause is persisted. A failed application remains paused with the prior index retained. All restored memberships/directories become unverified and roots unavailable; only the current configured roots are queued for guarded reconciliation. Current expected root identities take precedence over historical backup bindings. Backup roots do not become enrollment merely by being imported. Free-space reserve checks cover the raw snapshot, canonical staging and application. Parent-owned temporary files clean up on helper failure/cancellation; reopening under the sole-writer lock also prunes abandoned regular, mode-0600, same-owner scratch files from the reserved restore namespace, without following symlinks.

Seven restore tests now cover roundtrip vector/FTS preservation with current root authority, empty-cache import without enrollment, invalid-vector/cancel retention, discarding supplied triggers/FTS with generated-column rejection, real SQLITE_FULL rollback with retained fault provenance, a replaced original pathname and abandoned scratch cleanup under ownership. The serial broader suite passes 611 tests with one intentionally ignored (`/tmp/clip-restore-file-manager-final-tests.log`); its first parallel run exposed an unrelated shared optional-helper budget race in the shutdown test, which passed alone and in the serial run. After the final integrity-check extraction, all 74 focused search tests pass (`/tmp/clip-restore-search-final-tests.log`), the debug build passes, and all 23 Python contracts pass. Native dark restore/resume passes `/tmp/clip-restore-acceptance-20261019d/report.json`; the strengthened light run also rejects a malformed backup without pausing or removing existing vectors before performing a successful restore and explicit resume (`/tmp/clip-restore-light-20261019b/report.json`). Backup/clear/resume regression passes `/tmp/clip-restore-maintenance-regression-20261019b/report.json`. Earlier UI attempts needed the installed local Tesseract language path, a bounded private-pointer scroll, unique confirmation anchors and a settings-region OCR fallback; these were runner failures, with no active-index corruption. Maintenance now synchronizes the editor’s Pause checkbox from the worker’s acknowledged state after restore/clear, including rejected preparations, rather than guessing a successful pause at click time. The architecture comparison has no newly excessive-complexity function; restore and clear share their post-commit retirement lifecycle and native harnesses share backup/runtime setup. Overall clone warnings remain, with duplication below baseline. Corrupt-active-cache recovery remains outstanding: the current restore path requires opening a valid active cache. This checkpoint does not establish complete recovery or completion of Phase 6.


## Hidden-scope policy checkpoint — verified

A full reconciliation now retires a root's generated memberships/directories for paths excluded by the current policy, even if the underlying files still exist. Ordinary removal continues to require a guarded absence observation. The retirement uses the existing root-scoped reference cleanup and deferred orphan pruning, so a separately enrolled overlapping root retains its own references/vectors. Old watch notifications and queued directory deltas are also checked before enumeration, preventing excluded hidden subtrees from being added back to directory inventory after a preference change. Source files are never deleted by these operations.

Semantic Here/Recursive visibility now checks every descendant path component relative to the current browsing folder; a hidden folder explicitly opened as the scope does not hide ordinary children. Indexed visibility requires an actual verified membership in an available enrolled root whose relative path has no hidden component. Merely enrolling a nested hidden folder without verifying that path does not expose the parent's hidden inventory. This runs inside the existing SQL membership predicate before lexical/vector candidate limits, using BLOB operations for non-UTF-8 paths, with no per-result database query. Displayed hidden status still describes the file's own basename.

The source reader and policy retirement share a pure path eligibility check, including mandatory protected/generated/secret exclusions and relative hidden visibility. New tests cover hidden ancestors, explicitly selected hidden scopes, invalid-UTF-8 path components, actual nested-root membership, preserving overlapping-root data when retiring the parent, and hiding a path again when its owning membership is unverified. Focused search tests pass 77 and the broader serial file-manager suite passes 614 with one intentionally ignored (`/tmp/clip-hidden-search-tests.log`, `/tmp/clip-hidden-file-manager-tests.log`). Architecture comparison adds no excessive-complexity function; existing clone warnings persist with total duplication below baseline. The private-compositor scenario passes `/tmp/clip-hidden-index-acceptance-20261019d/report.json`: changing hidden indexing on/off/on, independent browser hidden visibility, mandatory exclusions, old watch events, return to ready and preserved source bytes. The first native attempts stopped on OCR dropping a filename’s leading dot or ignoring bordered Hidden-toggle text; the corrected runner uses the actual filename substring and a verified text-only toggle crop. SQL checks assert the full retained paths/vector counts and absence of excluded directory rows, independently of OCR. User-defined exclusions and per-root overrides remain outstanding, as do the other previously recorded Phase 6 gaps.


## Generic Q/A model correctness — 9B default verified

A repeat of the original 21-case CUDA baseline still passes only 20/21 (`/tmp/clip-answer-baseline-20261019.json`). Its failure treats text saying a source changed since indexing as a reason not to answer a literal-content question. Expanding the cases exposed more general confusion between text occurrence, missing facts and negation. Moving application-owned coverage after source data clarified provenance, but prompt-only 4B experiments still failed several distinctions and one emphasis variant incorrectly converted missing permission into false. Those experiments are recorded in `/tmp/clip-answer-layout-targeted-20261019*.json`; they are not accepted quality fixes. NuExtract3 also passed only 4/8 in the targeted expanded check (`/tmp/clip-answer-nuextract-targeted-20261019.json`).

The replacement [Qwen3.5-9B Q8_0 artifact](https://huggingface.co/bartowski/Qwen_Qwen3.5-9B-GGUF/tree/182be2fd6c7bc44887d88a91cb03ff009cc9f549) passes all 27 expanded cases with the same final prompt (`/tmp/clip-answer-9b-full-20261019.json`), in 78.048 seconds on RTX 3090 CUDA. The cases retain arbitrary numeric/date/text/boolean questions, valid false/zero, non-applicability, missing/ambiguous facts, partial coverage and embedded instructions; added cases cover literal presence despite a stale-source claim, ordinary absence, draft claims, other phrase vocabulary, negated absence and partial absence. This is a small synthetic gate, not a broad accuracy or final-Q/A acceptance claim.

Decision: replace the provisional Qwen4B answer default with Qwen9B rather than add a third model or a document-specific workaround. It occupies the existing one-at-a-time answer role. The artifact is 9,804,541,984 bytes with pinned revision/checksum in `answer_models.json`; a read-only source config/license check records Qwen's reviewed source revision separately without claiming that it proves the converter's exact source commit. During a 20-second interval, the model process used 9,216 MiB of VRAM (`/tmp/clip-answer-9b-memory-20261019.json`); this is observed residency for that workload, not a universal peak. The larger model consumes substantially more memory and CPU fallback may be slow; no automatic download or background Q/A is introduced. Existing install controls, helper launch and readiness use the selected model. Numeric/date/evidence validation and typed unknown/non-applicable states remain application-owned. The updated runtime explicitly passes its selected provider and rejects startup metadata reporting an older provider; its fake-helper admission test passes. All 78 focused search tests, 615 serial file-manager tests (one intentionally ignored), 24 Python contracts, the debug build, formatting and whitespace checks pass (`/tmp/clip-answer-9b-search-tests.log`, `/tmp/clip-answer-9b-file-manager-final-tests.log`, `/tmp/clip-answer-9b-python-tests.log`). The private dark integration passes embedding → Q/A → embedding, including the original literal-absence case (`/tmp/clip-answer-9b-role-acceptance-20261019d/report.json`). The old stale-source assertion raced successful live reindexing; the fixture now pauses indexing, allows either event invalidation or verifier rejection to omit old content, refreshes browser metadata before asking questions, and explicitly resumes reconciliation afterward. A shared settings-word helper removes the test-import cycle. The private light column gate passes exact numeric/boolean values, header reversal, retained selection, closing search, returning from another folder, and no implicit semantic index (`/tmp/clip-answer-9b-columns-light-20261019a/report.json`). Architecture comparison has no newly excessive-complexity function or new dependency cycle; existing clone warnings persist with duplication below baseline. Vision, large-document coverage, deeper evaluation and the other recorded Phase 6 gaps remain outstanding.


## Search result actions and refresh — 2026-10-19

Remote filename/content and semantic results now carry the original observed source-root identities alongside their observed entries. Opening a result in a studio verifies that context in the disposable reader before loading and before accepting the loaded result; photo collection navigation retains each result's guard. Folder opens and the search-only **Reveal in folder** context action use the same context around the worker-side directory read, including same-folder reveals. Failed guarded navigation retains the search and cannot become an unguarded pathname retry. The bookmark menus are unchanged.

The source reader now also compares the actual pinned file's resolved pathname with the enumerated pathname before and after extraction/verification. Moving a parent directory outside the source and putting a symlink at its old location preserves a child's inode and timestamps, so the old metadata-only file check was insufficient. A regression explicitly establishes that the child's identity still matches while both Verify and Extract reject the alias. Directory verification intentionally checks object identity rather than every directory timestamp: ordinary child changes must not make opening the same folder fail.

Refresh after studio return now resubmits the accepted remote query instead of replacing results with an ordinary directory listing. It cancels superseded work and leaves unfinished/invalid draft text intact. A focused lifecycle test asserts this without adopting the draft or submitting a plain directory read. Existing local filtering continues to refresh the directory normally.

At this checkpoint 81 focused search tests and 618 serial file-manager tests pass, with one intentionally ignored (`/tmp/clip-search-actions-tests.log`, `/tmp/clip-search-actions-file-manager-tests.log`); the debug build, formatting and diff whitespace checks pass. Private dark UI acceptance passes `/tmp/clip-search-actions-20261019b/report.json`: valid text open, return preserving the remote search, changed-file open rejection, valid cross-folder and same-folder reveal, moved-parent alias rejection and no implicit index creation. The first UI attempt clicked the duplicated Back text in the top-right guide rather than the actual studio button; the runner now uses the verified button position. The strengthened light run passes `/tmp/clip-search-actions-20261019d/report.json`, additionally verifying stale Reveal rejection while retaining the search. That scenario exposed an existing status-panel layout issue: long errors were painted partly below the Shelf boundary. The panel now reserves a minimum error height, wraps the error independently of its icon and Retry button, and avoids allocating an empty progress row. The final dark run `/tmp/clip-search-actions-20261019e/report.json` passes all 17 observations, including the visible Retry control; visual inspection confirms that both wrapped error lines and the entire button fit above the Shelf. The final debug build and formatting/whitespace checks pass. The serial suite was repeated after the initial status fix and again passed 618 tests with one ignored (`/tmp/clip-search-actions-final-tests.log`); the final empty-row layout correction is covered by the build and native run.

Architecture comparison finds no newly excessive-complexity function or increased cycle count; clone-group warnings remain (+19 versus the original baseline, total duplication 5.75% versus 6.04%). New small overlaps are fixture construction/assertions shared structurally with other tests, not a new production mechanism to consolidate. This checkpoint does **not** establish complete action coverage: external application launches and preview playback need further audit, and all previously recorded recovery/format/quality/performance gaps remain open.


## External launch admission — 2026-10-19

File-manager context-menu launches, studio header launches and unsupported-format fallback now share one external-launch owner. Search results retain their original source guard until worker-side admission; validation errors, missing launchers and nonzero launcher exit statuses are returned to the browser/studio instead of disappearing in a detached thread. Explicit launches use at most eight independent threads because `xdg-open` can wait for a GUI application: long-lived external applications must not occupy the bounded media executor. Closing Files cancels validation that has not yet launched; already launched external applications keep their independent lifetime and are reaped by their launching thread. No timeout after spawn masks an accepted launch.

Text and image studio handoffs use their observed `FileSource`, updated only by a successful owned save. This allows atomic in-app saves and Save As outputs to open externally, while rejecting a subsequent outside replacement. It does not freshly capture an arbitrary changed pathname to make a rejected search result pass. Refreshing the accepted remote search also clears old browser-operation errors without discarding an invalid search draft.

Three focused launcher tests cover owned-save admission versus outside replacement, missing program/nonzero status/cancellation, and the worker cap plus cancellation on owner retirement. The broader serial suite passed 620 tests with one ignored before the additional cap test; all 81 focused search tests pass after refresh error clearing. The strengthened light private run passes `/tmp/clip-external-20261019d/report.json`: exact mock-opener arguments, valid context launch, no opener invocation for a stale result, saving and then opening that new revision, no invocation after a later outside edit, visible nonzero-exit error, unknown-binary fallback returning to the browser, and no implicit index creation. The earlier dark run `/tmp/clip-external-20261019b/report.json` passed the core context/studio/save/error cases. The first dark fixture needed the editor's left-side OCR region; an initial unknown-format fixture was ordinary UTF-8 text and correctly opened in the text studio, so the fallback fixture now contains binary bytes rather than assuming an unknown extension implies binary content.

The debug build and formatting/whitespace checks pass. Architecture comparison adds no excessive-complexity function, cycle or clone group beyond the preceding checkpoint; total clone warnings remain +19 versus the original baseline with duplication below baseline. This covers the shared Files external actions, not all external-tool controls embedded inside individual studios. Audio/MIDI playback, preview-cache reads, recovery, offline/exclusion features and the other recorded Phase 6 gaps still require completion.


## Audio result playback admission — 2026-10-19

Waveform clicks in List/Music and Music-range Space playback now retain the result's original source guard. The guard runs before decoder preparation and again before accepting it, using the same checked-read operation as studio opens and guarded Reveal. The loader reuses the existing cancellable preview task instead of maintaining another ticket/receiver lifecycle. Audio decoder/range preparation remains separate from UI/request admission, retaining the shared file/decoder helpers used by spectrum, waveform and zero-crossing work.

An existing player may be reused for seeking only while the selected path and search observation remain the same. A new accepted search publication or changed source revision retires that player/load rather than carrying the old observation into a refreshed result at the same pathname. This does not silently adopt a replacement during an old request. Range bounds and existing FileSource/zero-crossing checks remain in force.

The private dark run passes `/tmp/clip-audio-search-20261019a/report.json`: after a valid two-second waveform preview is visible, replacing the source with invalid bytes produces the search-source error rather than entering ordinary audio preparation; moving the parent outside the source and replacing it with a symlink is also rejected. No index is created. The test uses silent audio. The serial file-manager suite passes 621 tests with one intentionally ignored (`/tmp/clip-audio-search-verified-tests.log`). The shared checked-read consolidation passes the same 621-test suite (`/tmp/clip-checked-read-final-tests.log`), the debug build, formatting and whitespace checks. Its light audio regression passes `/tmp/clip-audio-search-20261019b/report.json`. The final dark studio/Reveal regression also passes all 17 observations (`/tmp/clip-checked-read-actions-20261019a/report.json`). Architecture measurement has no newly excessive-complexity function or increased cycle count; clone warnings remain, including ordinary loader lifecycle/test scaffolding, with total duplication below the starting baseline. MIDI playback, passive preview-cache reads and studio-specific external controls have not been declared covered by this checkpoint. The complete Phase 6 objective remains open, including the previously listed functionality and quality/performance gates.


## Configurable search exclusions — 2026-10-19

`[file_manager.search].excluded_paths` now holds up to 128 exact, reversible absolute file/folder addresses (8 KiB each). Edit → Search settings → Excluded search paths edits the same persisted list; an excluded folder includes its descendants.
