The default is empty, and saving preferences never enrolls a root or creates an index. Decision: begin with literal component-prefix addresses instead of inventing an ambiguous glob language. Paths under a particular enrolled root naturally provide exclusions for that subtree; glob rules or independent per-root hidden/budget overrides remain separate work. Mandatory protected/generated/credential exclusions still apply independently and cannot be removed by preferences. Invalid stored exclusion syntax fails closed for background search while leaving settings repair available.

The same parsed exclusion paths gate enrollment, reader-side directory inventories, recursive/automatic/content traversal, queued indexing work, Q/A extraction and cached lexical/semantic candidates. Raw Unix path bytes survive helper transport through the existing list codec. Prefix matching respects components: excluding `Private` does not exclude `Private-ish`. Cached exclusions run before each component candidate cap, including exact filenames and FTS/vector ranking. Index hidden-file policy is now also enforced through actual verified memberships at query time, independently of the browser's hidden toggle, so paused reconciliation cannot expose old hidden-index coverage after that policy is disabled.

An accepted scope-policy change invalidates displayed remote results and pending queries, stops browser playback, and clears cached answer cells while preserving the session's questions. Existing semantic-only maintenance invalidation remains distinct. Indexing policy changes retire queued work/watch coverage and queue fresh guarded reconciliation of configured roots. Saving exclusions removes matching generated references and unreferenced vectors from the active cache even while indexing is paused; unrelated allowed memberships survive. Storage cleanup failures remain visible and do not authorize excluded queries or reads. Startup/recovery cleanup and independent per-root overrides have not been declared complete by this checkpoint.

Validation: 85 focused search tests and 625 serial file-manager tests pass (one intentionally ignored in the latter), alongside the core config persistence test, debug build, formatting and whitespace checks (`/tmp/clip-exclusions-search-tests.log`, `/tmp/clip-exclusions-file-manager-tests.log`, `/tmp/clip-exclusions-config-tests.log`). Tests cover invalid/reversible non-UTF-8 addresses, literal subtree boundaries, helper inventory filtering, source preservation, exclusion before the 500-candidate cap, and cached hidden-policy filtering. Architecture comparison has no newly excessive-complexity function or increased cycle count; the combined index eligibility rule is shared across directory scanning, queued files and retirement. Existing clone warnings persist (+21 versus the original baseline), with total duplication below baseline.

The private dark run passes `/tmp/clip-exclusions-20261019b/report.json`: excluded content is not searched before enrollment, an explicitly indexed folder omits its excluded subtree, changing exclusions removes an already indexed file/vector while paused, the previous search view retires, resuming exposes only the allowed sibling, removing exclusions re-includes ordinary files, whole-root exclusion empties generated data and blocks Index from undoing the exclusion, mandatory `.git` exclusion persists, and all source bytes remain unchanged. Its first attempt was deliberately stopped after finding that the runner toggled an already-expanded disclosure closed; the fixture now tracks its own disclosure action and clears the editor explicitly for an empty list. The strengthened script also asserts that the old matching-results status disappears; that assertion was checked against the successful run's retained screenshot text. The hidden indexing on/off/on regression passes `/tmp/clip-exclusions-hidden-regression-20261019a/report.json`. The light settings-without-enrollment regression passes `/tmp/clip-exclusions-settings-regression-20261019b/report.json` after replacing its off-screen empty-root-label expectation with the new visible section label; it still asserts empty persisted enrollment and no index file.

## Matching passages and source provenance — 2026-10-19

Search results now retain at most three contributing segment IDs per file without adding their scores together. After winning files pass source verification, the worker fetches bounded excerpts and their actual locations/coverage from those exact segments. Keyword results use FTS5 snippets around the matching terms, preserving original case; semantic-only results use a bounded beginning of the contributing segment. Text is not re-extracted or re-embedded for display. Segment lookups are constrained by both file path and model pipeline. On-demand content searches retain their literal matching context; cross-chunk matches explicitly describe the ending chunk rather than inventing a precise start location. Stopping at an early content match discloses when remaining content was not scanned.

List, Music and Video rows reserve a compact provenance footer, with parent directory and optional excerpt. Photos exposes the same evidence through hover. Hover provides up to three contributing passages and coverage; name-only matches make no content claim. A new pending query labels retained rows as previous results instead of presenting old excerpts as current evidence. Footer clicks participate in the existing selection/context/drag response.

Validation: 88 focused search tests and 628 serial file-manager tests pass (one intentionally ignored in the latter), plus the debug build. The added tests cover bounded/deduplicated evidence, Unicode case expansion and clipping, a keyword deep within a segment, and rejecting another file's segment or another model pipeline. Private dark `/tmp/clip-explanations-20261019d/report.json` and light `/tmp/clip-explanations-light-20261019a/report.json` runs pass: same-basename files in different folders display distinct parent provenance and real snippets, footer selection copies the correct file reference, literal search does not create an index, explicit enrollment enables indexed keyword explanations, and source bytes remain unchanged. Initial native attempts looked for an off-screen second footer; the runner now closes the Shelf through its toolbar before checking both rows. OCR had also confused that button with a Shelf label inside collection cards, so this one action uses its verified private-run toolbar coordinates. Architecture comparison adds no excessive-complexity function or increased cycle count for this checkpoint; existing clone warnings remain (+22 versus baseline), with total duplication below baseline.

## Lexical results before model readiness — 2026-10-19

Embedding queries now publish a verified filename/keyword result set before starting or querying the embedding helper, and keep the same request active for its final hybrid publication. If model setup/loading/inference fails, the lexical results remain usable with an explicit model-unavailable explanation. Cancellation still discards obsolete generations. This reuses the existing single coordinator and result transport; it does not add a second model instance or another indexing service.

Lexical retrieval accepts retained text from the current extraction format across model pipeline versions, while vector scoring continues to require the exact active pipeline. An inventory membership awaiting startup reconciliation may supply lexical candidates, but each winning source must pass the same fresh bounded helper verification before publication or an excerpt is displayed. This permits cached keyword search without requiring the model for a startup crawl; changed or unavailable sources are omitted. Checking memberships cannot contribute vector scores. Old extraction versions and changed/pending revisions remain excluded.

With no enrolled roots, an embedding command falls back to ordinary filename matching without creating a catalog or loading a model. The same fallback applies to a current folder outside existing enrollment; it does not implicitly enroll that folder. An explicitly empty indexed scope returns an empty explanation without creating a database. Recursive queries with mixed indexed/unindexed coverage still report indexed coverage rather than claiming complete semantic coverage.

Validation: 89 focused search tests pass, including model-free FTS evidence, obsolete extraction rejection, and checking-membership separation from vector scoring. The initial implementation passed the 629-test serial file-manager suite (one intentionally ignored) and all 24 Python model-helper tests; the final broader regression is recorded below when complete. Debug build, rustfmt and whitespace checks pass. Private dark `/tmp/clip-lexical-first-20261019a/report.json` and strengthened light `/tmp/clip-lexical-first-20261019b/report.json` pass: filename fallback with no index; close/reopen after removing only the private model symlink; cached keyword excerpts without model files; changed-source rejection; and, in the strengthened run, empty indexed scope without database creation plus an unrelated unindexed folder remaining outside enrollment. The normal hybrid/excerpt light regression also passes `/tmp/clip-lexical-first-hybrid-20261019a/report.json`. Architecture comparison has no newly excessive-complexity function or increased cycle count; clone warnings remain (+23 versus baseline), with total duplication below baseline.

The final serial regression passes 629 file-manager tests with one intentionally ignored (`/tmp/clip-lexical-first-final-file-manager.log`), after the unrelated-folder fallback and checking-membership assertions. The final focused suite passes 89 tests (`/tmp/clip-lexical-first-final-tests.log`). The full Phase 6 objective remains open, including recovery, offline results/reconnect behavior, broader extractors and Q/A coverage, performance/quality measurements and final integration.

## Explicit recovery of an unreadable cache — 2026-10-19

Search settings now offers **Recover unreadable index…**, followed by **Preserve old cache and reset**. This deliberately creates an empty cache rather than attempting to repair unknown/corrupt rows. It first persists indexing pause and retires pending work/results, then closes SQLite while retaining the application ownership lock. The closed database and any WAL, SHM or rollback journal are moved without replacement into a private `.search-recovery-*` folder. Configured roots and model files remain; a valid backup can be restored through the existing restore action afterward. Recovery archives are intentional retained copies, outside active search; ordinary index clear does not erase those copies.

The ownership lock/directory capability is independent of successfully opening SQLite. A small bounded recovery journal records the archive name and original file identities before the first move. Startup completes that accepted move before opening or creating SQLite; it refuses ambiguous replacements, symlinks and unexpected files. Directory synchronization orders archive publication, file-set movement and journal retirement before a fresh database is created. Explicit lock release also prevents a transient inherited descriptor in a concurrent fork from extending the retiring owner's lifetime. Unknown future schemas still fail ordinary open without reset; only the explicit recovery action replaces them, preserving their old files.

A native failure exposed SQLite removing invalid sidecars while attempting to open a database with a broken header. Live index open now checks the ordinary file's SQLite header before giving it to SQLite, sharing the header reader with backup validation. This preserves that damaged file set for recovery. This is a header check, not a promise to diagnose every form of B-tree/WAL corruption without SQLite recovery behavior. Schema upgrades now commit all required steps in one transaction; a targeted failure test proves rollback includes earlier column/index changes and the schema version.

Validation so far: 95 focused search tests pass, including corrupt-main/sidecar preservation, interrupted main/WAL movement, ownership retention/release, changed-source and symlink refusal, future-schema preservation and migration rollback (`/tmp/clip-recovery-final-tests.log`). The pre-migration-consolidation broader regression passes 634 tests with one ignored (`/tmp/clip-recovery-final-file-manager.log`). Private dark `/tmp/clip-recovery-20261019c/report.json` passes actual unreadable-startup → confirmed recovery → backup restore, including exact supplied DB/WAL/SHM bytes in the archive, unchanged source/backup/model files, retained configured enrollment and persistent pause. The first native attempt exposed sidecar removal before header validation; the second was explicitly interrupted after OCR clicked the recovery words in the error banner instead of the actual button. The corrected runner anchors the maintenance button below the Restore source field. Final light and broader checks are recorded below when complete.

Architecture note: invoking the baseline-establishment tool with the existing baseline name refreshed that snapshot rather than merely retrieving it. The pre-recovery snapshot was preserved under `phase6-pre-recovery`; subsequent recovery comparisons use that baseline. Historical global measurements in this ledger remain historical observations, rather than claims about the refreshed baseline. Recovery adds no excessive-complexity function or cycle; clone warnings are +1 against this pre-recovery snapshot, with total duplication slightly lower. The shared header reader and one migration transaction avoid duplicating validation or scattering partial upgrades.

The authorized real corpus was inventoried by metadata only: 249 visible ordinary files, 1,116,818,911 bytes, across text/PDF/image/audio/video/3D/binary formats. No real-corpus embedding-generation or retrieval-quality gate is claimed by this inventory. A separate read-only private-instance audit script now records modality counts, unsupported/failed coverage, source metadata preservation and exact-name retrieval, without treating that smoke test as the planned semantic-quality benchmark.

The final serial regression passes 635 file-manager tests with one intentionally ignored (`/tmp/clip-recovery-atomic-file-manager.log`), including the atomic-migration change. The light settings/no-enrollment regression passes `/tmp/clip-recovery-settings-20261019a/report.json`, with its empty semantic-scope expectation updated to the verified filename-only fallback. Formatting and whitespace checks pass. The first real-corpus runner attempt stopped before enrollment because OCR omitted the red Index text even though the screenshot shows the correct browser and red button; initial readiness now uses the stable toolbar text and enrollment is still proved by actual database activity.

The final light recovery/restore flow passes `/tmp/clip-recovery-light-20261019a/report.json` on the consolidated migration/header implementation. The first real corpus crawl completed in roughly three minutes and preserved source metadata, but exposed three extraction-policy gaps: a 296 MB PDF rejected by an unintended 256 MiB cap, an unsupported large STL reported as a document-budget failure, and SVG sent through the raster decoder. Its smoke-test success is not a claim that these failures are acceptable or that semantic retrieval quality is validated; those gaps are being corrected before the next crawl.

## Real-corpus extractor corrections — 2026-10-19

The first private crawl of `/home/vega/Documents/type_testing` completed in 181.697 seconds: 249 indexed paths, 201 files with generated content, 45 explicitly unsupported files, and three failures. It stored 354 audio, 111 image and 886 text vectors. This is an exploratory mixed-corpus observation, not a controlled throughput benchmark or semantic accuracy gate. Exact-name retrieval and unchanged visible source identities/size/mtime/ctime were checked. The retained summary is `/tmp/clip-real-corpus-20261019b/corpus-summary.json`.

That run drove concrete fixes. PDF admission now uses the planned 512 MiB source limit rather than the accidental 256 MiB cap that rejected a valid 296 MB manual. Image admission/read bounds now use the planned 256 MiB limit. Supported documents retain their 64 MiB limit, and audio retains 64 GiB. An oversized supported input is explicitly name-only rather than a misleading decoder failure. Unsupported formats are classified before document size limits, so a large STL remains unsupported without attempting document decoding. Existing shared text classification admits ordinary source-code/configuration formats through the bounded plain-text extractor; this is normal document embedding, not a new code-search model mode, and never executes source code.

SVG now has a bounded static image path using the already-present resvg version, enabling text/raster features and reusing the application's bundled fallback font bytes. It does not scan host fonts or load file/network image references. Self-contained PNG/JPEG/GIF data images are allowed. Embedded SVG and other unsupported data-image formats are omitted with partial coverage: inspection of usvg 0.37 showed that its default embedded-SVG loader constructs fresh options before sanitizing children, bypassing a parent's external-resource resolver. The restricted resolver prevents that path entirely. Both raster dimensions are capped at 1024, and the existing disposable-reader time/memory limits remain in force. SVG is rendered on white because the model's RGB loader discards alpha; black text/path art must remain visible. This is a static snapshot with bundled-font fallback, not browser-equivalent animation/web rendering.

The extractor revision is now 3, so previously unsupported code/SVG records are reconsidered rather than remaining permanently name-only. Re-preparation runs in the existing background queue; unchanged canonical inputs under the same model pipeline may reuse their exact stored vectors. Schema remains version 4. The passage acceptance script now expects extractor revision 3. Sparse-file tests cover large unsupported STL and oversized PDF/text/image/audio without reading their payloads; tests also cover source-code extraction, bounded SVG output, external-image rejection, embedded-SVG resolver reset prevention, bundled text and malformed markup. The first post-fix focused suite passes 101 tests, the serial file-manager suite passes 641 with one ignored, and all 24 Python model-helper tests pass. The second real corpus crawl and final SVG-background checks are still in progress; no corrected-corpus success is claimed here yet.

One deliberate scope change: ordinary code/configuration files now receive bounded plain-text content indexing when the shared text classifier recognizes them, rather than the original semantic plan's code name/path-only treatment. The real corpus includes source files, and the existing guarded text mechanism handles them without new parsers or execution. This makes literal/technical-term search useful in explicitly enrolled project folders while retaining mandatory generated/credential exclusions. It does not enable a separate code-search task, execute project tooling, or enroll projects automatically.

The corrected real corpus crawl passes `/tmp/clip-real-corpus-20261019c/report.json`: 249 paths, 207 content-ready files, 42 explicitly unsupported files, no failed jobs, and 1,527 vectors (354 audio, 119 image, 1,054 text). It completed in 195.916 seconds, reported ready coverage, passed SQLite integrity/vector-shape checks and exact-name retrieval, and preserved source identities/size/mtime/ctime and visible membership. Its summary is `/tmp/clip-real-corpus-20261019c/corpus-summary.json`. This remains a smoke/integrity observation, not the full positive/negative semantic-quality benchmark.

The finished SVG background path passes all 101 focused tests (`/tmp/clip-corpus-fixes-final-tests.log`) and a real-model light native check `/tmp/clip-svg-search-20261019a/report.json`: a generated file named `diagram.svg` is retrieved by “a blue circle on a white background” solely through its visual vector, with a Semantic image match/static SVG image explanation. Its source stays unchanged. Debug build, rustfmt, whitespace and Python compilation checks pass, and all 24 Python helper tests pass. Architecture comparison adds no excessive-complexity function or cycle; clone warnings are +2 against the pre-recovery checkpoint, with total duplication 5.72% versus 5.73% there. The original project-wide baseline was refreshed as described above, so these are explicitly checkpoint deltas.

The final serial regression passes 641 file-manager tests with one intentionally ignored (`/tmp/clip-corpus-fixes-final-file-manager.log`), including the final white-background SVG path. These checkpoints do not complete the full Phase 6 objective; offline behavior, remaining planned extractors/coverage handling, performance and broader quality gates, and the outstanding action/packaging audits still need completion.

## Opt-in unavailable results and reconnection — 2026-10-19

A persistent **Show offline results** preference now enables last-known filename/keyword/vector matches in embedding queries. It defaults off. Only configured, non-disabled unavailable roots qualify; exclusions, mandatory path policy, hidden-entry policy, metadata constraints and component limits still apply before ranking. A stale candidate in a live root cannot take this fallback. An available overlapping membership also prevents a record from being reclassified as offline. Cached snippets retain their actual locations/coverage, with an explicit **Unavailable · last-known** label rather than fresh-source claims.

Publications containing last-known records use a metadata-only List, retaining the prior manual view for restoration when the search closes. Rich preview producers and automatic Music/Video activation are bypassed, image-hash jobs retire, drag payloads and mutations are disabled in that view, and unavailable-file open/copy/collection handoffs require reconnection plus a new search publication. The check also runs before viewer/inspector dispatch and in retained source guards, so reconnecting a pathname cannot silently upgrade an old result. This intentionally does not retain historical image/waveform/PCM data just to preview offline matches. Available rows remain openable through the normal guarded viewer, but the mixed publication itself uses the metadata list. This is an explicit UI/resource decision, not a claim of offline media-preview support.

Literal content/regex/name scoped traversal still needs current source observations. Its indexed scope now attempts configured unavailable roots through their original guarded identities, reports incomplete coverage when they cannot be checked, and does not masquerade as a complete zero-match search. Empty indexed enrollment does not create a database. The cached last-known toggle is currently specific to embedding-query retrieval (whose lexical component works without a loaded model); it does not invent live literal-content matches. Changing this preference retires the current remote view and pending results while preserving accepted filesystem-operation completion handling. Successful new search submissions also clear obsolete browser-operation errors, fixing a native regression where the previous unavailable-open warning remained below genuinely reconnected results.

Validation: 104 focused search tests, 644 serial file-manager tests (one intentionally ignored), and the core preserving-config test pass (`/tmp/clip-offline-final-tests.log`, `/tmp/clip-offline-final-file-manager.log`, `/tmp/clip-offline-config-tests.log`). Tests cover opt-in, protected/excluded/disabled paths, overlapping live membership and refusing old unavailable guards even when the source pathname is currently accessible. Debug build, formatting, whitespace and Python syntax checks pass. Architecture comparison adds no excessive-complexity function or cycle; clone warnings remain +2 against `phase6-pre-recovery`.

Private dark `/tmp/clip-offline-20261019d/report.json` passes default hiding, incomplete literal-scope reporting, persistent enable/disable, metadata-only unavailable audio rows, blocked copy/open, original-root identity retention during replacement, and restored current results after the original directory is reconnected/refreshed. Its bounded syscall trace covers the unavailable period and reports zero replacement-file payload accesses (`offline-source-trace.json`), with a positive control proving the tracer saw legitimate reads of that same pathname before replacement. Tracing preserves the launched application's PID for the private runner with strace's verified `-D` behavior. Earlier runner attempts exposed wrapper-PID ownership, out-of-range pointer scrolling, and the real obsolete-error issue described above; they are not counted as successful acceptance. Last-observation age, broader mount/session identity handling and full remote failure/reconnect coverage are still open, rather than inferred from this directory-replacement test.

The light traced counterpart also passes `/tmp/clip-offline-light-20261019a/report.json`, including zero replacement payload accesses and a clean current-result view after reconnection. Both native runs keep source replacement tests within their own synthetic directories and private compositor sockets. This verifies the described root-unavailability behavior; it does not close the remaining Phase 6 scope or imply a full NAS/mount-lifetime audit.

## Video extraction and retained partial coverage — 2026-10-19

Video files now enter the guarded disposable reader rather than remaining filename-only. Extraction uses the shared ordinary-video stream selector (skipping attached cover art), CPU FFmpeg with bounded output and a container/protocol allowlist, and the same mono 16 kHz PCM16 audio-window preparation as ordinary audio. Sparse visual sampling uses the midpoint of each five-second interval, including a shortened last interval; clips under five seconds get one midpoint. Above 1,024 frames it uses uniformly spaced bin centers across the full duration. This midpoint policy is a concrete choice within the plan's representative-frame requirement. Frames fit within 1,024 pixels on both axes, use the planned 140 vision-token budget, and carry the decoded frame's actual PTS relative to the container origin. Source decode admission permits up to 4K pixel count, including portrait frames, with each dimension at most 8,192; larger sources currently fail explicitly instead of entering an unbounded decoder. Audio uses the selected default track, 30-second windows with 25-second hops and the existing two-hour ceiling. Visual coverage stays labeled sampled, including after the final audio segment.

Timestamp testing found a genuine container difference: Matroska may report an absolute end timestamp as its format duration when its timeline begins above zero. Stream duration takes precedence; otherwise a valid Matroska DURATION tag supplies the stream's endpoint. This prevents a one-second clip beginning at timestamp five from being treated as six seconds long. FFmpeg showinfo supplies actual frame timestamps rather than labeling requested seeks as actual decoded locations. The decoder whitelist excludes HLS/concat playlists even when disguised as MP4. Installed MOV demuxer help confirms external track references and absolute aliases default disabled; those unsafe options are never enabled.

The writer now limits files to 2,048 completed segments. When a later extractor or inference step fails, already completed segments are published only after re-verifying the original root and file observation, with a bounded error and explicit partial coverage. With no completed segments the file remains failed; a changed source cannot authorize publishing earlier work. A failed inference instance retires before another file can restart it. Storage failures still follow the existing capacity/error path. Publication remains one verified per-file transaction, so completed segments become queryable at final success/failure or the segment ceiling, rather than streaming every intermediate segment while extraction continues. This is a deliberate initial simplification; the UI must not claim that in-progress segments have already been published.

Canonical cache keys retain compatibility for ordinary audio and 280-token image inputs. The 140-token video-image key includes that budget, preventing silent reuse of a vector generated with a different vision-token configuration. Extractor revision is now 4 (database schema stays 4), triggering background reconsideration of previously unsupported videos; exact compatible canonical inputs may still reuse vectors. The shared reader now installs its address-space/CPU limits at disposable-helper dispatch for ordinary requests as well as restore; previously only restore installed them. This closes that resource-admission gap without applying process-wide limits to the desktop application or in-process tests.

The focused suite passes 109 tests (`/tmp/clip-video-tests.log`), the serial file-manager suite passes 649 with one intentionally ignored (`/tmp/clip-video-file-manager-tests.log`), and all 24 Python helper tests pass. The private dark `/tmp/clip-video-search-20261019b/report.json` and light `/tmp/clip-video-search-light-20261019a/report.json` runs verify real frame/audio embeddings, source-relative timestamps, cross-modal visual retrieval and unchanged source observations. A synthetic file with an overstated stream duration verifies two earlier visual segments survive a later decode failure. Its stored vectors remain 1,536-byte f16 vectors. An earlier native attempt had an incorrect expected frame count in its fixture; it exposed no additional production fault and is not counted as acceptance. Those two passing runs precede the final clarification that retained segment counts and planned coverage are explicit in the partial label; final-build verification is recorded below when complete.

Architecture comparison against `phase6-pre-recovery` flags the pre-existing checkpoint delta of two clone groups, while overall duplication is 5.70% versus 5.73%. The initial new `prepare` function's excessive complexity was removed by separating coherent stream admission from extraction scheduling; the final comparison has no new excessive-complexity function and no cycle-count increase. The relocated six-line PCM WAV-header sequence also matches an existing test fixture; replacing this fixed canonical wire header with a new serialization dependency would add complexity without reducing a production mechanism. No audio-sample similarity feature has been implemented. Full Phase 6 remains incomplete, including broader quality/performance gates and remaining source/action/packaging audits.

Final-build verification passes the 109 focused tests again (`/tmp/clip-video-final-tests.log`), debug build (`/tmp/clip-video-final-build.log`), formatting/whitespace and Python syntax checks. The dark final-build acceptance is `/tmp/clip-video-search-20261019c/report.json`; its partial label explicitly says two completed segments were retained and distinguishes planned coverage from completed extraction. Real-corpus `/tmp/clip-real-corpus-video-20261019a/report.json` passes: 249 paths, 209 content-ready files, 40 unsupported, zero failures, 1,533 vectors (355 audio, 124 image, 1,054 text), and 198.664 seconds to complete indexing. Both previously unsupported real videos now have sampled visual coverage; one has an audio track and the other has none observed. Three WMA fixtures have explicit partial coverage because decoded output ends before their reported duration; the decoder's output, rather than an invented full duration, determines the represented audio length. Source observations remain unchanged and SQLite integrity/vector-shape and exact-name checks pass. This corpus check remains a smoke/integrity check, not the full retrieval-quality gate.

Visual inspection of the native screenshot confirms the green Index indicator, semantic match/footer location and ordinary video view. It also exposes a pre-existing adjacent limitation: the preview's general video-duration reader still uses container duration, so the synthetic offset Matroska clip displays six seconds in the preview header even though the semantic extractor correctly represents one second and labels the actual frame at 0.5 seconds. Sharing the corrected timeline-duration interpretation with preview metadata remains an audit item; the semantic timing tests do not establish that all existing video-preview metadata is corrected.

The final-build light counterpart also passes `/tmp/clip-video-search-light-20261019b/report.json`, including the explicit retained-segment/partial label. All Cargo and native jobs for this checkpoint have completed. The original two unrelated `config.toml` changes remain intact; no commits were made.

## Shared video timing and command-building controls — 2026-10-19

The previously disclosed offset-video preview-duration limitation is corrected. The existing reader's pure stream-timing interpretation now lives in `file_manager/media/timing.rs` and is shared by extraction and ordinary preview metadata. FFprobe preview requests include the selected stream's duration/start and Matroska DURATION tag. The preview timeline uses the selected video stream's end relative to the container origin instead of blindly trusting the format duration. This preserves a real delayed stream's leading interval while correcting a one-second clip whose container starts at timestamp five. Tests cover stream-duration precedence and malformed/nonfinite metadata; the strengthened real-model native check explicitly requires `00:01` alongside the correct semantic frame location.

Search now exposes an ephemeral **Literal** on/off button and an **Options** menu. Literal mode treats the entire input as a filename, including a leading colon and meaningful surrounding spaces, under the same 8 KiB admission limit. Ctrl+F preserves that mode while focusing an existing query; starting a new command entry or leaving the search resets the session mode. Options edits filename/regex/content/semantic/question payloads, comma-separated filters, scope, ordinary/hash/reference sorting and question result type. **Apply to draft** generates visible command text and validates it through the same Query parser; only Search/Enter submits it. Invalid controls leave the applied view intact. There are no hidden query overrides or automatic embedding jobs while editing options.

Selectors retain their parsed source spelling for reconstruction, so exact decimal boundaries, date-range delimiters, negation and globs survive editing. Operator payloads are quoted with the parser's own escaping rules. Literal colon names cannot become embedding commands when converted to a draft. A deliberate UI choice uses bounded **Insert filter** presets and an operator-mode chooser for discovery/completion, rather than inserting automatic completions into ordinary typing. The presets expose content families, globs, exclusion, a size range and modified/created since-today constraints; users can edit their exact syntax in either field or command bar. This keeps the existing type-to-navigate and explicit-submit behavior stable. It does not claim free-form inline typeahead completion is implemented.

Native verification caught an actual Escape-focus regression: Options menus are separate from egui's popup registry, and this egui version's returned menu inner value is not a reliable open-state indicator. The code tracks whether the menu contents were rendered and leaves keyboard handling to that menu while it is open. Escape closes Options and retains the applied result; Enter in a menu field cannot activate a previously selected browser entry through browser shortcuts. Earlier failed runner attempts included an OCR punctuation mismatch on a colon filename and the real menu-focus issue; they are not successful acceptance evidence.

The focused search suite passes 112 tests (`/tmp/clip-search-controls-tests.log`); the final serial file-manager regression passes 653 with one intentionally ignored (`/tmp/clip-search-controls-verified-file-manager.log`). All 24 Python helper tests pass, as do the final debug build, formatting, whitespace and Python syntax checks. Private dark `/tmp/clip-search-controls-20261019d/report.json` verifies literal colon names, filter/scope draft editing without execution, explicit recursive submission, invalid filter preservation, Escape focus, and no database creation. Private light `/tmp/clip-shared-video-timing-20261019b/report.json` verifies the corrected preview duration alongside real video embeddings and retained partial segments. Screenshots were visually inspected. Light controls verification is recorded separately after it completes.

Architecture comparison against `phase6-pre-recovery` adds no excessive-complexity function or cycle-count increase. The checkpoint's clone-group delta remains +2, with overall duplication reduced to 5.69% versus 5.73%. Draft interpretation is separated from applying results so the three input modes do not increase the existing application function's complexity. No commits were made and the two original unrelated configuration changes remain intact. Full Phase 6 remains active: these controls and timing checks do not close the broader quality/performance, freshness/source-action and packaging audits.

## Mounted-source boundaries and runtime identities — 2026-10-20

The mount audit found that device/inode comparisons alone admitted same-filesystem bind mounts inside a chosen root. Search now resolves relative paths from its retained root descriptor using Linux `openat2` with `RESOLVE_BENEATH | RESOLVE_NO_SYMLINKS | RESOLVE_NO_XDEV`. This rejects mount crossings anywhere along the path, including crossing out and back to the original filesystem. Core still classifies and reopens the exact approved pin before payload access. No pathname-based fallback weakens these constraints when the kernel cannot provide them. This requires the Linux openat2/statx mount facilities available on the target workstation; unsupported kernels fail explicitly rather than silently dropping mount isolation.

Inventory admission excludes nested mount points, including bind-mounted individual files, without counting intentional scope exclusions as failed enumeration. Other admission/metadata failures remain incomplete coverage. The same root-relative rules govern extraction, result verification, watch registration and missing-path checks. A missing descendant behind an unapproved mount cannot become evidence that indexed content was deleted. A held root also retains its mount identity during revalidation, so even binding the same directory inode over its old pathname is detected. Explicitly adding a nested mounted folder as its own root remains supported; mount crossing from its parent does not authorize it implicitly.

SourceRoot requests/results now carry an optional runtime mount observation. A current content request requires that observation; database-only records do not invent it. The existing coordinator obtains current observations through its guarded root probe and binds stored roots to the monitor's matching observed device/inode context. Subsequent requests retain that mount expectation. During one application lifetime, automatic reconciliation cannot silently adopt a changed mounted source; remove its enrollment and explicitly choose the mounted folder again. These mount IDs are **not persisted as reboot-stable volume identities** and database schema remains version 4. Persistent boot/filesystem/session identity, uncertain network-server identity and reconnects that preserve a kernel mount ID remain open audit work; this checkpoint does not prove those cases safe merely because the mount tests pass.

Ordinary background directory snapshots carry their observed mount ID, which also participates in their signature. Index status uses that observation, without filesystem work on the UI thread: an excluded nested mount stays red even when a lexically enclosing root is green. A chosen building/paused root is yellow; ready matching mount coverage is green. Q/A folder keys also include the mount, so pending/completed answers cannot survive a changed folder mount merely because path, device/inode, timestamps and content revision happen to match. Unknown observations are not treated as an established current content source.

Notification coverage and expected source identity now have separate lifetimes in the existing monitor. Resetting watches for preference changes, restore, clear or recovery preserves expected runtime sources while retiring scheduler IDs and watch registrations. This avoids both accidental mount adoption on a reset and stale database IDs after a restored/reset index reassigns them. Explicit root removal releases its expected source and closes active indexed searches immediately; otherwise an already displayed indexed result could have outlived enrollment removal. Accepted filesystem mutation completion handling is unchanged. Per-path source expectations remain session memory, not file xattrs or a new database cache.

Validation: the serial file-manager suite passes **655 tests with two intentionally ignored** (`/tmp/clip-search-mount-final-file-manager.log`). One ignored test is the new namespace-only mount test and is executed separately with `scripts/search_mount_checks.py`; `/tmp/clip-search-mount-checks-20261020e.json` passes same-filesystem directory/file bind exclusions, extraction and verification rejection, watch admission, guarded absence, cross-out-and-back paths, explicit nested-root extraction, and captured/held-root rejection after a same-inode bind replacement. Its parent owns and removes the fixture after the private user/mount namespace exits, so recursive cleanup cannot follow the deliberate bind loop. No host mounts are changed.

Private dark `/tmp/clip-mount-ui-20261020c/report.json` passes pixel checks for red/yellow/green indicators, explicit paused enrollment without a default database, enclosing-root exclusion, separate nested-root enrollment, literal content retrieval, indexed-result retirement on removal, and removal/rebinding/re-enrollment of the same source. All mounts and input injection stay within that run's private namespaces and Weston/Niri sockets. Light `/tmp/clip-mount-ui-light-20261020a/report.json` verifies the colors and initial mounted-folder cases; the expanded light removal/rebind result is recorded below when terminal. Earlier dark runs `/tmp/clip-mount-ui-20261020a` and `b` passed their respective smaller scopes; the first script's Pillow deprecation warning was corrected without changing the color assertions.

The real-model Q/A regression also passes `/tmp/clip-mount-context-answers-20261020a/report.json`: exact numeric/boolean values, both sort directions, preserved selection, return to a folder, session-only columns and no implicit database. The post-lifecycle light restart regression `/tmp/clip-mount-lexical-20261020a/report.json` passes cached keyword retrieval without model files, current source verification after restart, changed-source rejection and unenrolled filename-only behavior. All 24 Python helper tests, debug builds, formatting, whitespace and Python syntax checks pass. Final architecture comparison adds no excessive-complexity function or cycle count; duplication is 5.67% versus the pre-recovery checkpoint's 5.73%. Clone groups are +3, including a new seven-line test-fixture setup shared by independent replacement/mount invalidation tests; retaining those distinct behavioral tests does not introduce another production implementation. No commits were made, and the two original unrelated configuration edits remain intact. The full Phase 6 objective remains active, including broader quality/performance gates, persistent source identity/ages and final action/packaging checks.

The expanded light run also passes `/tmp/clip-mount-ui-light-20261020b/report.json`, including paused yellow, explicit root removal, immediate indexed-result retirement, same-source remount and successful explicit re-enrollment. Yellow and rebound-green screenshots were visually inspected. Final build is `/tmp/clip-search-mount-lifecycle-build.log`; every Cargo/native/check process for this checkpoint has completed. The runtime expectation cache remains distinct from database IDs so restore/recovery can rebuild scheduling without losing per-path source authority; tests cover that rebinding and explicit removal releasing the old expectation.


## Retrieval at 100,000 files / 150,000 vectors — 2026-10-20

The new `scripts/search_retrieval_benchmark.py` drives an explicitly ignored Rust test against the production SQLite/ranking code. It creates 100,000 ordinary owned fixture files and 150,000 normalized 768-dimensional f16 vectors, with one/two segments alternating by file. The vectors are synthetic (1,025 reusable prototypes varying in two components), all in the text modality. This is a retrieval **component** measurement, not a quality or end-to-end acceptance test: it excludes embedding inference, source verification, explanation hydration and UI publication. First-query timings are not disk-cold measurements. Reports include sample counts, binary/source hashes and the actual bundled SQLite query plans.

The initial release baseline completed exact/rare lexical cases at roughly 278/280 ms p95, but its common-keyword case had not completed its full sample series after the overall run exceeded ten minutes. That diagnostic was explicitly stopped, its completed evidence retained in `/tmp/clip-retrieval-baseline-150k-partial.json`, and its owned fixture removed after the child exited. This is **not** a measured common-query p95 or a claim that one query took ten minutes. The Python SQLite plans captured during diagnosis were provisional; subsequent reports use the actual Rust/bundled SQLite planner.

Retrieval now uses persistent covering indexes for path/file lookup, current-file predicates, FTS segment identity and membership admission, plus an indexed parent lookup and BLOB prefix bounds for directory scopes. These are derived indexes installed when opening an existing version-4 index and in canonical restore staging; the record/backup format remains version 4. FTS explicitly uses its small identity/predicate indexes rather than fetching vector/metadata payload pages for each match. SQLite's page-cache target is 32 MiB. Production queries still apply root availability/membership, visibility and scope constraints, and Rust applies mandatory/user exclusions and metadata constraints before a candidate consumes any of the 500 result slots. There is no premature SQL LIMIT.

Metadata JSON is fetched only when needed for constraints or a potentially admitted candidate. Filename and keyword retrieval share their row/admission loop and keep their distinct ranking rules. Exact segment decoding remains normalized and unchanged numerically; it now uses half's aligned batch conversion, which dispatches to supported CPU instructions. Tests compare every finite f16 bit pattern with the prior scalar normalization, bit for bit, and reject zero/nonfinite payloads. Invalid query vectors and nonfinite scores fail explicitly. Cached credential names are now rejected during retrieval too, independently of content-opening safeguards.

Cancellation reaches SQLite prepare/step through a request-scoped progress callback using the existing coordinator token. The callback is removed before subsequent work, including accepted writes. A test cancels a recursive SQL aggregation that has produced no result row, verifies interruption, then performs a later write while the old token remains cancelled. The benchmark driver owns the fixture's outer temporary directory and kills/reaps its owned process group before cleanup on timeout/interruption; `/tmp/clip-retrieval-timeout-cleanup.json` verifies the timeout path leaves no new fixture directory.

Final optimized component report: `/tmp/clip-retrieval-shared-150k.json`, with 21 warm samples per case:

| Case | Warm p95 |
| --- | ---: |
| Exact filename | 117.198 ms |
| Rare keyword | 113.709 ms |
| Common keyword | 448.219 ms |
| Hybrid, default semantic floor | 781.378 ms |
| Hybrid, broad floor | 836.242 ms |

The slowdown is resolved, but **these figures do not meet the 100 ms lexical / 500 ms warm hybrid targets**, and do not establish the two-second CPU-fallback end-to-end target either. The final JSON deliberately records `end_to_end_acceptance_proven=false`. This implementation streams vectors with bounded per-row buffers rather than adding a second full vector matrix. The broader quality/precision gates, model/source-verification/UI timings, persistent filesystem/session identity, full-source digest policy and remaining action/packaging audits stay open.

The final serial file-manager regression passes **661 tests with three explicitly ignored** in `/tmp/clip-retrieval-shared-file-manager.log`; the ignored tests are the music benchmark, isolated mount-namespace check and explicitly executed retrieval-scale benchmark. All 24 Python model-helper tests pass. Architecture review against `phase6-pre-recovery` adds no excessive-complexity function, cycle-count increase or clone group beyond the previous checkpoint's +3; overall duplication is 5.66% versus 5.73%. Sharing the lexical loop removes the two new retrieval clone groups found during review. No commits were made and the two original unrelated configuration edits remain intact.

Real-model dark UI regression `/tmp/clip-retrieval-hybrid-20261020b/report.json` passes exact-name priority, technical FTS terms, type constraints, pagination and sorting. Its fixture was corrected for the already implemented source-code indexing: all 105 small text/code files now receive one vector, rather than treating Rust files as name-only. The old hard-filter expectation failed because semantically related Rust files legitimately became candidates; the updated test verifies the correct filtered count and uses an explicit filename glob to bound its pagination fixture. The current-code UI reruns are recorded below after completion.

The current shared-retrieval binary also passes private dark `/tmp/clip-retrieval-shared-dark-20261020a/report.json` and light `/tmp/clip-retrieval-shared-light-20261020a/report.json`, including the no-database-before-enrollment assertion. Final sorted screenshots were visually inspected. The final optimized component build and debug application build are `/tmp/clip-retrieval-shared-release-build.log` and `/tmp/clip-retrieval-shared-debug-build.log`.

Strict Clippy is **not green**: the ordinary command stops on 18 vendored `ntsc-rs` diagnostics (`/tmp/clip-retrieval-clippy.log`). The application-only `--no-deps` check initially reports 273 diagnostics. Its two primary spans on added Phase-6 lines were nested conditionals in startup enrollment and command-prefix routing; these were collapsed without changing short-circuit behavior. The repeated check reports 271 diagnostics (`/tmp/clip-retrieval-clippy-verified.log`), with all remaining primary spans outside the current added-line ranges (`/tmp/clip-retrieval-clippy-final-diff-check.json`). ## Persisted mounted-source continuity and scoped objects — 2026-10-20

The previous mount checkpoint's schema-4/runtime-only limitation is superseded by schema **5**. Disposable reader helpers now capture the kernel boot UUID, mount namespace, ordinary mount ID, `STATX_MNT_ID_UNIQUE` lifetime ID when supplied, and the pinned filesystem type. The boot getter is a fixed bounded procfs identity read, never generic file content. These observations are source-session evidence, not a reboot-stable volume UUID or proof of a network server's transport lifetime. A matching known local mount lifetime (Btrfs, ext4, XFS or tmpfs) can retain its metadata-only fast path across application restarts. Boot/mount changes, missing unique identity, an observed availability gap, or an unknown/network filesystem across a store restart require fresh preparation. Canonical-input hashes can still avoid inference after that preparation. This conservative remote policy may cause substantial re-extraction on NAS roots; its cost and server reconnects hidden behind an unchanged kernel mount remain open validation/policy work. Local ZFS is currently conservative as an unclassified filesystem.

File identity is now `(device, inode, source scope)`, so a recycled object number or a separately enrolled bind mount cannot overwrite another source lifetime's file record. Hard links within the same observed mount still share their object. The scoped membership predicate applies to live and known-context offline retrieval. Legacy/restored records without source-session authority can appear only as explicitly enabled last-known offline records; they cannot authorize live semantic results. Rebinding notification watches preserves the expected source session. Availability loss keeps last-known vectors, while reconnection atomically clears membership verification and marks affected content pending before exposing an available source.

The version-4 migration rebuilds the file table transactionally with foreign-key enforcement temporarily disabled outside the transaction and restored afterward. It preserves file IDs and segment references, checks foreign keys before commit, and rolls back failed migrations. Backup restore accepts native version 4 or 5, imports only canonical bounded records, and discards archived source-session authority. Version-5 scoped objects remain distinct through restore using staging-local scopes, then require verification/preparation against the newly observed source. No restored mount identity is trusted as current.

Focused search tests pass **132 with two explicitly ignored**, and the serial file-manager suite passes **673 with three explicitly ignored** (`/tmp/clip-source-session-final-focused.log`, `/tmp/clip-source-session-file-manager.log`). Added cases cover known local restart reuse, boot changes, unknown observations, remote restart re-preparation, availability gaps, migration rollback, and both backup versions. The isolated namespace check `/tmp/clip-source-session-mount-checks.json` additionally proves that separately enrolled bind views of one actual backing inode receive different scoped file IDs. Private dark mount acceptance passes `/tmp/clip-source-session-mount-ui-20261020a/report.json`. Private light restore acceptance passes `/tmp/clip-source-session-restore-ui-20261020b/report.json`, including preserved vectors, reset verification, explicit resume and canonical vector reuse; the rendered final outcome was inspected. Its preceding run failed because the final count was below the settings viewport, and the test now scrolls to that existing assertion.

Architecture comparison retains the prior +3 clone groups, unchanged five-cycle count, and no new excessive-complexity function; duplication is 5.64% versus 5.73% at the original baseline. The last schema-4 retrieval timings above are historical measurements, not measurements of this new source-scope SQL. A current release-scale rerun and post-lint verification are still required. Full-source digests, broader quality/performance gates, remaining source-action audits and packaging remain open. No commits were made.


## Full-source reuse for bounded inputs — 2026-10-20

Changed/new files up to 64 MiB now receive a full SHA-256 through a disposable guarded source reader, using a fixed 64 KiB buffer and exact observed length. The reader checks the retained source and path revision after hashing. Larger files return no source digest without reading their payload for hashing; their existing bounded extraction and canonical segment-input reuse remain the fallback. Unchanged files under an established source session still use metadata reuse and do not start this hashing work on every application open.

A small optional `source_digests` derived table associates a full digest with a file's published extraction, decoding suffix, model pipeline, extraction version and coverage. Exact matches can reuse all extracted text/locations and vectors without repeating decoders or passage tokenization. The ordinary final helper verification, storage/vector admission and transactional publication still apply. Partial/interrupted/name-only publications do not create a reusable complete-source record; planned sampled video is eligible only for the known completed visual/audio coverage variants. Every replacement publication invalidates the previous source digest in the same transaction that replaces its segments. Removing an unreferenced file cascades its cache row, so this adds no independent unbounded history. The cache is installed on open and deliberately excluded from canonical backup imports: losing it affects performance, not identity or freshness authority, and native backup compatibility stays version 4/5.

This is a second-level optimization for exact byte equality; it does not use sampled fingerprints, authorize a stale source path, or imply the model understands all content. Hashing and extraction are separate reads on changed bounded sources. In particular this adds initial read/process overhead, and uncertainty on large network files can still require expensive re-extraction. Those costs remain part of the startup/remote performance audit.

Focused search verification passes **134 tests with two explicitly ignored** (`/tmp/clip-source-digest-final-focused.log`). The serial file-manager run passes **675 with three explicitly ignored** (`/tmp/clip-source-digest-final-file-manager.log`); the final follow-up focused run also covers the narrowed sampled-coverage cache predicate. All 24 Python helper tests pass (`/tmp/clip-source-digest-python-tests.log`). The current debug application builds successfully (`/tmp/clip-source-digest-build.log`). Tests cover verified known bytes, stale observations, oversized sparse sources, exact format/model/digest matching, revision changes, invalidation after partial replacement, no stale revision publication, vector roundtrip and cascading removal. Architecture cleanup separates fingerprint admission from extraction, reuses the existing store fixture, and retains the previous +3 clone groups / no new excessive-complexity functions. Native real-model reuse verification is recorded after it completes below.

The release-scale rerun for the schema-5 source-scope predicate, before adding this optional non-query cache, is `/tmp/clip-source-session-retrieval-150k.json`. Warm p95: exact filename **130.152 ms**, rare keyword **125.130 ms**, common keyword **497.112 ms**, default hybrid **811.108 ms**, broad hybrid **870.584 ms** (100,000 files / 150,000 vectors / 21 warm samples). This still misses the 100 ms / 500 ms component targets. The report retains exact source/binary hashes; its source hash is historical after the later cache edit, and it does not establish model/source-verification/UI latency or retrieval quality. No performance target or goal-completion claim follows from a passing benchmark process.


Native CUDA reuse acceptance passes `/tmp/clip-source-digest-reuse-ui-20261020a/report.json`: one generated vector remains one through a cross-directory rename, a copy and a metadata-only touch, with three reuse completions. An actual text change then generates the second vector. The test checks populated full-digest cache records, equal source digests for the identical copies, orphan removal, and refusal to adopt a replacement enrolled root. The metadata-only outcome screenshot was visually inspected. The final application-only strict Clippy check still fails on **269 diagnostics elsewhere**, with none in `file_manager/search/` (`/tmp/clip-source-digest-final-clippy.log`); this is not a green strict-lint claim. The optional cache introduces no further architecture clone group or high-complexity function after cleanup. A broader workspace run is recorded after completion. No commits were made, and the original appearance/audio-spectrum configuration edits remain intact.


The ongoing source-action audit identified a remaining passive-preview gap: `ui/list.rs` passes the offline metadata-only flag but does not pass the search `SourceGuard` into `EntryPreviews` or the video timeline cache. `shelf/preview.rs::Source` carries paths/revisions for those readers, rather than the enrolled mount/session context. Opening studios and audio playback already use `SourceGuard::read_checked`; live passive rows need equivalent worker-side source checks and cache-context retirement. Existing revision-aware decoders reduce replacement exposure but are not evidence of the full mounted-source contract. The fix must retain functioning previews, rather than treating all live search results as metadata-only. MIDI rows use captured parsed documents for playback, but their source/context lifecycle also needs an explicit audit. This is open work, not a completed safety claim.

## Passive preview source contexts — 2026-10-20

The passive-preview gap identified above now has worker-side checks. Each published search result set supplies one shared immutable context containing the observed entries and original source expectations. Preview paths absent from that result set fail closed. Context generations are independent of index IDs and preview appearance/resolution; entering/leaving search or publishing another result set cancels old cache jobs and drops their data before painting. Browser shortcuts also see the new context before accepting playback, and old audio/MIDI preview ranges are cleared. This preserves deliberately accepted copy/export jobs through the existing stop mechanisms.

The existing `SourceGuard::read_checked` wraps payload-backed listing/music/image/MIDI/text/font/binary/material previews. PDF page strips and hover rasters, full photo/RAW thumbnails, image hover previews, material spheres and their hover, and gallery header probes retain the same context. Scene strips check the source around initial loading and each progressive render; scene hover checks each worker render. Video metadata is checked before its early publication, each progressive timeline image is checked before entering its bounded channel, and persistent hover decoders check each returned frame. Pure rasterization from already captured text/MIDI/font/binary documents keeps its in-memory inputs; context changes retire those rasters with the original payload cache. Ordinary non-search previews retain their prior behavior.

Cache invalidation and ordinary preview retirement have distinct lifetimes: changing view/spectrum or clearing a hidden cache does not erase its source expectations. Shared context transition logic and the existing test fixture/waiting mechanisms remove repeated setup and reset code. The parent `EntryPreviews` owns context propagation for its private PDF/material/image-hover subcaches. This checkpoint reduces the goal's clone-group delta from +3 to **+2** against `phase6-pre-recovery`, with no new excessive-complexity function or cycle-count increase. It introduces no new model, database schema, enrollment default or configuration setting.

The serial file-manager suite passes **678 tests with three explicitly ignored** (`/tmp/clip-preview-context-verified-file-manager.log`). New regressions cover fail-closed unpublished paths, shared generation identity, cache retirement, retaining context through spectrum/view retirement, restoring ordinary previews after search, and preventing an unverified video progress snapshot from entering the UI channel. All 24 Python helper tests pass (`/tmp/clip-preview-context-helper-tests.log`); formatting, whitespace and Python syntax checks pass. The debug application builds (`/tmp/clip-preview-context-build.log`). Strict application-only Clippy remains at 269 diagnostics (`/tmp/clip-preview-context-final-clippy.log`), so the repository does not have a green strict-lint result.

Private dark `/tmp/clip-preview-context-ui-20261020d/report.json` passes the stale-admission scenario: a file changes while its result is outside the preview viewport, then scrolling it into view performs three traced source metadata observations and **zero payload reads**. F5 admits the new revision and its 100-line preview, with 14 traced payload reads. A subsequent image-only query renders the checked Photos thumbnail, verified by its fixture pixels. No index is created. The syscall assertion uses fresh trace offsets after mutation and descriptor annotations, so earlier directory enumeration cannot satisfy the preview-admission proof. Earlier runner attempts found test issues: a pointer placed on the Shelf rather than the list, an expectation of a text-strip error where the UI intentionally produced an alert row, and a toolbar-word lookup using the listing's horizontal region. These attempts are retained as failures, not acceptance evidence.

Private light real-model video regression passes `/tmp/clip-preview-context-video-ui-20261020a/report.json`, preserving explicit enrollment, source timestamps, visual/audio segment counts, retained partial samples, semantic explanations and duration display. Photos and video-result screenshots were visually inspected. The video screenshot captures early metadata/preview publication; this is not proof that the entire progressive strip completed or that GPU/CPU performance targets passed. The offline/source-replacement regression is recorded below after completion.

The broader source-action audit remains open. In particular `viewers.rs::load` returns `Loaded::Path(FileSource)` for PDF/scene/model inputs, but only its audio handoff consumes that observation; the PDF, splat and model constructors start additional asynchronous reads. Their deferred payload workers need the original source context too, rather than relying only on verification around the preliminary load request. Parsed MIDI playback uses captured score data rather than rereading the source, and now stops on a result-context transition; export/studio handoffs still require the final audit. Quality/precision gates, latency targets, large-document/visual Q&A coverage and packaging also remain incomplete. No commits were made.

Offline/source-replacement native acceptance also passes `/tmp/clip-preview-context-offline-ui-20261020a/report.json` in light appearance, including zero replacement-payload access, persistent opt-in, metadata-only last-known rows, rejected copy/open, disabling offline visibility and returning to the original source. ## Deferred PDF/model/scene inputs — 2026-10-20

Shared studios now accept an optional `file_source::ReadCheck`, a worker-side callback that retains the originating source observation without making studio modules depend on file-manager search types. `SourceGuard::read_checked` delegates to that common before/after validation protocol. The original public constructors keep their existing behavior; Files search passes its retained check into the extended constructors before they submit deferred jobs.

Model and splat/mesh-fallback loaders check around parsing and initial preparation. PDF metadata, progressive page generation, selected-page detail rerenders, outline/page-text indexing, layout extraction, OCR input processing and image extraction carry the same check. Cached raster decoding uses its already captured bytes; it does not reopen the selected document. A failed detail rerender now exposes its error even while the prior raster remains visible, so a retained page is not silently described as current.

A deliberate behavior choice: search-opened PDFs stay on the selected source rather than opportunistically substituting a pre-existing OCR sibling. Such substitution would otherwise discard the result's observation and provenance. Ordinary workbench/folder PDF opens retain their existing OCR reuse behavior. Explicit PDF edits/OCR still produce separate owned derivatives. Input validation ends before installation; after a successful publication the newly authorized derivative follows the ordinary viewer workflow, rather than remaining bound to the old selected file revision. A regression verifies that loss of the old input context after completed staging does not discard an accepted PDF publication or hide its successful result.

The full workbench library suite passes **1,332 tests with 11 explicitly ignored** (`/tmp/clip-studio-source-final-library-tests.log`). The preceding 1,333-test run predates consolidating two raster tests into one positive/negative sequence; no assertion was dropped to reduce that count. The revised sequence verifies the selected page's successful early publication, rejection of an in-flight later page when its source changes, and rejection before rendering the next page. Other additions cover source checks before model/scene parsing, detail rerenders and returning decoded values.
