## Matched Q8/BF16 model-weight comparison — 2026-10-21

Added scripts/search_model_precision.py as an explicit evaluation driver, reusing setup.download/digest, quality.py's actual readers/worker, production ranking and the matched f32 oracle. It checksum-verifies the pinned official Q8 files and both official BF16 files at model revision bfcd298762cc34d0357ece5ebdd31791a3a374d8. BF16 bytes live only in a separate reference cache (`~/.cache/little-screen-clip/search-reference-bf16`, directory 0700, weights 0600); application Q8 weights/settings/index roots are unchanged. Downloads occur only with --download-reference. The helper's fixed Q8 basenames are privately aliased to actual verified variant files in a temporary directory; manifests record real names, sizes and hashes, and aliases are cleaned after each run. The runtime and model processing code are unchanged.

quality.Worker now retains SHA-256 hashes of canonical successful requests before protocol IDs. This evaluation-only record binds all prepared image/audio bytes, text passages, tokenization requests and query strings in order. The driver refuses a precision claim when either requests or selected backend/device differ. The original rank-comparison function is factored into a generic matched comparison plus the existing one-point storage policy, avoiding another scoring/fusion implementation. The model gate compares float32 working representations to isolate weights and additionally reports canonical f16 retrieval; both use fixed application defaults, never separately recalibrated candidate floors.

The actual CUDA/3090 experiment **passes** (`/tmp/clip-model-precision-20261021a/report.json`). Both variants generated **88 segments** from 60 owned synthetic files, with **242 identical successful model requests** on CUDA0 (NVIDIA GeForce RTX 3090). The text/projection files are Q8 309,855,456 / 554,821,024 bytes and BF16 557,950,176 / 982,074,784 bytes. Both models achieve **72/72 positive Recall@10** and every modality 100%, with all ten exact-name queries first and both f16/f32 storage gates passing. Q8 has **2/32** negative queries with any result, BF16 **1/32**. Q8 Recall@10 loss is **zero percentage points** in both f32 and canonical-f16 comparisons, within the five-point model target.

Three top-ten lists differ: text-06 gains an extra above-floor audio result in BF16; audio-01 changes the order of lower-ranked audio alternatives; negative-11 has a marginal Q8 audio hit (0.6407686 against the 0.64 audio floor) that BF16 omits. Expected-file ranks are unchanged across all 104 search cases. Maximum matched returned-segment score differences are **0.0066913962** in f32 and **0.0067015290** in canonical f16 (90 matched occurrences). The complete per-model reports and prepared vectors are retained. These real-model synthetic checks support retaining Q8 for these fixtures, not general equivalence. Component elapsed values include reader/preparation work and are not inference-only speed comparisons, cold-load measurements or VRAM evidence.

All **37 search-model Python tests** pass (`/tmp/clip-model-precision-python-tests-20261021.log`), plus four ranking/precision gate tests. New gate coverage exercises the five-point boundary and refuses comparisons when request hashes differ. Syntax and whitespace checks pass. ## Runtime backend, memory and controlled model-page startup probes — 2026-10-21

Added the standalone scripts/search_model/runtime_probe.py, reusing the actual owned Worker and smoke fixtures, with an optional post-spawn callback only in the evaluation wrapper. No application runtime, inference settings, source reader, index root or model weight is changed. It measures explicit CUDA/Vulkan/CPU selection in text-only and media-capable helpers, short and near-limit queries, a four-item near-context text batch, a 1024-square image at the production 280-token budget and a maximum 30-second prepared mono audio window. Every response passes finite/unit-normalized vector checks. GPU profiles use 21 warm samples, CPU seven; CPU p95 is deliberately absent. Request timings include helper IPC and validation under concurrent telemetry, not kernel-only inference.

A PID-scoped nvidia-smi XML sampler includes both graphics and compute process entries, so Vulkan memory is not silently omitted by a CUDA-only query. Unknown/unlisted values remain unknown, not zero. `/proc/PID/status` supplies process RSS/high-water values. GPU peaks are sampled, while RSS high-water is kernel-reported; RSS includes file-backed mappings and is not exclusive system RAM. The sampler keeps at most 10,000 raw records but preserves lifetime peak aggregates and discloses record truncation. New tests cover PID isolation, unavailable memory, bounded media fixtures, mincore and peak retention after raw-record eviction.

Cold probing uses checksum-verified private model copies, fsync, per-file POSIX_FADV_DONTNEED and mincore verification before spawning; it does not flush global caches or touch the installed model files. The first `/tmp` attempt (`/tmp/clip-runtime-cuda-text-20261021a/report.json`) correctly reports nonresident verification **false** because `/tmp` is tmpfs; its startup timing is not used as cold evidence. The six final b runs store private copies on local Btrfs under `~/.cache/little-screen-clip/search-runtime-probes/20261021-<backend>-<mode>-b/`. Every run verifies zero resident model-file pages before startup. This is controlled model-page-cache cold, not physical-disk/system cold. All six helpers are reaped and private copies are removed.

Authoritative summary: `/tmp/clip-runtime-probe-summary-20261021.json`, matrix process results `/tmp/clip-runtime-probe-matrix-20261021.json`; individual report paths are in the summary. Startup is one fresh-process observation per mode, not a startup percentile.

| cuda-media | 1376.3 | 1526.0 | 1209.8 |
| vulkan-text | 833.2 | 592.0 | 950.4 |
| vulkan-media | 1329.2 | 1389.0 | 808.8 |
| cpu-text | 698.4 | Not listed | 900.6 |
| cpu-media | 1259.8 | Not listed | 1684.1 |

| Media-capable helper request | CUDA p95 ms (21) | Vulkan p95 ms (21) | CPU median ms (7) |
|---|---:|---:|---:|

Both GPU backends meet the existing 2 GiB steady embedding-model VRAM target in all captured ready/after-request snapshots. CPU has no listed GPU allocation in these observations; the report does not fabricate a zero or a GPU-target pass. Results support retaining the existing CUDA → Vulkan → CPU preference and batch bounds. Individual first-request times, resource samples, source/native/runtime/weight hashes and raw first vectors are retained in each report.

Numerical backend agreement (`/tmp/clip-runtime-backend-agreement-20261021.json`) uses normalized double-precision cosine over saved first vectors matched by case and batch position. Minimum cosines against CUDA are Vulkan text **0.9998518642**, Vulkan media **0.9983817720**, CPU text **0.9998591397**, CPU media **0.9985276061**. Outputs are close but not identical. This is not a retrieval-quality gate; mixed-backend index/query quality should be checked directly before accepting backend-independent vector reuse as fully verified. Do not force full index regeneration solely from these numerical differences without quality evidence.

After the six b measurements, a peak-retention regression was added so raw-record eviction cannot erase earlier peaks. All **41 search-model Python tests** pass (`/tmp/clip-runtime-probe-summary-python-tests-20261021.log`). A fresh current-code CUDA media check (`~/.cache/little-screen-clip/search-runtime-probes/20261021-cuda-summary-c/report.json`) verifies nonresident model pages, preserved resource aggregates, no truncation, helper reaping and complete private-copy cleanup. It uses one warm sample per case and is not a percentile replacement for the b reports. No staging/commits or personal configuration changes were added. The next common-keyword experiment should investigate a query-scoped SQL scalar predicate reusing Top::accepts before expensive membership/session lookups. It may reject only rows already dominated by fully admitted candidates; all surviving candidates must retain the existing hard filters, natural-name tie ordering and explanation merging. A request-local shared Top can avoid a second eligibility cache, with scoped unregister/cleanup on errors. Benchmark equality, corruption/filter behavior, dynamic exclusions, ties and callback lifetime before adoption. This is only an investigation direction, not implemented or a claimed speedup.

## Query-local lexical rank threshold before membership checks — 2026-10-21

Implemented the previously proposed keyword-scan optimization using the existing exact Top::accepts comparison. A query-local nondeterministic/direct-only SQLite predicate rejects a posting only if it cannot contribute to the already admitted lexical list. Complete SQL membership/source/session checks and Rust policy/metadata admission still apply to every surviving candidate; only those fully admitted entries update the shared Top. This does not cap FTS postings before hard filters, reorder ties, cache eligibility, skip semantic scope scoring or replace BM25/rank fusion. Retained paths are always permitted through the rank predicate so additional matching locations can still merge.

LexicalRankFilter owns the shared Top for one lexical pass and restores it when dropped, including error paths; it unregisters the scalar callback before later work. SQLite callbacks require owned Send state, so one Arc<Mutex<Top>> shares the existing bounded ranking object instead of cloning a second threshold/path set or adding a worker. Shared path-argument decoding is reused with the existing filename callback. The Name pass uses the same ownership guard without adding a new name-query SQL filter. No schema/version/pipeline/dependency, persistent data, source read, model, root or UI input behavior is changed.

The initial affected search run passes 169 tests (`/tmp/clip-lexical-sql-prune-search-tests-20261021.log`); the optimized run including a new regression passes **170, zero failures, three ignored** (`/tmp/clip-lexical-sql-prune-release-tests-20261021.log`). The regression checks natural numeric-name ties, rejection of worse candidates, retained-path explanation admission, a changing boundary after eviction, Top restoration and callback unregistration on a SQL argument error. Existing hard-filter-before-cap and exclusion tests continue to pass.

Frozen production quality passes (`/tmp/clip-lexical-sql-prune-quality-20261021.json`). Every full case record in baseline/default/candidate profiles, and the entire matched f32 reference, is identical to `/tmp/clip-matched-storage-precision-release-final-20261021.json`. Thus returned files, scores, ranks and explanation locations stay unchanged across those cases, not merely aggregate recall. Defaults retain 72/72 positives and 2/32 negatives with any result, exact-name and f16/f32 gates passing.

Optimized scale evidence (`/tmp/clip-lexical-sql-prune-150k-20261021.json`, unchanged late-winner fixture, 100,000 real owned files / 150,000 synthetic vectors / 21 warm samples) gives p95 **45.086 ms exact name, 39.951 ms rare keyword, 259.102 ms common keyword, 405.302 ms default hybrid, 449.526 ms broad hybrid**. Common-keyword p95 falls from the preceding 389.453 ms (~33%) but still misses 100 ms; both hybrid component cases stay within 500 ms. This measured improvement justifies retaining the new scoped mechanism; it is not end-to-end UI or cold latency proof. Diagnostic full-SQL scans intentionally use an empty, never-updated Top so they still count the full admitted scope; EXPLAIN and diagnostics scope/remove their dummy callback before production search.

Architecture comparison retains 1,410 clone groups, max complexity 195 and the same historical warnings, with no new reported excessive-complexity function or clone group from this change. Final verification passes: 2,712 workspace tests, zero failures, 26 ignored across 26 suites (`/tmp/clip-lexical-sql-prune-workspace-20261021.log`); rebuilt desktop binaries (`/tmp/clip-lexical-sql-prune-debug-build-20261021.log`); formatting and whitespace checks. Rebuilt native quality acceptance passes in both dark and light themes (`/tmp/clip-lexical-sql-prune-native-dark-20261021a/report.json` and `/tmp/clip-lexical-sql-prune-native-light-20261021a/report.json`), including completed image/audio/late-video searches, keyword recovery, negative omission and filename priority. No staging or commits have been added.

A current-state audit also found an unresolved input-policy conflict: the Phase 6 section still documents Ctrl+F as search entry, while the semantic plan explicitly says all search entry should use colon commands and prohibits Ctrl+F. The implementation and older acceptance helpers currently retain the Ctrl+F alias. This required an explicit reconciliation before the final completion claim; it was subsequently resolved in the 2026-10-25 input-policy decision below.

### Native scale-fixture validation — 2026-10-25

Added an explicitly ignored test-only export mode to the existing `retrieval::benchmark::Corpus` and `scripts/file_manager_scale_acceptance.py`. The exporter reuses the canonical schema, actual ordinary-file metadata and source-session enrollment, then labels synthetic vectors with the installed pipeline. It does not add a production CLI or weaken admission. ### Restricted front-matter extraction — 2026-10-26

The user's structured-document example now has a first declarative fast path. The same Qwen3.5-9B Q8_0 answer role maps a column question to a front-matter field; there is no model/precision/server change and no third model. The neural planner receives field paths and scalar formats only, not field values or the document body. A finite path grammar and strict JSON protocol cannot name a shell, executable or arbitrary source pathname. The initial operation is `frontmatter_field`; the broader bounded text/regex/window/table operations remain planned, not implemented by this checkpoint.

The ordinary source reader supplies a verified, bounded 8 KiB prefix. A dedicated native interpreter accepts complete YAML/TOML headers within 4 KiB, at most 128 bounded scalar/unsupported-field descriptors and eight path components. Existing date validation classifies date-format strings, and those formats join paths/kinds in the schema hash. Each ephemeral column caches up to eight layout-specific plans/refusals; it stores paths rather than values. Every execution reparses the current header, applies the existing answer type parser, retains literal header evidence/location, and shares the existing final source-identity verification. Source replacement, mount/session changes, pipeline changes, retry, cancellation and column lifetime keep their existing guards. A cancelled inference checks its flag under the invalidation lock before inserting a plan.

Unsupported/null/array/floating fields retain their labels rather than disappearing and making another value look like the best match. Their placeholder is never accepted as an empty text answer; a genuinely empty quoted string remains a valid text value. Excessive key/path/field budgets decline the entire layout rather than hiding possible competitors. Duplicate YAML/TOML keys reject the structured path. Unquoted floats do not pass through binary-float rounding into exact numeric answers. Unsupported captures/layouts, interpretation and worker failures use ordinary guarded answering. A plan refusal or missing field does not establish global absence/non-applicability. Header evidence explicitly discloses that the body was not examined.

The interpreter installs an OS boundary before parsing requests: piped standard descriptors, closed ambient descriptors, resource limits, no-new-privileges and a native-architecture default-deny seccomp filter synchronized across threads. Opening files, filesystem writes, networking, descriptor duplication, process creation and execution are denied. Standard input is also required to be a pipe, closing a potential writable-file `mmap` escape. Only transport writes and bounded computation/memory syscalls remain. An installation failure exits without processing the request; there is no unisolated tool fallback. This is a deliberately byte-only interpreter, not a shell-command allowlist or a claim that seccomp alone sandboxes an arbitrary agent.

Retained planner failures drove two corrections: an early synthetic check declined a direct boolean flag (9/10), and an eight-post real check declined the stored date on a draft (7/8). Schema-only planning prevents a first file's draft/value content from influencing a reusable mapping; scalar formats and explicit direct-field semantics resolve the tested ambiguity. Intermediate schema-only probes and their failures remain under `/tmp/clip-frontmatter-planner-20261026{c,d,e,f}`. The current real-model test passes **13/13**, including explicit publication/modification labels, draft publication metadata, boolean inversion, nested fields, incompatible and unsupported preferred fields, ambiguity and untrusted input (`/tmp/clip-frontmatter-planner-20261026h/report.json`). This is narrow evidence, not general semantic correctness.

`frontmatter_probe.py` reuses the actual model Worker and guarded reader for reproducible checks of an explicitly chosen directory without enrollment or an index. The real posts directory contains 31 immediate Markdown files and seven header layouts (`/tmp/clip-frontmatter-post-schema-20261026.json`). The full component check passes **31/31**: 30 exact stored dates and `_index.md`'s missing-date abstention, using seven planning calls (`/tmp/clip-frontmatter-real-posts-20261026d/report.json`). The earlier attempt requested 31 date-bearing fixtures and failed because `_index.md` has no date; the revised probe explicitly checks abstention for a missing expected field. These checks read bounded headers and verify the source after answering; they do not modify posts or configure an indexed root.

The private native Files check passes YAML/TOML dates, same-layout rule reuse, typed date sorting, generic fallback, folder return and no source writes/implicit index (`/tmp/clip-frontmatter-native-20261026b/report.json`). The observed reused-rule worker time is **34.4 ms**, first answer including model startup **2.313 s**, and another new layout **256.5 ms**. These are individual diagnostic observations under other validation load, excluding presentation, not a latency distribution or controlled speed comparison. A prior native run observed 87.8 ms for reuse; both reports remain. Existing numeric/boolean columns and lifecycle still pass (`/tmp/clip-structured-fallback-native-20261026a/report.json`).

### Semantic candidate review and escalation — 2026-10-26

The user's follow-up correctly distinguishes a reusable literal extraction from a question that requires a fresh judgment. A layout/type match alone is insufficient. Added a bounded semantic-review policy to the existing ephemeral column plans, using the same Qwen3.5-9B Q8_0 role, with no new model, precision change, shell permission or indexing default. The preceding scale-performance work established `phase6-retrieval-performance-20261026` and rebuilt a release harness, but did not change retrieval/scoring before this correctness steering. Its latency gates remain outstanding. `phase6-answer-review-20261026` separately records the review feature's starting point.

A new proposal is not accepted immediately. The answering model first answers from the bounded actual header **without receiving the proposed value or field path**. Rust applies the existing selected-evidence sufficiency, quotation and type checks and compares the result with the tool capture, retaining units and date precision. Literal text agreement is exact rather than borrowing the looser case/numeric equivalence used for sorting. A mismatch, missing evidence or unsupported/global answer escalates. Agreement permits a second, scoped review of the actual capture/question/header. The review commits `literal`, `per_file` or `document` scope before its decision. Only literal retrieval may authorize reuse; a per-file question may accept only this row or escalate, while document scope must escalate. Once a plan needs per-file review it cannot upgrade itself on a later convenient example. Each subsequent capture still revalidates structure/type/evidence and the source after answering.

Per-file plausibility or interpretation cannot be inferred from one reasonable first value. Actual-child-age questions retain per-file assessment, whereas an explicit question about the stored age field can return the stored number even if it is not a plausible actual age. Tone/reliability judgments evaluate the content rather than echo a classification tag. Stored date/draft/configuration metadata does not prove an actual external event. The generic answering prompt now records these distinctions as `generic-qa-v6`; answer pipeline hashing invalidates old session results. The embedding model/pipeline and canonical vectors do not change.

An escalation invokes the existing guarded full-document path once, with a bounded prior-review reason outside source evidence. That reason is explicitly unverified analysis, not an instruction/fact, and cannot supply a quotation. Source/coverage/type checks and existing inference limits remain mandatory. No-match/refusal does not establish non-applicability or global absence. Both review passes are the same model and can still share errors; this is not an independent truth oracle or a substitute for the broader quality gate. General regex/window/table extraction operations remain planned separately.

Retained tests exposed real weaknesses before acceptance. The first critic-only check passed 11/12 but rationalized reusing a positive tag for a tone judgment (`/tmp/clip-candidate-review-20261026a/report.json`). A stronger prompt still stalled on several decisions after a forced reason-length boundary. The raw synthetic diagnostic (`/tmp/clip-review-debug-20261026a/raw.jsonl`) shows whitespace generation following a truncated string; review grammar now bounds whitespace as well as its short reason. Classifying scope before decision prevents interpretation-to-reuse rationalization in the tested cases, but a critic-only repeat still accepted a wrong negated boolean and declined a valid draft-date capture (`...review-20261026c/report.json`, 12/14). The unanchored answer-and-compare stage resolves that demonstrated anchoring error rather than trusting a critic's explanation. Intermediate failures are retained, not removed from the evaluation.

The composed real-model check passes **16/16**, requiring actual decisions without output-budget exhaustion (`/tmp/clip-candidate-review-20261026f/report.json`). It covers literal metadata, wrong labels, negation, actual versus stored status, a sensible first age, implausible ages, an explicitly stored negative value, per-file interpretation, contradictory tags/content, source instructions and prohibition on upgrading per-file review. All **27/27** existing generic Q/A fixtures also pass on explicit RTX 3090 CUDA (`/tmp/clip-review-generic-answer-20261026c.json`), including false/zero, missing versus unrelated, ambiguity, interpretation, numeric/date precision, negation and incomplete coverage. An earlier harness invocation could not write its report into a missing parent; another used the helper's default Vulkan concurrently with a second test model and exhausted device memory. Validation was rerun serially with explicit CUDA. No user GPU process was stopped and the product's one-role model ownership was unchanged.

The private native test passes differing tone answers for identical header layouts, approved literal-flag reuse, a reasonable first age followed by a corrupt age, and full-document escalation to the corrected **6 years** value (`/tmp/clip-answer-review-native-20261026c/report.json`, `review-checks.json`). The corpus and config stay private; source hashes are unchanged and no index is created. The revised equivalent question removes that unavailable named-entity dependency and exercises the captured-age review. The next attempt's final OCR assertion found a preview line count rather than the answer column; a verified column crop now checks both answer cells, with original screenshots retained. The existing YAML/TOML/date sorting/folder-return/no-index UI check also passes after semantic review (`/tmp/clip-reviewed-frontmatter-native-20261026a/report.json`).

### Portable semantic scoring and current scale measurements — 2026-10-26

Resumed the outstanding retrieval-performance gate with an unchanged current-tree baseline, `phase6-retrieval-scan-20261026`. The optimized component run at 100,000 ordinary fixture files/150,000 synthetic f16 vectors passes correctness but misses the common-keyword target: p95 exact filename **40.178 ms**, rare keyword **42.306 ms**, common keyword **264.028 ms**, default-floor hybrid **390.323 ms**, broad-floor hybrid **442.085 ms** (`/tmp/clip-retrieval-baseline-20261026.json`). These component observations exclude inference, final source verification, explanation assembly and UI acceptance; they do not establish end-to-end acceptance.

Changed the existing normalized-vector dot product from one serial 768-add chain to eight independent accumulation lanes. This is portable safe Rust, permits baseline-ISA compiler vectorization without unsafe dispatch, new dependencies or fast-math, and leaves canonical f16 storage, query admission, floors, ranking rules, cancellation and memory limits intact. Invalid query dimensions now fail explicitly rather than silently shortening a dot product. The reduction order changes floating-point rounding, so equivalence is numerical rather than bit-for-bit. A seeded dense/parallel/opposed/cancelling-vector regression test compares 2,048 dot products with double-precision references to an absolute error below 1e-6 and checks invalid dimensions/nonfinite inputs. Cached and streamed vectors retain the same scorer.

All **188 search tests pass, zero failures, four ignored** in the optimized harness (`/tmp/phase6-lane-search-tests-20261026.log`). The matched frozen real-model outputs retain every top-ten file order and expected-file rank under all three ranking profiles and the f32 oracle; recall loss is zero and the largest returned-segment score difference from the previous implementation is **1.133e-6** (`/tmp/clip-retrieval-lanes-quality-comparison-20261026.json`). Production defaults still retrieve **72/72** positives, with **2/32** negative queries producing results and all exact-name tiers passing; the deliberately unthresholded diagnostic profile still fails the negative gate. This does not substitute for the remaining representative-corpus quality work.

The new 21-sample component run reports p95 exact filename **38.995 ms**, rare keyword **36.803 ms**, common keyword **251.831 ms**, default-floor hybrid **332.885 ms**, broad-floor hybrid **376.916 ms** (`/tmp/clip-retrieval-lanes-20261026.json`). Independent read-only SQL diagnostics show substantial posting/join/admission scan cost; simply limiting an unfiltered FTS posting list would violate the hard-filter and tie-order requirements and was not adopted. Sampling profiler recording was unavailable under this host's existing perf permissions; no host settings were changed.

Current end-to-end scale results and final validation will be recorded separately; this checkpoint does not close Phase 6. Changes remain unstaged and uncommitted.

The actual application now closes after eight completed passages, resumes those eight and finishes 392 with unchanged canonical prefix bytes and full source coverage. A changed-source restart rejects the old cursor. Final broad validation is recorded with that checkpoint; this does not close the other audit items.
- **Settings mismatch needing a decision:** `FileSearchConfig` has global hidden/exclusion/index-quota preferences and path-specific exclusions, not independent per-root hidden/budget overrides. Only index MiB/vector quotas are adjustable; per-file extraction/segment limits remain fixed bounded policy constants. The plan currently describes broader configurability. Reconcile that deliberately rather than claiming the absent settings are present.
- **Performance/quality proof:** retained 150,000-vector native p95 is above target, common-term lexical latency remains above 100 ms, and the large-index CPU warm target still needs matched native evidence. The above-cache scope/memory proof now passes, but cancellation/progress needs its final operational exercise. Q8/BF16 and canonical-f16/f32 tests on frozen generated fixtures pass; the representative-corpus precision/relevance gate still needs stronger evidence. Do not substitute vector cosine agreement or synthetic-only success for it.
- **Final release audit:** the complete current functional/delivery requirements, packaged helpers, freshness/reconciliation/recovery and window/session behavior still require a consolidated source-and-evidence audit. Earlier passing checks are useful evidence, not a declaration that every current requirement is proved. All changes remain unstaged, the original stash is retained, and no commit was made.


### Durable partial indexing checkpoints — 2026-10-26

The source audit found a real implementation gap rather than just another pending benchmark: unfinished files kept all generated segments/cursors in `FileWork` until completion. Added an optional source-bound checkpoint table and unified complete/partial publication in `store/publication.rs`. Every eight newly completed segments, the worker repeats guarded source verification and transactionally appends only the new tail plus extraction position/text position. The acknowledged prefix length is checked before append, preventing stale retries from duplicating batches. Vector counts change only after commit. A failed write rolls back segments, cursor and status together. No source file, xattr, model precision, enrollment default or decoder permission changes.

Partially published segments are eligible only through the existing pipeline/source-session/membership/visibility admission and final source verification. Coverage explicitly says indexing is in progress and distinguishes planned coverage; an outstanding checkpoint prevents a folder from being marked ready even though its accepted partial segments are searchable. Successful completion clears the checkpoint atomically. Reopening observes that work is incomplete and schedules it again, rather than accepting its partial `ready` record as a completed file.

Resume requires the same scoped file ID/revision, pipeline, extraction version, dispatch suffix and fresh full digest when the fingerprint policy supplies one. Large files retain the existing metadata/verified-source best-effort contract; this does not add a separate full read to hash a large source. A text cursor also records the prepared-text hash: extraction output is regenerated and checked, with a valid UTF-8 boundary, before applying that position. Changed output or incompatible format/digest discards the hint, hides the old partial rows and restarts extraction with canonical-input reuse. Source/pipeline observations that change retire incompatible cursors. When mount/source continuity cannot be established, old scoped observations cannot authorize cursor reuse.

Finalization matches the committed input/location prefix and retains its original segment IDs and canonical float16 bytes. It writes only the remaining tail instead of replacing the committed prefix with re-quantized values or rewriting all prior segment rows after every batch. All supplied vectors still pass the existing encoder validation before any write; temporary encoded prefix values are discarded rather than installed. Checkpoint metadata is bounded to 4 KiB, one row per file, with foreign-key cleanup; vectors/text remain in the canonical existing segment table. The schema-v5 record format is retained with an optional derived table, like the existing source-digest cache. Restore's trusted staging schema creates an empty checkpoint table but never imports supplied cursors; restored canonical inputs remain available for fresh verified-input reuse.

Four new transactional tests pass with the 188 existing search tests (**192 passed, zero failures, four ignored**, `/tmp/phase6-checkpoint-store-tests-20261026.log`). They cover reopening, incomplete root state, append-prefix rejection, exact half-byte/ID preservation at completion, changed digest/dispatch/revision/pipeline/extractor, and an injected failure after segment writes that must roll back the entire cursor/batch. The existing shared source-session fixture supplies actual scoped files/reopening, rather than duplicating its setup. Publication validation, segment insertion and status/cursor writes were separated by responsibility after the architecture measurement identified excessive complexity; repeated partial-source test setup now shares a fixture.

Private native Files shutdown/reopen passes (`/tmp/clip-checkpoint-native-20261026a/report.json`, `checkpoint-checks.json`): the actual model generates **eight committed passages**, the application closes normally, startup resumes at **byte 34,089**, and completes **392** passages. The original eight IDs/input hashes/float16 vectors remain byte-identical. No passage location is duplicated, the source hash stays unchanged, the checkpoint disappears only on completion, and a query finds the final sentinel passage. An additional direct verification checks every stored offset and canonical model-input hash against the owned ASCII/LF source: **all 1,663,052 bytes are covered with no gaps** (`coverage-checks.json`). This native test proves the long-text path, not native restart coverage of every media decoder.

The complementary light private run changes only its owned synthetic source while Files is closed (`/tmp/clip-checkpoint-changed-native-20261026a/report.json`, `closed-checkpoint.json`, `checkpoint-checks.json`). The previous eight-passage cursor is not resumed; the replacement has a new revision, one fresh passage covering all **70 bytes**, no checkpoint after completion and no result for the old sentinel query. Both runs use private config/data and the private Weston/Niri input sockets. User files/config and the real catalog are untouched; no implicit root enrollment is introduced.

The shared acceptance runner now checks the session's current process after an exercise that restarts Files, rather than only checking the already-exited first instance. Whole-workspace validation, final lint and final architecture results after the small SQL-initialization/coverage-label cleanup are pending below. Audio/video-specific cursor restart checks, the broader Phase 6 scope audit and the previously listed performance/representative-quality gaps remain open. Nothing is staged or committed, and the original stash remains retained.


Final checkpoint validation passes **2,911 workspace tests, zero failures, 38 ignored, 26 suites** (`/tmp/phase6-checkpoint-final-workspace-20261026.log`). Two additional cases verify that a cursor without a full digest cannot cross an uncertain source scope, and that a lexical-only prefix can receive vectors on finalization. ### Shared catalog settings decision — 2026-10-27

Resolved the settings mismatch called out in the 2026-10-26 audit deliberately. Keep one global disk/vector quota and hidden-indexing preference under `[file_manager.search]`, plus explicit root enrollment/removal and exact per-file/subtree exclusions. Root memberships share canonical file segments/vectors; assigning independent physical-storage quotas would require an arbitrary owner or double-charge overlapping roots, and introduce eviction/admission ambiguity on moves or removal. One shared quota describes the actual catalog storage the user is limiting. A shared hidden policy also keeps eligibility predictable across overlap and does not inherit the browser's Show hidden control. Users can exclude individual hidden subtrees; an explicitly enrolled hidden ordinary root retains the existing root-relative policy. The cost is that two roots cannot independently choose hidden-indexing modes or separate storage allowances. This is now the documented design rather than a claim those missing controls exist.

Keep per-file decoder byte/page/duration/segment caps as bounded reader policy, while exposing catalog MiB/vector quotas as configurable settings. Raising arbitrary decoder limits would undermine the current memory/time admission bounds; unsupported/partial/name-only coverage continues to report why work stopped. Future lower per-root work budgets can be considered with explicit overlapping-membership accounting, but are not represented as implemented. Mandatory credential, protected-filesystem and generated-directory exclusions, especially `.git`, remain non-overridable as the user's objective requires. This corrects the older plan sentence allowing per-root generated-directory overrides.

Evidence is the current `FileSearchConfig`/`FileSearchLimits`, settings validation/persistence/UI, root/membership policy, and the existing hidden/exclusion/quota/overlap search tests included in the current 208-test passing search run. This decision changes the plan to fit the shared-catalog model; no safety or resource guard was weakened and no implicit root was enabled.


The rebuilt release application passes the private 100,000-file/150,000-vector scale scenario with 21 warm samples per case (`/tmp/clip-fts-native-150k-20261027a/report.json`, `scale-latency.json`). Measured native p95: exact-name description **579.81 ms**, rare keyword **562.40 ms**, common keyword **757.38 ms**, broad query **585.33 ms**. Every case uses actual model/coordinator/source-verification/explanation/UI acceptance with synthetic vectors and an intentionally permissive -1 floor; exact/rare cases are hybrid scans, not pure lexical timing. Source memberships complete normal startup reconciliation and the stored-vector fingerprint remains unchanged. The 500 ms native warm-hybrid target is still missed. The older native observations are not a controlled paired before/after experiment, so changes in their absolute timings are not claimed wholly attributable to this SQL edit.

The current `--no-deps` Clippy invocation succeeds (`/tmp/phase6-fts-clippy-20261027.jsonl`); two needless-borrow warnings touch unchanged older retrieval tests, with no new production-query or regression-test diagnostics. The dependency-inclusive CMYK lint limitation recorded earlier remains separate. The most recent full workspace run (2,924 passing tests) predates this SQL/test-only increment; current optimized search validation is 208 passing/four ignored, including the new result-window regression. No further broad workspace pass is implied here. All work remains unstaged/uncommitted and the original stash is retained. Remaining goal work includes general regex/window/richer-table tools, the unresolved large-index/representative-quality gates, keyboard investigation and the final full requirement audit; shared-settings reconciliation is now explicitly decided above.


### Bounded model-directed text searches — 2026-10-27

Added literal/regex evidence selection for larger complete text documents, before the existing all-window Q/A path. Question-only plans are cached in the answer worker (32 question/type/label entries), with at most three model-proposed searches plus one literal field label from the existing shared question parser. No file content, applicability result or answer is stored in that cache. Each file executes the searches independently, produces its own exact offset windows, obtains a typed evidence-backed answer, and receives a separate sufficiency review before acceptance. Small complete sources and the earlier fast field templates keep their existing paths. The generator/reviewer use the same current Q8 model; no third model or external inference server is required.

Execution uses the existing isolated extraction child, not a shell/command allowlist. Sources have strict data-only fields, with no descriptor/path/executable opening instruction. Search input stays within 512 sources/512 KiB text; patterns are at most 512 bytes, Rust non-backtracking regex programs/DFA caches are bounded to 256 KiB each, nesting is bounded, and empty-match searches are rejected. Default case-insensitive matching is specific to these internal evidence searches; the user-facing basename `:regex:` syntax remains unchanged. At most eight exact windows/4 KiB each/16 KiB combined are returned as source IDs and UTF-8 offsets. The parent slices its original source to construct evidence, rejecting fabricated/out-of-range/non-boundary offsets. Saturation is explicit. The transport cap becomes 4 MiB to accommodate worst-case JSON escaping of the unchanged decoded-text budget; the child retains its 512 MiB memory, two-second CPU and three-second wall limits, closed ambient descriptors, no-new-privileges and default-deny seccomp boundary. No direct file access, networking, process execution, mutation, index enrollment or persistent answer storage was added.

A positive search match does not establish that it answers the question. Selected-window answering must report a directly supported value, then a separate same-model critic may accept or escalate. Restricted-subject questions decline the partial shortcut and retain the existing document workflow and its applicability/coverage limits. Invalid/empty/limited searches and non-value or uncertain results fall back, with a shared 180-second deadline rather than adding another unbounded job lifetime. A review's short escalation reason is unverified feedback outside source evidence. Omitted content cannot prove absence/non-applicability or exhaustive totals. The code does not universally replace interpretation with a regex, cache file verdicts, or claim the critic is an independent truth oracle.

The initial private dark run passed late code extraction but failed an expected-ambiguity assertion (`/tmp/clip-text-tools-native-20261027a/report.json`). A retained direct probe (`/tmp/clip-text-search-debug-20261027a/report.json`) shows both full/selected answering and review treated `Redemption code` and `Coupon code` as distinct named fields when the fixture merely called both current. The revised fixture explicitly states that they alias the same conflicting value and also includes a literal repeated-label case; both require ambiguity. The dark native rerun passes those cases and exercises actual critic escalation (`/tmp/clip-text-tools-native-20261027b/report.json`). Its large Unicode/JSON input also proves the real isolated child accepts the expanded transport and returns exact original offsets without reopening a file.

Further retained checks exposed layout/case assumptions in model plans and excessive conservatism on explicitly stored negative values: the first composed model run passes 11/15 (`/tmp/clip-text-search-smoke-20261027a/report.json`). Default case-insensitive search fixes missing mixed-case labels. Revised instructions and an application-owned literal-label alternative avoid relying on numeric-assignment-shaped regexes to find a table header. The second run passes 15/16 (`...27b/report.json`), with the remaining miss being that label coverage. The seeded final check passes **16/16** (`...27c/report.json`), including actual isolated execution of proposed searches, wrong candidates, explicit alias/repeated conflicts, whole-file questions, implausible actual ages versus stored negatives, a scoped zero-valued table row, and source instructions. An unscoped quantity question correctly requests broader context instead of assuming one selected row is the whole table. The light intermediate UI run returned the right 0/42-unit values through full answering but failed its overstrong fast-path assertion (`/tmp/clip-text-tools-native-20261027c/report.json`); the assertion now requires that conservative fallback. These retained failures are not represented as universal extraction success.

Current Python contracts pass **70 tests**. The first full workspace run passes **2,927 tests, zero failures, 38 ignored, 26 suites** (`/tmp/phase6-text-tools-workspace-20261027.log`); a second run also passes before the last literal-label parser extraction/test. The focused answer suite then passes 46 cases. Final rebuilt UI/generic-model/workspace/lint results after that last shared-parser change are recorded below when available. Architecture checking identified and eliminated new excessive-complexity functions by separating source-budget validation from scanning, review payload validation from inference, and replacing the expanding model-operation branch chain with a strict dispatch table using existing functions. The added standalone native test repeats only existing source-audit/entry-point boilerplate; actual observation/hash/runner/completion/model helpers are reused. Final aggregate measurements follow separately.


The final light native attempt exposed a further real limitation of same-model criticism: with the application literal-label seed present, a nearby unscoped table quantity was approved despite the isolated reviewer declining the shorter example (`/tmp/clip-text-tools-final-native-20261027a/report.json`). No universal critic-reliability claim is made from the earlier 16/16 run. A deterministic guard now records whether the actual matched source line is pipe/tab-separated. A plain text-search plan has no validated table row/column selection, so such a match cannot authorize the fast answer and goes to complete table/document answering. The guard is based on the matched line, not an unrelated table elsewhere in its context; a dedicated regression proves that an adjacent table does not block an ordinary code-field capture. This is a conservative structural guard, not a general CSV/table parser or an added model permission. Future accelerated table-cell selection requires its own bounded row/column plan; current generic table questions remain supported through the full workflow.

The rebuilt light native application passes (`/tmp/clip-text-tools-table-guard-native-20261027a/report.json`, `text-search-checks.json`): two distinct late code values through reviewed searches, exact UTF-8/page-location evidence, correct 0/42-unit table values through full answering, two ambiguous conflicting-code cases without a confident shortcut, actual critic escalation, unchanged source bytes and metadata, and no index creation. The current search suite passes **212 tests, zero failures, four ignored** (`/tmp/phase6-text-tools-search-tests-20261027.log`). The most recent focused answer suite before the last validator-only refactor passes **48 tests**; all **70 Python protocol contracts** pass after that refactor. The ordinary generic Q/A regression also passes **29/29 on CUDA** (`/tmp/phase6-text-tools-answer-regression-20261027.json`), covering the unchanged full-source path under the expanded strict operation dispatcher. An earlier attempt passed the wrong report CLI option and did not run that regression; its argument error is retained, not counted as a pass.

Final architecture comparison against `phase6-bounded-text-tools-20261027` has no newly excessive-complexity function/entity after splitting bounded review and plan decoding from orchestration. Maximum CCN stays 195, cycles stay 15, and two additional clone groups (1,469 → 1,471) are the new native driver's source-observation setup and ordinary standalone-entry/report boundary shared with the previous driver. Existing runner, source observations, streaming hashes, completion polling and model test/report helpers are reused; no second runner or parser was introduced merely to hide that boilerplate. The aggregate regression classification remains disclosed. Final current-tree workspace/model/lint results follow below.


Final text-tool validation passes **2,929 workspace tests, zero failures, 38 ignored, 26 suites** (`/tmp/phase6-text-tools-table-guard-workspace-20261027.log`). The final same-model plan/execution/critic check passes **16/16** (`/tmp/clip-text-search-final-smoke-20261027a/report.json`), and all **70 Python protocol tests** pass. After two lint-only Rust cleanups, the affected answer suite passes **48 tests** again (`/tmp/phase6-text-tools-lint-cleanup-unit-20261027.log`). Current targeted `--no-deps` Clippy succeeds with no diagnostics in the changed tool/context/runtime implementation (`/tmp/phase6-text-tools-clean-clippy-20261027.jsonl`); pre-existing broader warnings and the previously disclosed dependency-inclusive CMYK lint error remain separate. This completes the bounded literal/regex/window tool mechanism at this checkpoint, not the whole Phase 6 objective. ### Indexed semantic scan admission order — 2026-10-27

Global `scope:indexed` semantic scans now visit each ready file/path membership before its segments, using SQLite's existing covering indexes and an explicit file → path → segment join order. This avoids repeating the same source-session/root-membership admission for each segment. Here/recursive scopes retain the planner's directory/path-range choices; forcing the global strategy on a small folder would waste work scanning unrelated files. Eligibility, vector scoring, floors, rank fusion and result limits are unchanged. Published `unsupported` records contain no segments, so semantic scans skip those records before membership admission; lexical filename searches still admit them. Ready records with lexical-only/NULL-vector segments retain their existing handling.

Read-only SQL diagnostics are retained in `/tmp/phase6-semantic-join-diagnostic-20261027.json`, `/tmp/phase6-semantic-path-first-diagnostic-20261027.json` and `/tmp/phase6-semantic-name-only-diagnostic-20261027.json`. The last uses an owned in-memory SQLite backup with 90% name-only records and demonstrates why a ready-state guard is necessary: naive membership-first admission regresses that case. These SQL-only timings exclude real scoring/model/UI work and are not product acceptance measurements.

A fresh paired optimized component measurement uses 100,000 fixture files, 150,000 synthetic f16 vectors and 21 warm samples per case (`/tmp/clip-semantic-before-150k-20261027.json`, `/tmp/clip-semantic-after-150k-20261027.json`). P95 default-floor hybrid improves **335.49 → 304.98 ms**, and broad-floor hybrid **381.15 → 344.88 ms**, approximately 9%. Exact/rare/common lexical measurements are **44.50 / 37.73 / 210.20 ms** after the change; their code is unchanged and timing variation is not attributed to this optimization. The common lexical 100 ms target remains missed. These are retrieval-component measurements, not native end-to-end results.

All **212 search tests pass**, with four ignored (`/tmp/phase6-semantic-search-tests-20261027.log`). The strengthened semantic-progress test exercises both Here and Indexed scopes, shared hard-link vectors, excluded hidden/stale aliases, a name-only record, final partial-batch progress and cancellation. The frozen quality run passes its declared targets (`/tmp/clip-semantic-after-quality-20261027.json`); complete returned payloads equal the before report. Each quality query additionally asserts identical ordered paths, segment IDs, keyword flags and score bits between Recursive and Indexed over the equivalent single-root fixture. See `/tmp/clip-semantic-quality-comparison-20261027.json`. This preserves controlled-fixture quality; it does not replace the outstanding representative-corpus evaluation.

The above-cache component run (`/tmp/clip-semantic-after-above-cache-20261027.json`) covers 125,000 files / 187,500 vectors, retains 167,751 cached vectors within 522,736,640 reserved bytes, reports all admitted references and finds the final uncached high-scoring item. Its three samples are a correctness/resource check, not a p95 gate or native cancellation proof.

The optimized application build and targeted `cargo clippy -p clip-workbench --lib --tests --no-deps` succeed (`/tmp/phase6-semantic-release-app-20261027.log`, `/tmp/phase6-semantic-clippy-20261027.stderr`). Architecture comparison against `phase6-semantic-join-order-20261027` keeps 1,471 clone groups, 15 cycles and maximum CCN 195, with 53 additional Rust lines including expanded scope/alias assertions. The aggregate tool still reports REGRESSION from pre-existing clone blocks touching modified files; no additional clone group is introduced by this step. Native timing validation follows separately; the full Phase 6 goal remains open.

The optimized native application subsequently passes fixture/UI correctness and unchanged-vector checks (`/tmp/clip-semantic-native-150k-20261027a/report.json`, `scale-latency.json`). All cases use actual inference, coordinator scheduling, final source verification, explanations and UI acceptance/sorting over synthetic vectors with a permissive -1 relevance floor; these are hybrid cases, not pure lexical queries. With 100,000 files / 150,000 vectors and 21 warm samples per case, median/p95 milliseconds are exact-name hybrid **487.57 / 512.96**, rare-keyword hybrid **479.34 / 505.80**, common-keyword hybrid **666.36 / 706.65**, and broad hybrid **479.77 / 518.58**. GPU frame presentation is excluded. The native 500 ms p95 target is still missed, even where the median is below it. No target was lowered, and the older native run is not claimed as a controlled paired experiment.

### Active above-cache cancellation — 2026-10-27

The existing optimized retrieval benchmark now verifies cancellation during an active above-cache scan, outside its timing samples. For each semantic floor, a separate observer requests cancellation only after the request's own progress counter exceeds the entire cached vector population by at least 2,048 references. This corpus has exactly one path/reference per vector, so that threshold proves uncached vectors have been visited without assuming cache IDs or SQL order. The test requires cancellation before the full scan completes, unchanged cache population/reservation, and a subsequent fresh full scan recovering the same ordered result paths and final progress count. The observer has a bounded lifetime and receives only shared atomic progress/cancellation state; it never shares SQLite across threads.

The 125,000-file / 187,500-vector run passes for both default and broad floors (`/tmp/clip-stream-cancel-above-cache-20261027.json`). Cache population is 167,751 vectors with 522,736,640 reserved bytes; both requests cancel at the reported 169,984 references, below 187,500, with `Search cancelled`. Both fresh scans recover their original ordered paths. Recorded 344.79/393.81 ms values are elapsed scan-to-cancellation times, **not** cancellation-response latency. The three warm timing samples remain a correctness/resource check, not a p95 acceptance gate. This closes the active component cancellation proof; private UI above-cap progress/cancellation still needs its operational exercise.

Architecture comparison against `phase6-stream-cancel-20261027` adds one test-only helper and 59 Rust lines, retaining 1,471 clone groups, 15 cycles and maximum CCN 195. The initial placement of workload selection in `measure` pushed that existing benchmark method from CCN 14 to 16; moving applicability into the cancellation helper restores its prior complexity and avoids that new hotspot. Existing modified-file clone warnings keep the aggregate REGRESSION classification, without a new clone group. No production retrieval behavior changed in this test step.

Final current-tree validation passes **2,929 workspace tests, zero failures, 38 ignored, 26 suites**, using `TESSDATA_PREFIX=/home/vega/.local/share/tessdata cargo test --workspace -- --test-threads=1` (`/tmp/phase6-stream-cancel-workspace-20261027.log`). Serial execution is deliberate because of the previously documented shared-admission conflicts at default concurrency; no default-concurrency pass is claimed. The optimized harness build, formatting, whitespace checks and targeted `cargo clippy -p clip-workbench --lib --tests --no-deps` also pass (`/tmp/phase6-stream-cancel-final-build-20261027.log`, `/tmp/phase6-stream-cancel-clippy-20261027.stderr`), with no Clippy diagnostics in the changed benchmark. The native timing evidence above predates only this test-only helper; production code is unchanged afterward. Changes remain unstaged and uncommitted. The full Phase 6 objective remains active.

### Native above-cache progress and cancellation — 2026-10-27

The existing private scale driver now has an explicit `--exercise-cancellation` mode. It uses the same real source metadata, normal startup reconciliation, real model and production retrieval/UI, with synthetic stored vectors. Two consecutive searches must display a nonzero count before completion; the driver captures that frame, presses Escape before OCR, verifies restoration of the original empty browser, then requires a fresh query to finish without accepting an obsolete request. Screenshots preserve the private clipboard and remove reused destinations before capture. The final stored-vector fingerprint must remain unchanged.

The first attempt (`/tmp/clip-native-stream-cancel-20261027a`) deliberately retains a failure: 200,000 entries in one folder exceed the existing 100,000-entry bounded inventory, so ordinary reconciliation correctly reports incomplete enumeration and retains inventory. No production limit or eligibility check was relaxed. The fixture exporter now optionally creates additional synthetic segments per file. Normal scale runs retain 1.5 vectors/file; cancellation runs use 3.5 vectors/file and the native driver rejects oversized flat directories before fixture generation. The component benchmark retains its original larger-file workload independently of native enumeration.

The corrected release/light private run passes with **100,000 files / 350,000 vectors** (`/tmp/clip-native-stream-cancel-20261027b/report.json`, `stream-cancellation.json`). Captured counts are **14,336** and **32,768**; requests 10 and 14 are never accepted after cancellation, while fresh requests 12 and 16 complete. Source memberships finish normal startup reconciliation, vector/input-hash/pipeline fingerprints remain unchanged, and the owned source fixture is removed after the application exits. I also inspected the active frame: the visible status reports the checked-part count while the search remains pending. This operational UI check complements the component proof above, which specifically cancels after uncached vectors have been visited. The UI captures do not claim cancellation after the cache-population threshold themselves. One timing sample per case is intentionally insufficient for p95, recorded as null; no performance gate is claimed from this run.

The optimized harness build and the default 100-file/150-vector component fixture check pass (`/tmp/phase6-native-cancel-build-20261027.log`, `/tmp/clip-native-fixture-default-20261027.json`). Targeted no-dependency Clippy, Python compilation and whitespace checks also pass (`/tmp/phase6-native-cancel-clippy-20261027.stderr`). These changes affect test fixtures/drivers only; the 2,929-test workspace pass above remains the latest full-suite validation, not a claim that it was repeated afterward.

### Audio restart shortcut diagnosis — 2026-10-27

The checkpoint driver now supports `--keyboard-close`, retaining Wayland input traces and the resumed window's focus observation, and a separate diagnostic `--refresh-virtual-keymap` option. The unmodified-input release/dark run reproduces the earlier Ctrl+Q timeout (`/tmp/clip-checkpoint-audio-keyboard-20261027a`). The resumed window is focused. Its two keyboard objects initially receive the default 35,572-byte keymap, then the virtual keyboard sends Control plus key code 1 without delivering a replacement map. This is consistent with Escape under the default map rather than the dynamically mapped Q sent by wtype. The first process, which did close successfully, had received the replacement virtual keymap before its shortcut.

The controlled release/dark comparison changes only the private input sequence: send unbound F24 first, then Ctrl+Q (`/tmp/clip-checkpoint-audio-keyboard-20261027b`). The trace now shows a 1,436-byte replacement map for F24, then a 1,434-byte replacement map before Control plus key code 1, and the application exits successfully. No application shortcut or shutdown code changed. This isolates the observed failure to virtual-keymap reuse across clients in the private input path; the precise internal compositor/client caching cause has not been independently established. The diagnostic refresh remains explicit in the test CLI rather than a hidden application workaround or an assumption that the original failed run passed.

That passing run also verifies the original eight-segment/200-second checkpoint resumes to all 17 canonical audio windows covering 425 seconds, preserving the saved segment/vector prefix and unchanged source content. This resolves the earlier unexplained keyboard observation as an automation-input limitation; it does not establish a physical-keyboard defect in Files. The original failure and its protocol evidence remain available.

The final driver passes the same release/light comparison as well (`/tmp/clip-checkpoint-audio-keyboard-20261027c/report.json`, `checkpoint-checks.json`), including successful keyboard close after explicit virtual-keymap refresh. Diagnostic options are passed directly through the existing checkpoint workflow, rather than adding mutable configuration to the shared Session or a new runner. Final architecture comparison against `phase6-native-cancel-20261027` adds one helper and 73 measured source lines, with clone groups unchanged at 1,471, cycles unchanged at 15 and maximum CCN unchanged at 195; existing modified-file clone warnings remain disclosed. One interim wrapper produced a false symbol-resolution cycle through `subprocess.run`; removing that unnecessary wrapper also removed the new cycle report. The remaining Phase 6 work is performance, broader representative relevance/precision evidence and the final requirement audit; no completion claim or audio-similarity feature follows from these checks.

### Filename admission join order and native CPU fallback — 2026-10-27

Filename retrieval now starts at paths and runs the existing Unicode/escaped-filename matcher before looking up the file's covering record and source-session/root membership. SQLite previously chose file → path even when no basename matched, causing a lookup for every file. The only production change is an explicit path → file `CROSS JOIN` using the existing covering index; Here/Recursive queries retain directory/range index choices. Matching, filters, current-source admission, ranking, evidence and the post-admission limit are unchanged. There is no new cache, schema field or filename index.

Read-only diagnostics are retained in `/tmp/phase6-name-join-diagnostic-20261027.json`. They use a Python matcher over the frozen ASCII fixture, so their timings are not production measurements. A separate comparison of hidden FTS rank versus explicit BM25 (`/tmp/phase6-fts-rank-diagnostic-20261027.json`) finds identical row/score digests but only a small timing difference; that alternative was not retained. Sampling profiling attempts were unavailable under current process permissions; no host performance/security settings were changed.

Fresh optimized before/after component reports each use 100,000 files, 150,000 synthetic f16 vectors and 21 warm samples (`/tmp/clip-names-before-150k-20261027.json`, `/tmp/clip-names-after-150k-20261027.json`). P95 milliseconds:

| Case | Before | After |
|---|---:|---:|
| Exact filename | 37.53 | 11.13 |
| Rare keyword | 33.68 | 10.45 |
| Common keyword | 200.25 | 179.70 |
| Default-floor hybrid | 295.28 | 261.80 |
| Broad-floor hybrid | 345.08 | 313.22 |

The filename prefilter is part of keyword and hybrid requests too, so this removes work from all five cases. The common lexical case still misses 100 ms. The optimized search suite passes **212 tests, zero failures, four ignored** (`/tmp/phase6-names-search-tests-20261027.log`). Frozen quality targets pass and all returned results, exact-name cases and f32-reference payloads equal the previous production report (`/tmp/clip-names-after-quality-20261027.json`, `/tmp/clip-names-quality-comparison-20261027.json`). The fixture's input hashes are identical; the existing equivalent Recursive/Indexed assertion also runs. This establishes controlled-fixture equivalence, not representative-corpus quality.

The rebuilt release application passes the private dark native 100,000-file / 150,000-vector run with 21 warm samples/case (`/tmp/clip-names-native-150k-20261027a/report.json`, `scale-latency.json`). Exact-name / rare-keyword / common-keyword / broad hybrid p95 are **467.56 / 483.41 / 676.70 / 459.79 ms**. Three cases are within the measured 500 ms budget; common-keyword hybrid remains above it. Every case is hybrid with the intentional -1 floor over synthetic vectors, including actual inference, normal source reconciliation, final source verification, explanations and UI acceptance/sorting. GPU frame presentation and cold loading remain excluded. Older native observations are not a controlled paired experiment, so their full difference is not attributed solely to this SQL change.

The same driver now offers `--cpu-fallback`: it hides CUDA devices and points Vulkan driver discovery at an absent path **only in the owned application's environment after the private compositors start**. It changes no installed runtime, desktop environment or user configuration. After querying, the driver inspects only direct children of its own Files process and requires one embedding worker with `--device cpu`; production startup validates that worker's selected backend. The CUDA run likewise records its actual worker arguments. This prevents an assumed CPU label from standing in for evidence of the selected backend.

The matched release/light CPU-fallback native run passes (`/tmp/clip-names-native-cpu-150k-20261027a/report.json`, `scale-latency.json`), also with 100,000 files / 150,000 vectors and 21 warm samples per case. P95 exact-name / rare-keyword / common-keyword / broad hybrid is **440.48 / 434.33 / 617.34 / 436.57 ms**, within the separate **two-second warm CPU target** for all four measured cases. Its scope and exclusions match the CUDA run. These short warmed text queries do not establish CPU superiority for longer text, media indexing or Q/A inference. Both native runs verify unchanged vector fingerprints and remove their owned source fixtures after application exit.

Optimized library/application builds, targeted no-dependency Clippy, Python compilation and whitespace checks pass (`/tmp/phase6-names-build-20261027.log`, `/tmp/phase6-names-release-app-20261027.log`, `/tmp/phase6-names-clippy-20261027.stderr`). Architecture comparison against `phase6-filename-join-order-20261027` adds no functions, clone groups, cycles or complexity; 12 measured lines come from extending the existing native driver with backend verification/fallback setup. Clone groups remain 1,471, cycles 15 and maximum CCN 195; existing modified-file clone warnings retain the aggregate REGRESSION classification. No new architecture hotspot is reported. The previous 2,929-test workspace run is not claimed as repeated after this narrow SQL change. Common-keyword performance, representative relevance/precision evidence and the full requirements audit remain open; the goal is not complete.

### Independent real-file quality and weight precision — 2026-10-27, reviewed 2026-10-30

The quality tools now also accept a declared frozen local corpus (`--corpus`) instead of requiring the original generated fixtures. The same production extractors, embedding workers, SQLite publication, source admission, fusion and Recursive/Indexed equivalence assertions are reused. Fixture ingestion bounds the flat inventory and file sizes, opens ordinary files without following symlinks or blocking on FIFOs, and verifies byte lengths and SHA-256 hashes. It copies only into a new owned output directory. Local validation manifests travel byte-identically with the primary manifest; legacy generated-corpus behavior remains available. The added ingestion tests cover changed bytes, extra files, traversal, symlinks and FIFOs. Python search-model tests pass 73 cases, and ranking-driver tests pass six; the optimized legacy quality gate also passes before the ranking adjustment below.

The independent corpus is `/home/vega/Documents/type_testing/clip-representative-search-20261027`: 36 files (six PDFs, eight text files, eight images, twelve audio recordings and two videos), 60 primary positive queries, 20 primary negatives, ten exact-name queries, and a separate ten-positive/twelve-negative validation manifest. Expectations were frozen before inference. Primary manifest SHA-256 is `65cb62116a9beb64d1f082cefed239e48d46812ad818985e9d1bbea36c074061`; validation SHA-256 is `88721eaca98dbfb1b4f268784271c11d7b2cc0a04b5be5c4e405b595b4f9390c`. No source assets or personal contents are committed. PDF excerpts retain substantive body pages as well as opening pages; all 57 excerpt-page rasters were compared with their corresponding original pages, and image/video contact sheets were inspected. Final PDF QA is `/tmp/clip-representative-review-20261027/pdf-qa-final/checks.json`. The audio recordings use published class labels from [ESC-50](https://github.com/karolpiczak/ESC-50), pinned to revision `33c8ce9eb2cf0b1c2f8bcf322eb349b6be34dbb6`; source identifiers, URLs and checksums are in the fixture manifest. The videos deliberately reuse two source clips with multiple descriptions: those are correlated paraphrases, **not twelve independent video examples**. This remains a small local quality sample, not a population-quality guarantee.

The first production run **fails** the default product gates: 62/70 expected files appear in the top ten (88.57%); audio 85.71%, image 100%, PDF 92.86%, text 85.71%, video 78.57%. Three of 32 negative queries return results (9.375%). Report: `/tmp/clip-representative-q8-ranking-20261027a.json`. A calibration-only candidate with floors text 0.64/image 0.63/audio 0.65 improves aggregate recall to 68/70, but has five negative results and still misses some split-level targets. It is **not installed as the application default**. The earlier generated-corpus pass does not override this failure.

The matched Q8/BF16 run completes and records the failed product gate without disguising it as a precision failure (`/tmp/clip-representative-precision-20261027a/report.json` and `.driver.log`). Identical request/device checks, exact-name tiers and storage-precision checks pass. Both weight variants retrieve 62/70 at default settings: **zero percentage-point Recall@10 loss from Q8** in both float32 working representations and canonical f16 storage. BF16 produces two negative results versus Q8's three. Lower ordering changes occur and one expected audio rank changes, so identical recall does not mean identical rankings. The maximum matched returned-segment score difference is approximately 0.0058. These observations support retaining Q8; neither weight variant repairs the underlying default retrieval miss.

Inspection of the frozen failures exposes a production ranking defect: short descriptions such as “A dog barking” or “A baby crying” trigger the fallback OR keyword search. An unrelated document containing only “a” receives the same lexical fusion weight as a document matching every query word, pushing the correct audio to rank 14. The next adjustment addresses partial-keyword evidence strength, without changing fixtures, model weights, cosine floors or protected-file admission. Representative quality, common-keyword latency and the full requirements audit remain open.

### Partial-keyword fusion correction — 2026-10-30

The existing bounded lexical list now records whether it came only from the short-query OR rescue. Full-name/all-term matches retain their existing weight 2; partial rescue uses half of one semantic modality's weight, normalized by the number of semantic lists. The same Top storage, SQL admission, cancellation, exact-name tiers and evidence merging remain in place. No stopwords are discarded, and literal content/name commands are unaffected. The `window invoice` partial-recall test remains green. New regression coverage places a strong semantic recording ahead of 24 unrelated documents containing “a”, preserves the partial results, and checks literal single-letter/non-English tokens. The existing modality-weight test now also proves partial evidence cannot gain an advantage in a mixed-modality folder.

Optimized search tests pass **213 cases, zero failures, four ignored** (`/tmp/phase6-partial-keyword-tests-20261030.log`). Frozen Q8 and BF16 production-ranking replays improve default recall from 62/70 to **64/70**; audio improves to 14/14, and the remaining misses are below cosine floors. Exact-name tiers and f16/f32 storage gates pass. Reports `/tmp/clip-partial-q8-ranking-20261030.json` and `/tmp/clip-partial-bf16-ranking-20261030.json` retain the failed video product gate. A matched ranking-only weight comparison reuses the original inference outputs and confirms zero Q8 recall loss (`/tmp/clip-partial-weight-comparison-20261030.json`). It is explicitly a ranking replay, not fresh inference. The generated-corpus regression still passes 72/72 positives and 2/32 negatives (`/tmp/clip-partial-legacy-ranking-20261030b.json`). An initial legacy replay used the parent run folder instead of its `cuda` child and fails with missing inputs; that invocation is retained separately.

Targeted no-dependency Clippy passes with existing unrelated warnings (`/tmp/phase6-partial-clippy-20261030.log`). Architecture comparison against `phase6-partial-keyword-ranking-20261030` adds one regression-test function and 37 measured lines; maximum CCN remains 195, clone groups 1,471 and cycles 15. No new high-complexity function is reported. The tool retains the existing modified-file clone warnings; it also reorders the same unrelated video-benchmark cycle's members, reporting it as both removed and added without changing the cycle count. This does not establish an actual new dependency. Full workspace validation and the image-cutoff calibration follow below; these checks do not close the goal.

The final weight replay verifies unchanged inference/manifest/validation input hashes, includes the current core configuration source checksum, and passes all its recorded gates (`/tmp/clip-final-floor-weight-comparison-20261030.json`). These are stronger small-corpus results, not general equivalence or a guarantee of zero false matches.
