### Common-keyword profiling and rejected hasher experiment — 2026-10-30

Current native timing logs isolate the remaining expensive common-term case to retrieval, rather than the approximately 40 ms source-verification step or approximately 15 ms keyword-evidence step. A GDB diagnostic now captures **400 stopped stacks** from an owned 100,000-file/150,000-vector test. Of 383 stacks containing lexical retrieval, 114 include BM25, 74 the lexical rank callback, ten the filename callback and 185 other SQLite/admission work. Raw samples and classification are `/tmp/clip-keyword-profile-20261030/samples4.json` and `analysis.json`. These are interrupted wall-time observations, not an additive time decomposition or latency acceptance. The sampling window includes filename scans between full-text requests. The analysis pins the pre-experiment executable hash from the separate before benchmark; it does not hash the subsequently rebuilt executable and pretend that was profiled.

The first debugger CLI invocation fails argument handling; three preliminary GDB attempts produce no samples and are retained as failed diagnostics. GDB's `noprint` signal setting also implies `nostop`; explicit `stop print nopass` and an external timer produce the actual samples. Only the owned test process receives signals. The owned sampler is reaped before stopping the test, and the interrupted test's fixture tree is removed after GDB exits. No host profiling permissions, compositor input or system settings are changed.

A small experiment replaces only Top's path-map hasher with egui's existing randomized hasher. All 214 then-current search tests pass, but matched plain optimized component runs (100,000 files/150,000 vectors, 21 samples/case) show **no useful gain**: common-term p95 181.44 → 183.53 ms, broad hybrid 344.46 → 344.27 ms (`/tmp/clip-common-hasher-before-20261030.json`, `/tmp/clip-common-hasher-after-20261030.json`). Exact/rare cases move from 11.13/10.46 to 12.26/11.90 ms. The hasher change is **reverted**; the final implementation retains the original collections and ranking. These results narrow the next performance work toward the SQLite/postings path rather than supporting a new cache or weakening admission. The 100 ms common-lexical and 500 ms common-hybrid goals remain open.

### Date-filter audit: daylight saving and resolved boundaries — 2026-10-30

The requirement audit finds two missing pieces of direct evidence/behavior: no explicit daylight-saving-day regression, and no displayed resolved date bounds. A new test launches only itself in an isolated child with `TZ=America/New_York`; it checks the 23-hour 2026-03-08 and 25-hour 2026-11-01, exact UTC endpoints, inclusive day/range bounds and whole-day comparisons. It never changes the parent test process's timezone. The existing parser already handles these days correctly; the new check closes a verification gap rather than changing date semantics.

Applied date filters now show a compact UTC-boundary summary in the search status. It formats the captured interval, never reparses the date under a later timezone. Original selector text, exact inclusive/exclusive comparison, nanoseconds and exclusion are preserved. Long summaries truncate within the available width and expose the full text through egui's standard tooltip. The same resolved constraints appear in answer-column/header tooltips. Invalid drafts continue showing the last applied query and its bounds. Numeric size filters and ordinary filename queries produce no date summary. UTC is the displayed resolved timezone, so local-day DST changes remain visible as different UTC endpoints without ambiguous abbreviations.

Query regressions cover explicit offsets, nanoseconds, negation, non-date selectors and question-column selectors. The final optimized search suite passes **215 tests, zero failures, four ignored** (`/tmp/phase6-date-summary-release-search-tests-20261030.log`); the narrower original DST run passes all three range tests (`/tmp/phase6-dst-tests-20261030.log`). Native rendered validation and final checks follow below. This closes only the date items inspected so far, not the consolidated full Phase 6 audit.

The rebuilt release application passes both final private native controls runs: `/tmp/clip-phase6-date-controls-dark-20261030b/report.json` and `/tmp/clip-phase6-date-controls-light-20261030a/report.json`, **27 captured observations each**. They check an explicit +02:00 input displayed at the corresponding UTC instant, an excluded date interval, and retention of the prior result/bounds after an invalid date draft. I inspected dark and light screenshots, including the invalid-draft frame. The first dark attempt stops at its initial OCR because its test environment omitted `TESSDATA_PREFIX`; no feature assertion ran in that attempt. The corrected runs use `/home/vega/.local/share/tessdata`. The native fixture's modification time is fixed explicitly, keeping the date assertions independent of the current clock.

Both final runs also recheck `/proc`, `/sys`, `/dev`, owned `.ssh`/`.git` directories and an owned kernel-target symlink. Each records **no database created, no enrolled roots and no installed model** in `protected-enrollment.json`. These checks exercise disabled enrollment through the real UI and retain the original filter/draft/literal-name scenario.

The final current-tree workspace run passes **2,932 tests, zero failures, 38 ignored, 26 suites**, serial with the configured Tesseract data (`/tmp/phase6-date-summary-workspace-20261030.log`). Final targeted no-dependency Clippy passes with existing warnings (`/tmp/phase6-date-summary-clippy-20261030.log`); formatting, Python compilation and whitespace checks pass. Architecture comparison against `phase6-common-keyword-20261030` adds 145 measured lines and four functions (two formatters and two regressions), with no new high-complexity function. Clone groups remain 1,471, cycles 15 and maximum CCN 195. The aggregate remains REGRESSION from modified-file clone warnings/necessary added functionality; no new clone group or cycle is introduced. No hasher experiment remains in production.

A final read-only, interleaved SQLite diagnostic also rejects pager memory mapping as the next optimization: eight warmed samples per setting yield median **155.95 ms without mapping versus 165.47 ms with a 1 GiB mapping**, with identical 750-row/score digests (`/tmp/clip-keyword-profile-20261030/mmap-diagnostic.json`). Its callback uses a static fixture-only threshold and Python SQLite, so these are diagnostic timings rather than production acceptance. No mmap setting is changed in the application or persisted to user configuration. Common-keyword performance and the consolidated requirements audit remain open. Changes remain unstaged and uncommitted, with the goal active.

### Keyword-path audit and rejected alternatives — 2026-10-30

A fresh plain optimized component baseline still records common-keyword p95 **178.52 ms**, versus exact/rare filename/keyword 10.68/10.62 ms (`/tmp/clip-raw-path-before-20261030.json`, 100,000 files/150,000 vectors, 21 samples). Raw `OsStr` keys were considered but not implemented: `Path` equality normalizes equivalent spellings, and changing only the map keys would make its identity disagree with ordering/fusion, especially for imported noncanonical spellings. A standalone lookup diagnostic preserving `Path` semantics rejects an ordered map too: 100,000 lookups against 500 retained paths take approximately **5.4–5.9 ms with HashMap versus 93.6–98.7 ms with BTreeMap** (`/tmp/clip-path-lookup-micro-20261030.log`). This is a collection diagnostic, not application latency.

A read-only exact-token/prefix SQL comparison returns identical row/score digests on the special fixture with only one matching vocabulary term, but median 153.00 versus 147.74 ms is insufficient to justify vocabulary machinery, and replacing prefix semantics generally would lose valid matches (`/tmp/clip-prefix-diagnostic-20261030.json`). No ranking collection, prefix rule or eligibility rule is changed. Review also confirms that the existing excerpt implementation already uses character indices and its long-CJK/case-expansion regression passes; there is no excerpt bug or fix in this checkpoint. The semantic plan's stale code/name-only table entry is corrected to match its existing recognized-source-code text decision.

### Literal content extraction skips unrelated media work — 2026-10-30

The scope audit finds real unnecessary work in literal content search: it used the same full extractor as embedding indexing, decoding images/audio/video even though those preparations contain no searchable text. The actual old WAV reader returns an audio payload even when sent a `text_only` field (then unknown/ignored), recorded in `/tmp/clip-literal-text-mode-20261030/before.json`.

`Request::Extract` now has a backward-compatible, default-false `text_only` field. The existing reader continues to own source/root validation, pinned input handles and post-read identity verification for either mode. Literal traversal requests text-only extraction. A shared supported-text classifier reuses the document reader's format selection and existing media classification, allowing traversal to skip unsupported media before dispatching any extraction helper. It preserves the unsupported/incomplete count. Direct text-only reader requests likewise return an explicit name-only reason without preparing media. Full indexing and visual Q/A callers explicitly retain full extraction; JSON clients omitting the new field keep that behavior.

For PDFs, literal search extracts page text without generating a model image. Sparse/scanned pages still disclose partial text coverage and unavailable OCR; the optimization cannot turn an incomplete no-match into a complete result. Page numbers, next-page cursors and extracted text are unchanged. Default full preparation still supplies the normal text plus visual segments for sparse pages. No automatic OCR/transcription or index/pipeline migration is introduced.

New regressions cover invalid image/audio/video/MIDI inputs without decoder invocation, plaintext parity, old JSON requests, replacement rejection, both pages of a sparse PDF, and traversal skipping media before helper dispatch. The PDF case reuses the existing deterministic acceptance-fixture generator. Search tests pass **219 cases**, zero failures, four ignored (`/tmp/phase6-literal-text-tests-20261030b.log` and the final fixture-refactored `/tmp/phase6-literal-text-final-search-tests-20261030.log`). An initial compile error came from a test calling the private dispatcher; the test now uses the existing root-capture API, without widening dispatcher visibility. Reusing `only_source` for independent malformed-media fixtures removes the one added clone group.

The rebuilt release helper passes operational and syscall checks. With identical owned WAV bytes, full preparation still returns audio, while text-only returns no media data (`after.json`). Annotated `strace` checks match the complete owned source path, excluding library and standard-I/O descriptors: **12 WAV reads and FFmpeg in full mode, zero WAV payload reads and no decoder in text-only mode**. Sparse PDF text-only runs `pdfinfo`/`pdftotext`, preserves text/cursor/partial coverage and launches no `pdftoppm`/other renderer; full mode still renders. All four checks pass (`/tmp/clip-literal-text-mode-20261030/trace-report.json`). Exec lists include failed PATH lookup attempts and must not be interpreted as counts of decoder processes. The single before/after timing samples are operational observations, not a latency benchmark.

Private mixed-document acceptance passes in both appearances (`/tmp/clip-literal-documents-{dark,light}-20261030a/report.json`). Its previously outdated seven-result expectation is corrected to include the already-supported `Source.rs`: eight matching document/code/encoding fixtures. It also includes sparse PDF text-only reads and three malformed media inputs, preserving external-entity/script omission and no implicit index. After explicit enrollment, nine remaining files produce twelve vectors, including two text and two image segments for the two-page sparse PDF. Unsupported media fixtures are removed before enrollment, since full media indexing deliberately retains its decoder behavior.

A strengthened dark run starts literal queries with **no inference runtime or models installed**, verifies the index is still absent, then links existing test weights only for explicit enrollment. The complete literal → enrollment → semantic flow passes (`/tmp/clip-literal-documents-no-runtime-20261030a/report.json`). This distinguishes model-free literal extraction from a run that merely had models available but did not need them.

The complete workspace run passes **2,936 tests, zero failures, 38 ignored, 26 suites**, serial with the configured Tesseract data (`/tmp/phase6-literal-text-workspace-20261030.log`). It predates only the malformed-fixture refactor; the final 219-test search run above covers that refactor. Release build, targeted no-dependency Clippy, formatting, Python compilation and whitespace checks pass (`/tmp/phase6-literal-text-release-app-20261030.log`, `/tmp/phase6-literal-text-clippy-20261030.log`). Architecture comparison against `phase6-literal-text-mode-20261030` adds 191 measured lines and five net functions, with no new high-complexity function, clone group or cycle; groups remain 1,471, cycles 15 and maximum CCN 195. Existing modified-file warnings/necessary functionality retain the aggregate REGRESSION classification. Final real-corpus input/ranking replay follows below.

The audit also confirms that literal traversal currently re-extracts supported documents even when a current index could supply their text. Safe reuse of current indexed text remains an implementation item, alongside common-keyword latency and the consolidated requirements audit. This checkpoint does not close the full Phase 6 goal. Changes remain unstaged and uncommitted; audio-sample similarity remains excluded.

The fresh actual-reader Q8 run over the unchanged real-file corpus prepares **170 segments with all 424 model request hashes identical** to the prior precision corpus, preserving both fixture and validation hashes (`/tmp/clip-literal-text-input-comparison-20261030.json`). The current debug production ranker passes `--require-targets`: default **66/70 Recall@10**, 3/32 negatives, all exact filename tiers and the f16/f32 storage gate (`/tmp/clip-literal-text-ranking-20261030.json`). This is a correctness replay, not a release-latency measurement. Matched returned cases, exact-name cases and f32-reference comparison are retained in `/tmp/clip-literal-text-ranking-comparison-20261030.json`. The previously disclosed small-corpus/video-diversity limits still apply.

### Reuse current indexed text for literal content queries — 2026-10-30

Literal `content` searches now consult the existing segment text before starting another extraction. They still enumerate the requested scope afresh; this does not make ordinary queries create an index or enroll a folder. The cache requires the currently observed path/device/inode/revision, extraction version, verified membership, an available source and matching source-session continuity. Unknown unique mount observations, mismatched mount IDs and optional-cache errors fall back to extraction. The existing source scope supports a subdirectory query on the same observed mount and retains its process-only restriction for remote/unknown filesystems. It does not require embedding-model inference or a matching current encoder pipeline; stored file/segment pipelines must agree.

Tokenized passages overlap, so their exact UTF-8 source ranges are reconstructed and overlap bytes checked before literal matching. Missing/malformed/gapped ranges, repeated location groups, incompatible pipelines, more than 2,048 stored parts or more than 16 MiB of stored text cause a cache miss. No new schema, persistent text copy or resident cache is added. Fresh and cached paths share the previous streaming literal matcher, preserving case folding, whitespace normalization, excerpts and cross-part matches. Reuse checks cancellation and uses the existing SQLite cancellation/epoch guards. The status and source explanation disclose indexed-text reuse.

A partial/checkpointed or visual publication can prove a positive text match, with incomplete coverage retained. It cannot prove a negative: a no-match in that publication falls back to current extraction instead of concealing unindexed text. Complete text-only publications can supply either result. Unsupported media retain the text-only skipping behavior from the preceding checkpoint.

The final search suite passes **226 tests, zero failures, four ignored** (`/tmp/phase6-cached-content-search-final2-20261030.log`). Seven initial cache tests were consolidated into six without losing their cases: exact reconstruction/no invented overlap phrases; partial/visual/checkpoint negatives; stale/unverified/unavailable membership; changed live file/session/mount identities; malformed/oversized inputs; UTF-8 splits/gaps/repeated groups; and cancellation followed by successful reuse. A shared-matcher regression additionally checks case/whitespace and cross-part evidence. Capability-aware unit tests require a miss on kernels without unique mount observations; the native runs below separately prove positive reuse on this machine.

Both private native runs pass with eleven rendered observations each: `/tmp/clip-cached-content-dark-20261030b/report.json` and `/tmp/clip-cached-content-light-20261030b/report.json`. Each starts without inference runtime/models, completes the model-free literal queries without creating a semantic index, then explicitly enrolls nine files and produces the unchanged twelve vectors. The subsequent literal query reports **eight matching documents and eight indexed-text reuses**. An additional PDF-only positive reuses one file and explicitly labels unexamined non-text/unfinished content as partial, even when its complete multimodal index has `complete` coverage. The reuse count uses singular/plural file wording. After background indexing is paused and `Source.rs` is edited, the old phrase returns seven documents and the new phrase finds the changed file through fresh extraction. Semantic retrieval still finds the original eight documents before that edit. I inspected the reuse screenshots in both themes. These are operational correctness checks, not a controlled latency benchmark or proof of general extraction accuracy.

Architecture comparison against `phase6-indexed-literal-reuse-20261030` adds 465 measured lines and 19 functions (including tests), with no new high-complexity function, clone group or cycle. Clone groups remain 1,471, cycles 15 and maximum CCN 195. The initial dense cache function and duplicated mutation-test setup were simplified by separating source eligibility, SQL publication lookup, exact passage assembly and literal interpretation; the shared matcher avoids duplicating the old walk logic. Aggregate classification remains REGRESSION from existing modified-file warnings/necessary added functionality; unchanged cycles sometimes appear as resolved in the analyzer and are not claimed as improvements.

The full workspace passes **2,943 tests, zero failures, 38 ignored, 26 suites**, serial with the configured Tesseract path (`/tmp/phase6-cached-content-workspace-20261030.log`). That run precedes only the final coverage-label/conditional-style/singular-wording adjustments. The final 226-test search run above covers the coverage assertion and conditional cleanup; final native runs additionally cover singular wording and the PDF partial label. Final release build and targeted no-dependency Clippy pass (`/tmp/phase6-cached-content-release-final-20261030.log`, `/tmp/phase6-cached-content-clippy-final-20261030.log`); Clippy has no warnings in the new cache/matcher modules or updated walker, while existing project warnings remain. Formatting, Python compilation and whitespace checks pass. Source/binary/report fingerprints are retained in `/tmp/phase6-cached-content-evidence-20261030.json`. Common-keyword latency and the consolidated requirement-by-requirement Phase 6 audit remain open. Audio sample similarity remains excluded; these changes are unstaged and uncommitted.

### Final source verification for literal results — 2026-10-30

The consolidated audit found a real freshness hole: literal search verifies each source while extracting it, but an early match could change while the remaining subtree is scanned and still be published. The same hole also affected indexed-text reuse after its fresh inventory observation. Semantic search already had a final source-validation pass.

The shared source-verification pass now lives in `reader/verification.rs` and serves both semantic and literal result publication. It uses the existing pinned `VerifyBatch` reader request and owned bounded helper transport; no source payload hashing/decoding or UI-thread filesystem work is added. Requests stay within the existing 1,000-entry reader cap, including literal result sets larger than the semantic 500-candidate window. Reader failures admit no unchecked paths, one failed source does not erase paths verified under another source, and cancellation prevents publication. Literal traversal removes rejected entries **and their explanations**, increments its incomplete-read count and explicitly reports stale/unavailable omissions. Ordinary metadata/name queries retain their existing path; explicit offline semantic records retain their separate last-known label/handling. This is a final best-effort source observation, not a promise that a file cannot change after publication; action-time guards remain necessary.

Two new component tests use the actual pinned-source/root validation with a deterministic transport seam. A 1,002-file case changes the first file and removes the last, proves batches of 1,000 and two, preserves the unchanged files including the later batch, and rejects both obsolete records. A second case exercises an invalid root alongside a valid root and cancellation during/preceding validation. Shared setup reuses the reader's existing single-source fixture instead of duplicating it; the helper dispatcher remains private. The search suite passes **228 tests, zero failures, four ignored** (`/tmp/phase6-final-content-verification-tests-final-20261030.log`). The parser's existing command-example test now additionally includes every input-table form in the Phase 6 plan; the final workspace run covers that expansion.

A new private native test, `scripts/file_manager_content_freshness_acceptance.py`, proves the actual race rather than relying on a guessed sleep. It watches close events only inside its owned nested fixture directory, waits until offscreen `0500.txt` and its successor `0501.txt` have been read by the sequential search, then changes `0500.txt`. The parent browser is outside that nested directory, avoiding a current-directory refresh accidentally masking the race. The before executable reproduces **1,002 matches with zero incomplete reads**, despite the edited file no longer containing the phrase (`/tmp/clip-content-freshness-before-20261030a/report.json` and `source-edit-checks.json`; `--expect-stale` explicitly marks this as a diagnostic reproduction of bad behavior). Both current dark/light runs instead return **1,001 matches and one stale/unavailable omission** (`/tmp/clip-content-freshness-{dark,light}-20261030a/report.json`). All 1,001 unchanged matches survive beyond the first verification batch. Neither executable's test creates an index, runtime or model installation. All edits/input are confined to owned fixtures and private Weston/Niri sockets. I inspected the before/current rendered results.

The updated shared semantic path also passes the mixed-document native regression (`/tmp/clip-final-verification-documents-dark-20261030a/report.json`): model-free initial literal search, explicit nine-file/twelve-vector enrollment, eight cached text reuses, the PDF's partial visual-coverage label, eight semantic matches and fresh literal results after a paused-index edit. This checks both callers of the shared verifier; it is not a retrieval-quality or latency benchmark.

The final current workspace passes **2,945 tests, zero failures, 38 ignored, 26 suites**, serial with the configured Tesseract data (`/tmp/phase6-content-final-verification-workspace-20261030.log`). Release build, targeted no-dependency Clippy, formatting, Python compilation and whitespace checks pass. The final Clippy run has no diagnostics in the new verification module; existing project warnings remain (`/tmp/phase6-content-final-verification-release-20261030.log`, `/tmp/phase6-content-final-verification-clippy-final-20261030.log`). The release build precedes only test-array/fixture-style changes, so it includes all final production behavior exercised natively.

Architecture comparison against `phase6-content-final-verification-20261030` adds 225 measured lines and ten functions, including tests/native automation, with no new excessive-complexity function, clone group or dependency cycle. Clone groups remain 1,471, cycles 15 and maximum CCN 195. Aggregate classification remains REGRESSION from necessary added functionality/existing modified-file warnings; structural health moves from 62.68 to 62.57 and is not presented as an improvement. Existing semantic validation was moved and shared rather than copied.

The new [completion audit](docs/File%20Manager/phase6_audit.md), linked from the main plan, inventories the complete non-audio-similarity scope with explicit checked/partial/pending/open dispositions. It records the source/evidence still needing inspection, rather than equating a large passing suite with full completion. The mid-scan literal freshness obligation is closed for its declared test scope. The broader audit and previously measured common-keyword latency failures remain open; no other Phase 6 scope is removed. Source/binary/report fingerprints are retained in `/tmp/phase6-content-final-verification-evidence-20261030.json`. ### Enrollment/navigation/image audit and local catalog storage — 2026-10-30

The requirement audit now distinguishes inspected behavior from missing proof for the enrollment/button, protected-content, command/navigation and image-order groups. The default root list is empty; enrollment still runs guarded admission before creating/persisting a root. I rechecked the actual retained red/yellow/green screenshots in both themes against the current RGB constants: each expected color has at least 36 matching pixels in the Index control (`/tmp/phase6-audit-index-color-recheck-20261030.json`). Ready green also requires the current observed mount. Yellow deliberately covers paused/incomplete/error states as well as active work; its tooltip supplies the specific state, rather than falsely declaring those folders ready.

Fresh private native runs pass: ordinary type-navigation (10 observations), progressive navigation (7), private same-filesystem bind mount (11), hidden-index policy (12), exclusions (22), command controls in both themes (31 each), and image ordering (7). Their reports are `/tmp/clip-audit-type-navigation-dark-20261030a`, `clip-audit-progressive-dark-20261030a`, `clip-audit-mount-dark-20261030b`, `clip-audit-hidden-dark-20261030a`, `clip-audit-exclusions-dark-20261030a`, `clip-audit-controls-dark-20261030a`, `clip-audit-controls-light-20261030b`, and `clip-audit-image-order-dark-20261030b` (all under `/tmp`, each with `report.json`). These are **131 captured observations**, not 131 independent feature/quality benchmarks. Mounts and input remain confined to private user/mount and Weston/Niri namespaces. The type/navigation/mount runs precede the catalog-hosting guard below; their relevant input/mount/UI code is unchanged. Subsequent actual local-catalog hidden/exclusion runs exercise that guard too.

The checks prove offscreen reveal, Backspace/expiry/activation and editor isolation; local typo/progressive return, extra-root fallback and explicit scope isolation; mount exclusion until separately enrolled, root removal and remount/re-enrollment; hidden indexing independent of browsing with retirement/re-inclusion; component-safe exact exclusions, paused-cache retirement and blocked excluded-root enrollment. The controls additionally retain resolved date bounds and invalid-draft results, keep options draft-only, and reject enrollment in `/proc`, `/sys`, `/dev`, owned `.ssh`/`.git`, and a `/proc` alias without installing models or creating roots/database. Source/membership, parser, date/birth-time, header-sort and pagination code/tests were inspected against the corresponding audit rows. Same-path overlapping-root and unsupported-record details remain explicitly assigned to the storage/format audit, not assumed complete from these runs.

Two small implementation gaps were fixed:

- **Live SQLite placement:** the former network denylist did not establish locality; FUSE, Ceph and unknown filesystems could bypass it. The existing pinned-directory `fstatfs` admission now accepts supported local types explicitly: Btrfs, ext2/3/4, XFS, ZFS, F2FS, bcachefs, tmpfs and overlayfs. Other/network/FUSE types fail before SQLite opens, with an instruction to choose local `XDG_DATA_HOME`. The tradeoff is deliberate refusal of some genuinely local FUSE/unknown implementations for hosting the live catalog. This restriction does not classify indexed source folders or exported self-contained backups; ordinary remote/FUSE source readers remain available. No schema/pipeline change or automatic relocation/deletion is introduced. The semantic plan documents the supported types and override. A policy test covers eight accepted types plus network/FUSE/unknown rejection; the actual sole-writer test now also proves mode 0700 for the directory and 0600 for database/lock after initially loose directory permissions, empty enrollment and reopening after release. This is type-policy/ownership evidence plus native local operation, not a claimed real ZFS/FUSE hosting trial.
- **Unknown family metadata:** a broken/unreadable entry previously passed a negated family such as `!image` because unknown was coerced to false. Family selectors now preserve unknown through the existing Option-based predicate, like size/date constraints. A real broken-link fixture checks all seven positive and negative families and preserves known-basename glob matching. The dark/light native controls prove `image`, `!image` and `!file` cannot classify the unreadable `broken.jpg`, while `*.jpg` still finds its known name. Source format ambiguity is a separate remaining item below.

Image-order source inspection and the fresh native run prove explicit 64-bit field parsing independent of version/color, unsigned raw order including `u64::MAX`, XOR/popcount distances 0/64, header reversal, captured reference unaffected by selecting another image, edit-triggered invalidation/reapplication, bounded two-job analysis and failed/missing-last behavior. The actual helper hash and absence of index/models/runtime are asserted. The first run completed its UI checks but failed its final direct-helper request because the old test omitted `List.exclusions`; that request now supplies an empty exclusion list explicitly. No application protocol was weakened to accept the outdated request.

The first mount run and first light controls run stopped on Tesseract's five-second subprocess timeout, not a failed product assertion. Their failures remain recorded in the respective `...20261030a/report.json` artifacts. Mount passes on its second isolated run. The shared private acceptance runner now gives only OCR subprocesses 15 seconds; application assertions, screenshot freshness, pointer socket isolation and product latency measurements are unchanged. The complete light retry passes after that infrastructure adjustment. This does not relax the 100/500 ms search targets. Python compilation and final native execution cover the runner/test changes.

The final source suite passes **229 search tests**, four ignored (`/tmp/phase6-audit-unknown-family-tests-20261030.log`). The final current workspace passes **2,946 tests, zero failures, 38 ignored, 26 suites**, serial with configured Tesseract data (`/tmp/phase6-audit-storage-family-workspace-20261030.log`). Release build, targeted no-dependency Clippy, formatting and whitespace checks pass (`/tmp/phase6-audit-storage-family-release-20261030.log`, `/tmp/phase6-audit-storage-family-clippy-20261030.log`); existing project warnings remain. The subsequent changes are Python acceptance/documentation only. Architecture comparison against `phase6-local-index-storage-audit-20261030` adds 91 measured lines and two functions, with no new excessive-complexity function, clone group or cycle. Counts remain 1,471 clone groups, 15 cycles and maximum CCN 195. Aggregate remains REGRESSION from necessary added policy/test functionality and existing modified-file warnings; analyzer claims of resolved unchanged chokepoints/cycles are not treated as improvements.

**An additional format gap remains open, with actual-helper proof:** `/tmp/phase6-audit-format-gaps-20261030a/report.json` shows a normal `module.ts` loses its TypeScript classification in the pinned inventory and consequently returns no searchable text. The ordinary GUI inventory sniffs TypeScript, while descriptor-relative inventory deliberately disables that path sniff and currently supplies no guarded replacement. Binary `recording.ts` also receives video treatment, appropriately for its data. The next implementation must disambiguate bounded bytes only after ordinary-root/pinned-source admission, preserve revision/mount checks, and align document/video selector behavior; do not merely remove the missing source-code case from expectations. RTF also remains explicit name-only on this machine without an installed `unrtf`; its generic non-text reason and optional extractor coverage need clarification in the format audit. C9/S3 remain partial/open for these details. Source/binary/report fingerprints are retained in `/tmp/phase6-enrollment-command-audit-evidence-20261030.json`.

The [completion audit](docs/File%20Manager/phase6_audit.md) records checked input/image/enrollment obligations and these concrete remaining items. Answer-column, storage/runtime and representative evidence review, the demonstrated TypeScript gap, and common-keyword latency remain open. Audio sample similarity is still excluded. Changes remain unstaged/uncommitted; personal config and the retained original stash are untouched.

## Pinned TypeScript classification checkpoint (2026-10-30)

Fixed the demonstrated search-inventory gap where ordinary `.ts` source code was dispatched as video and had no searchable text. Search inventory still does not run the browser's path-based sniff before admission. After policy/exclusion checks and ordinary-source pinning, ambiguous `.ts`/`.mts`/`.cts` files now use the same pure text-byte classifier as browser previews, reading at most 8 KiB and rechecking source/root identity and revision afterward. Failed probes make inventory incomplete rather than silently guessing a content type. This adds bounded header reads for these ambiguous suffixes during reconciliation; it is not a wholly metadata-only scan. It does not execute code or resolve imports. Document selectors share the reader's text support, including RST/ORG, while binary ambiguous suffixes cannot enter plain-text extraction.

A changed subtype retires the affected object's ready state. Source-digest reuse, checkpoint dispatch and cached literal text all distinguish the decoder route. Existing incompatible legacy cursors fail closed, while compatible canonical input vectors remain available for reuse after fresh preparation. There is no global extraction-version bump or forced re-extraction of unrelated files. Component tests cover text/binary ts/mts/cts/TS, literal and full extraction, changed revision/exclusion admission, unchanged-ready invalidation, retained vectors and digest/cursor/literal-cache incompatibility.

The unchanged owned fixture before/after helper reports are `/tmp/phase6-audit-format-gaps-20261030a/report.json` and `/tmp/phase6-pinned-typescript-helper-20261030.json`: source code now has complete text and binary transport-like bytes remain non-text. The actual private Weston/Niri run `/tmp/clip-typescript-search-dark-20261030d/report.json` passes six captured observations: four literal document matches with no index/runtime/model installation, one real MPEG transport-stream video match, then explicit enrollment and semantic matches. Its `indexed-formats.json` records five ready files, four text vectors and one video-frame image vector. Earlier diagnostic attempts a/b expected all rich rows to fit simultaneously; c also used an undersized stream detected by FFmpeg as raw MPEG without duration. The final test scrolls to the offscreen row and validates a real 1-second MPEG-TS fixture before relying on its indexing behavior. No product assertion or source-safety restriction was removed.

Validation: 232 search tests plus ten text-strip tests pass. The final serial workspace run passes **2,949 tests, zero failures, 38 ignored, 26 suites** (`/tmp/phase6-pinned-typescript-workspace-20261030.log`); the optimized Files build and targeted Clippy complete, with existing warnings. Formatting, Python compilation and whitespace checks pass. Architecture comparison against `phase6-pinned-typescript-20261030` shows +237 NLOC, +10 functions, one fewer clone group (1471 to 1470), unchanged max CCN 195 and 15 dependency cycles. Shared preparation/test-install helpers remove repeated setup. The tool's aggregate regression classification still reports older modified-file clones and unstable ordering of unchanged cycle members; this checkpoint introduces no new clone group, high-complexity function or cycle. The complete measurement is `/tmp/phase6-pinned-typescript-architecture-20261030.json`.

RTF remains a real open extraction gap in the declared scope. The prior observation about absent `unrtf` was not a diagnosis of an implemented optional dependency: the search reader currently has no RTF extractor at all. It returns name-only with an explicit unsupported text-extractor reason. Existing shared native document readers are candidates for safe reuse; they must be evaluated rather than removing RTF from the plan. The native TypeScript scenario retains RTF during literal coverage checks, then moves it outside the enrolled fixture to keep the supported-format dispatch check independent. C9 is now checked; S3 remains partial for RTF and the other outstanding format/state audit. Q/A/storage/runtime verification and the known common-keyword latency misses remain open. No commit has been made.

## Native passive RTF extraction checkpoint (2026-10-30)

Closed the declared RTF search-extraction gap by reusing the existing core `rdocx` dependency rather than adding an external extractor, launching an office renderer or writing another RTF parser. The shared `document::extract_rtf_text` accepts bytes, extracts accepted body paragraphs/table cells, and returns text plus coverage. Search supplies those bytes through the existing ordinary-source pin and revalidates after extraction. The native reader already bounds input to 64 MiB, nesting to 256 groups, runs/blocks to 100,000, cells to 50,000 and retained output to 64 MiB. Search additionally limits output to 8 MiB at a UTF-8 boundary, rejects nonzero single-part cursors, and runs RTF preparation in its owned reader with a 30-second outer timeout, 20/25-second CPU limits and the existing 2 GiB reader address-space ceiling. Cancellation retains the existing process-group kill/reap behavior. No dependency or global extraction-version change was needed.

Coverage is deliberately conservative. Native conversion diagnostics, embedded images and output truncation make RTF partial. Unsupported headers/footnotes/objects/fields are not quietly treated as searched. Field instructions, external links and embedded objects are data, not execution requests; this path does not fetch resources, evaluate fields, render images or execute macros. Simple RTF can supply complete text for the generic Q/A path, while incomplete RTF returns Unknown / partial before inference. This is bounded body-text support, not a claim of full visual RTF fidelity. The document selector now uses the same reader-support predicate without a separate RTF-only family exception.

Reconciliation specifically retries existing RTF records whose previous outcome was `unsupported` / `name-only: extractor unavailable`. Other formats, already complete RTF, and RTF already rejected for the source-size budget keep their state; compatible canonical vectors remain available. Tests cover uppercase suffixes and prove the retried record becomes reusable after successful publication. This targeted transition avoids forcing an entire catalog to regenerate embeddings for one newly supported format.

Validation evidence:

- Three core extraction tests cover formatting boundaries, Windows-1252 and Unicode surrogate pairs, UTF-8 output truncation, skipped headers/fields/objects/images, malformed/truncated binary groups and excessive nesting. A reader integration test exercises full and literal extraction, partial coverage, identity replacement, cursor rejection and oversize source admission. The public API's runnable example passes in the workspace doctests; `cargo doc -p little-clip-core --no-deps` completes and its rendered contract/error/example sections were inspected.
- `/tmp/phase6-rtf-helper-20261030a/report-final.json` records four syscall-traced cases on the final executable. They prove complete versus partial text, no reads of a named external file canary, no network socket access, no child-program execution, and the actual 20/25-second CPU limits. Both literal/full modes use the same native text extraction.
- `/tmp/clip-rtf-documents-dark-20261030a/report.json` passes eleven native observations across guarded DOCX/ODT/PPTX/XLSX/HTML/RTF/Unicode/code/PDF paths. The owned corpus now has eleven indexed files/fourteen vectors, with ten literal/semantic matches; its two RTF files independently have complete and partial coverage. Literal searches before enrollment create no index/runtime/models, skipped field/header text stays absent, compatible indexed text is reused, and a paused source edit falls back to fresh extraction.
- `/tmp/clip-rtf-partial-answer-dark-20261030b/report.json` proves actual Q/A abstention for incomplete RTF without installing/loading models or creating an index. Its retained scenario is `/tmp/phase6-rtf-partial-qa-scenario-20261030/acceptance.py`; diagnostic attempt a failed only because OCR read the initial filename's lowercase l as uppercase L. Attempt b uses `Document.rtf` and passes the same behavior assertion.
- The extended real-model labelled answer test remains **failed/unverified** (`/tmp/clip-rtf-labelled-answer-dark-20261030a/report.json`), timing out on its initial text examples before reaching the new RTF cases. A direct pinned CUDA model-load probe confirms `cudaMalloc failed: out of memory` for an 8,062.67 MiB allocation (`/tmp/phase6-rtf-answer-cuda-load-20261030.json`); the UI process was observed falling back to CPU. No unrelated application was stopped, model swapped, expected answer weakened or timeout enlarged. Successful typed RTF dates and mixed valid/partial sorting still need a real-model run when resources permit. The script retains those assertions for the later rerun.

The broader workspace run passes **2,955 tests, zero failures, 38 ignored, 26 suites** (`/tmp/phase6-rtf-workspace-20261030.log`), including the new rustdoc example. After tightening the final RTF operation limits, all **234 search tests** pass again and the final release/helper resource traces pass. Targeted Clippy completes with the existing warnings; formatting, Python compilation and whitespace checks pass. Architecture comparison against `phase6-rtf-extraction-20261030` adds 212 NLOC and six functions (one production API plus tests), with unchanged 1,470 clone groups, 15 cycles and max CCN 195. The aggregate regression label reports existing modified-file clone warnings; there is no new clone group, excessive-complexity function or dependency cycle. Full measurement: `/tmp/phase6-rtf-architecture-20261030.json`.

S3 now records the TypeScript/RTF extraction fixes with bounded proof while leaving the broader modality/state audit open. A13 distinguishes passed partial-answer abstention from the unsuccessful real-model run. Q/A, storage/runtime and common-keyword performance audit work remains. These changes are uncommitted, and the original stash and personal configuration remain untouched.

## Overlapping membership and scoped watch-failure checkpoint (2026-10-30)

The reconciliation audit found a concrete coverage-recovery problem. `Monitor::watch` previously treated every helper error as an uncertain watch installation, closed the shared inotify instance and invalidated every registered root. A definite permission failure before registration therefore caused global invalidations and repeated scans, rather than leaving unrelated watched branches useful. The before-change native run (`/tmp/clip-membership-audit-dark-20261030a/report.json`) enrolled the child and enclosing parent and verified their shared canonical vectors, but timed out waiting for their permission failures to settle. The pre-change executable is retained as `/tmp/phase6-watch-registration-before-20261030`. A separate catalog sampling artifact explicitly identifies that its samples were collected after the failed scenario's `finally` block restored permissions; those samples are not evidence of the chmod-0 interval.

Reader registration replies now distinguish `Ready(wd)` from `Unavailable(reason)`, where the latter guarantees the inotify instance was not changed. Pre-registration root/path admission failures and a failed `inotify_add_watch` return that definite outcome. The monitor reports the affected root's error/backoff but retains other watches. Transport failure, a lost reply, invalid reply shape, or failed post-install validation remains uncertain and still retires the complete instance, invalidates coverage and schedules reconciliation. Post-install rollback can affect an existing shared watch descriptor, so it intentionally remains on the conservative path. This uses typed helper replies, not error-message/locale matching. The internal protocol is not persisted and both ends are the same application binary; there is no catalog/model migration or new daemon.

Extended the existing store retention test to give the same hard-link paths parent and child memberships, fail a scan, retire one owner's subtree, then remove that owner while retaining the remaining paths and vector. New monitor tests prove shared descriptor events route to each owner, removing the enclosing owner retains the child registration, and definite errors preserve coverage while uncertain errors reset it. The private scenario `scripts/file_manager_membership_acceptance.py` reuses the existing runner/search/setup helpers and performs read-only SQLite assertions in consistent transactions. Its three natural phases cover enrollment, failed/recovered inventory and owner retirement; assertions preserve the actual query/source/index behavior rather than changing the catalog from the test.

The fixed native run `/tmp/clip-membership-audit-dark-20261030b/report.json` passes nine captured observations and records catalog snapshots:

- Two explicitly enrolled overlapping roots share three paths, five memberships and two vectors. The two hard-link names share one file identity/vector, and the parent/child memberships are independent.
- While the shared folder is unreadable, both roots report errors, all three paths/five memberships/two canonical vectors remain byte-identical, the four affected memberships are unverified, and the healthy branch remains verified and searchable.
- Restoring permissions returns both roots to ready with all five memberships verified. Removing one actual hard-link name retires its two memberships but preserves the other name and unchanged canonical vectors.
- Removing the enclosing enrollment leaves the child path/vector. A subsequent child edit is detected automatically and becomes searchable, proving the surviving watch still works.
- Removing the final owner clears generated paths/memberships/vectors. The post-run SQLite check also finds zero files, directory-coverage records, source sessions, digest caches, checkpoints and FTS rows (`derived-cleanup.json`). The script retains these cleanup assertions for future runs; the added SQL checks were independently executed against the completed run, without replaying UI actions or mutating its catalog. The independent source is unchanged and the deliberately edited surviving file remains on disk.

The source audit confirms that incomplete/skipped directory inventories fail before observation writes; ordinary unseen-path retirement follows completed inventory plus a fresh guarded absence check within the captured root/mount. An absence-check failure retains the record. Explicit exclusions/root removal are separately authorized retirements. Cancellation cannot authorize completion of an unfinished read; already completed, independently checked per-directory work need not be rolled back. S12 is now checked at these retirement boundaries. E3 retains its separate unsupported-record audit; S11 still needs existing-catalog startup and genuinely stalled-I/O/child-lifecycle verification. The coordinator makes synchronous helper calls, so the passing permission-error case must not be used to claim good hung-root query latency. S14 only closes this last-owner cleanup case, not all continuous-churn/history/scratch bounds.

Validation: **236 search tests** and the full serial workspace pass (**2,957 passed, zero failures, 38 ignored, 26 suites**; `/tmp/phase6-watch-registration-workspace-20261030.log`). The optimized Files build and targeted Clippy complete with existing warnings; Python compilation and whitespace checks pass. Architecture comparison against `phase6-membership-audit-20261030` adds 274 NLOC/eleven functions, predominantly regression coverage; the production fix against the later watch baseline adds 51 NLOC/two functions. Clone groups remain 1,470, cycles 15 and max CCN 195; no new excessive-complexity function is reported. Existing modified-file clone warnings keep the aggregate tool classification at regression. Full measurement: `/tmp/phase6-membership-watch-architecture-20261030.json`.

The model Q/A CUDA OOM and common-keyword latency misses remain open, together with the rest of the full completion audit. No commit has been made; personal configuration, unrelated work and the retained original stash are preserved.


## Asynchronous inventory and parent-owned readers checkpoint (2026-10-30)

A controlled before-change native test held only an internal directory inventory helper for an unrelated enrolled root. The healthy root could not complete its query within five seconds (`/tmp/clip-stalled-reader-before-dark-20261030a/report.json`). Directory inventories now run on the existing bounded task mechanism with two private source-job slots, one pending or retiring job per root. Commands and other runnable work proceed while a directory waits. A cancelled worker retains its global and root admission until actual terminal completion; timeout/cancellation cannot repeatedly replace a still-running child. Foreground verification has separate admission so a healthy query is not forced behind these background slots. This is not a claim that the entire application has only two processes.

The coordinator remains the sole catalog/model owner. Source workers only return replies. Acceptance requires the exact root, generation, path and SHA-256 signature of the complete serialized request. Observed source events cancel affected pending/ready inventories before invalidation is applied. Root removal, policy changes, recovery and shutdown retire the appropriate jobs. Skipped/incomplete inventories still fail before observation writes and cannot authorize missing-file retirement. Two pool tests exercise held workers, per-root/global admission, retirement until actual completion and rejection of mismatched late replies.

An additional before-change crash run found a held helper surviving SIGKILL of Files (`/tmp/clip-stalled-reader-crash-before-dark-20261030a/report.json`). Core now exposes opt-in Linux parent-death ownership, arming SIGKILL before exec and rejecting a parent-exit race. The creating parent thread must outlive the child; ordinary cancellation/reaping remains the owner's responsibility. Search reader processes, their managed read-only document/media tools and shared PDF read tools use it. Native video playback reuses the same guard, replacing its equivalent local implementation. The default remains off, preserving the existing completion semantics of accepted mutations. This does not prove lifecycle coverage for every other model/setup helper or arbitrary third-party grandchildren.

Final native evidence on the current optimized executable:

- `/tmp/clip-stalled-reader-light-20261030a/report.json`: six observations pass; healthy query UI completion is 62.354307 ms with the unrelated helper still alive and its release gate closed. Removing that root kills/reaps the helper and the healthy root remains searchable. The earlier intermediate async executable also passed at 100.312915 ms, but is not the final build.
- `/tmp/clip-stalled-reader-crash-dark-20261030a/report.json`: four observations pass; the private application exits from SIGKILL (-9), the reader dies and is reaped while the release gate remains closed. Reaping after application death belongs to the surviving system owner, not the dead application.
- `/tmp/clip-async-inventory-membership-dark-20261030a/report.json`: nine observations pass, retaining overlap/hard-link memberships and canonical vectors through permission failure/recovery, owner removal and surviving-watch edits. The scenario now directly asserts final derived-record cleanup.
- `/tmp/clip-parent-owned-video-dark-20261030b/report.json`: video advancement/pause, replay, returning to the folder and application-close cleanup pass. Attempt a stopped before playback because its old y>=300 filename locator rejected the visible filename at y=290. The locator now permits the actual list header boundary (y>=250); playback assertions remain unchanged.

The injected wait is a narrowly scoped, test-only LD_PRELOAD fixture targeting the internal reader and exact owned directory. It does not stall the browser or other roots and always releases during cleanup. It models a user-space stall, not an uninterruptible kernel I/O state. These tiny fixture timings do not meet or replace the separate 150k-vector/p95 performance obligations. Startup probes, watch registration, fingerprints, extraction, absence/publication checks, model calls and large inventory application still have synchronous coordinator work; S11 remains partial.

Validation: 238 search tests and the full serial workspace pass (2,960 passed, zero failures, 38 ignored, 26 suites). Optimized build, formatting and core API documentation pass; targeted Clippy completes with existing warnings. The new public API's rendered lifecycle/error contract was inspected. Architecture comparison against `phase6-stalled-reader-20261030` adds 421 NLOC, 25 functions and three files, with clone groups unchanged at 1,470, cycles unchanged at 15 and no new excessive-complexity function. The tool flags existing modified-file clones and a higher score on the already-existing shared acceptance-runner chokepoint: supporting intentional process death adds an exit-code parameter and a live-process check to that runner instead of duplicating compositor lifecycle code. That small test-only coupling is intentional. Full comparison: `/tmp/phase6-async-inventory-architecture-20261030.json`.

The real-model Q/A CUDA OOM, common-keyword latency misses and the remaining completion-audit rows stay open. No commit has been made.


### Model and extraction launch follow-up

The model worker already installed its own Linux parent-death signal in Python, but only after interpreter startup and imports. Its Rust launch now uses the same core pre-exec guard as the source reader, closing that earlier startup interval. The existing Python owner check remains as defense in depth. The byte-only extraction-plan helper also opts into pre-exec ownership; its existing process group, three-second deadline, seccomp policy and input/output limits remain intact. Setup/build subprocesses are not covered by this change and remain part of the lifecycle audit.

The existing explicit-provider startup test now queries the inherited parent-death signal in its fake Python worker, which never arms a guard itself; it observes SIGKILL on Linux. This checks the actual Model::start launch path while retaining provider-compatibility validation. All 238 search tests pass again, including cancellation/reaping and isolated extraction security checks. Architecture comparison against `phase6-helper-launch-20261030` adds seven NLOC with no new function or file, unchanged duplication percentage and maximum CCN; it retains existing modified-file clone warnings. Full comparison: `/tmp/phase6-helper-launch-architecture-20261030.json`. The preceding 2,960-test workspace result belongs to the inventory/video checkpoint; the follow-up is separately validated below.


The follow-up optimized executable also passes the held-reader/crash native scenario (`/tmp/clip-helper-launch-crash-dark-20261030a/report.json`): the healthy query completes in 127.183115 ms while the unrelated reader is held, then SIGKILL of Files kills/reaps that reader without releasing its gate. This remains a tiny controlled-stall fixture, not the large-catalog latency gate. The optimized build completes with the six existing library warnings (`/tmp/phase6-helper-launch-release-20261030.log`).

The follow-up serial workspace rerun also passes **2,960 tests, zero failures, 38 ignored across 26 suites** (`/tmp/phase6-helper-launch-workspace-20261030.log`). This confirms the current model/extraction launch changes as well as the earlier inventory changes. Formatting and whitespace checks pass. The goal remains active and incomplete.


## Asynchronous file-source stages checkpoint (2026-10-30)

The existing asynchronous directory inventories did not remove file-read stalls: a new private before-change fixture held the Fingerprint payload read for Slow.txt, and a previously indexed Healthy folder could not complete its query within five seconds (`/tmp/clip-async-file-before-dark-20261030a/report.json`; `held-query.json` records no completion while the reader is alive and the gate remains closed). The test-only preload now supports exact file/operation targeting in addition to directory enumeration. It observes the reader request prefix and only holds reads by the internal reader of the owned fixture file; it does not alter file bytes or stall the ordinary browser.

Fingerprinting, extraction, checkpoint verification and final publication verification now use the same two-slot reader pool. Every FileWork retains the root scan generation. A pending fingerprint leaves source_check pending; resume is attempted only after that fingerprint completes. Pending extraction preserves existing segments, text/media cursors and resume hashes. A saved-checkpoint mismatch explicitly discards that checkpoint and resets the cursor; waiting for a reply no longer goes through that destructive branch. Checkpoint writes wait for their fresh verification without duplicating the next segment. Final publication has an explicit continuation flag, so resuming it cannot repeat decoding or inference. Publication still requires a valid source verification reply and the existing revision/pipeline guards. Source workers never mutate the catalog.

The same per-root/global bounds, exact request signature and generation ownership, event cancellation and retirement-until-terminal-completion apply to these file stages. Shared FileWork initialization centralizes fresh continuation state; shared file-read dispatch binds every stage to its owning root/generation/path and refuses shutdown dispatch. The implementation adds no executor, daemon, database schema or extraction/model migration. Startup probes, watch registration, absence checks, model loading/inference and large inventory application remain synchronous and are still open under S11.

All 238 search tests pass after the final simplification, including shutdown preserving committed checkpoints and pending state. Architecture comparison against `phase6-async-files-20261030` adds 72 NLOC and four functions with no added files, clone groups or excessive-complexity functions. Clone groups remain 1,470 and max CCN remains 195; existing modified-file clone warnings retain the aggregate regression label. Full comparison: `/tmp/phase6-async-files-architecture-20261030.json`. Native stall/restart and final workspace outcomes are recorded below after completion.

It is retained as a fixture-coverage failure, not a product pass or failure. Attempt b uses an RTF body read in the intended process. Neither run proves kernel D-state behavior, independently held verification or Python-decoder payload reads, or large-catalog latency.
- `/tmp/clip-async-files-checkpoint-dark-20261030a/report.json`: closes with eight committed segments, restarts through verified checkpoint reuse, and completes all 392 passages covering 1,663,052 source bytes. ## Asynchronous absence checks and directory retirement checkpoint (2026-10-30)

The next controlled before-change run held an internal Absent request after its JSON input arrived and before source validation. The healthy root's query could not finish within five seconds while that helper remained alive and unreleased (`/tmp/clip-async-absence-before-dark-20261030a/report.json`, `held-query.json`). This is a test-only request-delivery hold, not a kernel filesystem stall. The preload matches the exact internal-reader command, operation and owned fixture path from a bounded 4 KiB request prefix; all waits release during scenario cleanup. Ordinary file readers, decoder children and the host compositor are outside the gate.

Directory retirement now uses the same bounded source-reader pool as inventory/file jobs. Work::Directory retains an optional remaining-candidate queue to resume the completed inventory's retirement phase. It processes one candidate per coordinator step. Waiting preserves the candidate, root/generation and parent path; the directory is not relisted, its watch is not registered again and coverage is not completed while that reply is pending. Only a fresh Reply::Verified retires an ordinary unseen path. A failed or unexpected reply retains that record; current policy exclusions still explicitly retire generated references without reading the source. Cancellation, request/generation matching and retirement-until-worker-completion retain the existing pool rules. Helpers never write the catalog.

The 100,000-entry work-queue budget now reserves one entry for the directory continuation before admitting more child work. This avoids accidentally raising the existing bound by adding a completion task after a full inventory. Policy admission runs before either directory phase, and the single coordinator remains the only writer. There is no new executor, schema, request protocol, daemon or model/extraction migration.

## Asynchronous watch registration checkpoint (2026-10-30)

The before-change private run `/tmp/clip-async-watch-before-dark-20261031a/report.json` held a Watch helper after request delivery and before source validation. A previously indexed healthy folder could not complete its query within five seconds while the unrelated helper was held. As with the preceding absence fixture, this is controlled user-space waiting, not kernel D-state evidence.

Watch registration now uses the same two-slot source-reader pool. The monitor supplies an Arc<File> for its actual inotify instance, which is retained by both the pending job and its running worker. reader::watch still constructs the inherited owned descriptor; serialized parent descriptor numbers are only part of request identity, never reopened as borrowed descriptors. Root/generation/path/request matching and job-kind matching reject mismatched continuations. Only the coordinator updates watch ownership or the catalog.

Completions are accepted only for the same Arc instance currently owned by the monitor. A late success, failure or cancellation from an older instance cannot modify or retire a replacement. Cancellation/clear returns potentially affected instance handles to the coordinator: an unconsumed installed or unknown outcome retires its matching instance and invalidates coverage. A finished typed Unavailable reply guarantees no installation and preserves healthy coverage even if cancellation arrives before ordinary consumption. Retired workers retain both process admission and the descriptor until actual completion. There is no new executor or helper budget.

Retiring an instance during event cancellation can create another coverage gap. The coordinator drains those generated invalidations without another I/O poll, cancels all affected jobs and updates catalog freshness before admitting replacement work. This prevents a stale gap from repeatedly cancelling newly registered watches. The existing periodic reconciliation/backoff remains the fallback when registration is unavailable.

Every pending registration reserves watch capacity, including a familiar pathname that may refer to a replacement inode. At the 8,192-watch limit, existing watches remain active and new registration attempts yield a monitoring warning plus periodic reconciliation, rather than exceeding the budget. Pinned retired instances can remain alive until their bounded workers actually finish; this does not claim that cancellation can instantly release a kernel instance held by a genuinely uninterruptible child. The two source-job slots bound such outstanding ownership, and replacements never bypass occupied retirement slots.

Four new tests cover retired-instance late success/error/cancellation, descriptor lifetime through worker retirement, pending watch-budget reservation, and definite no-install outcomes during cancellation/clear. All 242 search tests pass. The final architecture comparison against phase6-async-watch-20261030 adds 228 NLOC and 17 functions, with unchanged 1,470 clone groups, 15 cycles and max CCN 195. No new excessive-complexity function is reported; existing modified-file clone warnings retain the aggregate regression label. Full comparison: `/tmp/phase6-async-watch-architecture-20261031.json`. Native, release and full-workspace results are recorded below after completion.


Final optimized native validation passes 25 observations across four private scenarios:

- `/tmp/clip-async-watch-dark-20261031a/report.json`: the healthy query completes in 89.653204 ms while a Watch helper for another root remains alive and unreleased. Removing that root kills/reaps the helper. Because an abandoned registration may have modified the shared instance, the scenario explicitly waits for fresh healthy coverage before verifying the retained search and single-root catalog.
- `/tmp/clip-async-watch-crash-light-20261031a/report.json`: the healthy query completes in 63.634901 ms while the Watch helper is held. SIGKILL of the private application terminates that reader, which is reaped by the surviving system owner while its release gate stays closed.
- `/tmp/clip-async-watch-membership-dark-20261031a/report.json`: all nine overlap/permission/recovery/retirement observations pass. Definite unavailable registrations retain usable healthy coverage, shared vectors survive errors and recovery unchanged, removing one owner preserves the other owner's watch, and final removal clears derived records.
- `/tmp/clip-async-watch-fingerprint-dark-20261031a/report.json`: ordinary read cancellation also passes after the shared dispatch/cancellation refactor. Its healthy query completes in 32.665875 ms while the fingerprint reader is held, and removing that root preserves the healthy watch without forcing the watch-specific reconciliation path.

These are bounded user-space request/read holds on tiny owned fixtures under concurrent validation, not actual uninterruptible I/O, large-catalog latency or universal helper-lifecycle proof. The controlled before-change Watch run timed out at five seconds; its failed report is retained separately. Startup probes, model loading/inference, large inventory application and directory-replacement/delta identity still need work/audit, alongside Q/A, storage/runtime/packaging and the measured common-keyword performance gap.

## Asynchronous enrollment and startup probes (2026-10-30)

The controlled before-change native run `/tmp/clip-async-probe-before-dark-20261030a/report.json` confirms that a held Probe request blocks the coordinator: a query against an already indexed healthy root cannot complete within five seconds. The fixture holds request delivery before source capture; it is an owned user-space gate, not a kernel D-state simulation.

Enrollment, startup restoration, rebuilds and automatic full reconciliations now enqueue Probe work on the existing two-slot reader pool. The coordinator no longer invokes source capture synchronously in start_scan. A successful reply still passes monitor/source identity, self-cache and current exclusion checks before catalog enrollment/scan admission; only then is a fresh explicit enrollment persisted. Hard exclusions and user exclusions reject before queueing where the path itself suffices; the existing guarded helper still enforces resolved aliases and source identity. No source payload, model inference or catalog mutation occurs in the probe helper.

Known-root probes use the stored positive root ID and unverify memberships immediately while retaining canonical vectors. An exact SQL root-ID getter reuses the lookup previously embedded in unavailable(), avoiding materializing every root for each queued probe. Invalidation preserves the disabled state of an unconfigured cached root. Fresh requests have session-only negative tickets, shown through the existing yellow checking state and removable through the normal menu; no catalog/config root is created until validation succeeds. Removing a fresh pending ticket cancels it without creating a DB. Duplicate requests merge explicit enrollment intent without submitting another probe. Root IDs in validated backups remain positive; temporary tickets never enter the storage/backup format.

Probes remain runnable while normal indexing is paused/capacity-blocked or yielding to answers, so validating/removing a pending enrollment does not require starting embeddings. Reader retirement now also retains its exact path. A new ticket for the same path cannot bypass a pending or retiring helper, even after cancel/re-enroll; unrelated paths retain the other slot. Only actual worker completion releases admission. Existing request signatures, cancellation, parent-death ownership and single-writer publication are retained; no new executor, daemon, model or schema is introduced.

Five focused tests cover pending paused/capacity-blocked enrollment with no catalog/config side effects and removal, queue deduplication, known-root membership invalidation with byte-identical vectors, hard/user exclusions before queueing, and pending/retired same-path fencing. All 249 search tests pass. The existing test Worker constructor is shared instead of cloning its setup.

Current optimized native evidence (33 observations across five scenarios):

- `/tmp/clip-async-probe-dark-20261030a/report.json`: fresh pending enrollment is yellow/removable; the healthy query completes in 38.21 ms while the probe remains alive and unreleased. Removing the pending root kills/reaps its helper and leaves only the healthy catalog/search.
- `/tmp/clip-async-probe-restart-light-20261030a/report.json`: both folders are first indexed, Files closes normally, then a new process restores the same config/catalog with one Probe held. The healthy root reconciles and its query completes in 107.45 ms while that restored probe remains held. Removing the stored slow enrollment reaps the child and preserves healthy data.
- `/tmp/clip-async-probe-crash-light-20261030a/report.json`: the healthy query completes in 34.68 ms with the fresh probe held; SIGKILL of the private Files process kills and reaps that reader before its gate is released.
- `/tmp/clip-async-probe-membership-dark-20261030a/report.json`: all nine overlap/permission observations pass, preserving canonical vectors through failed reads and recovery, the surviving child watch after parent removal, and final cleanup.
- `/tmp/clip-async-probe-directory-dark-20261030a/report.json`: all eight automatic directory rename/replacement/nested-move/root-replacement observations pass. Moves reuse embeddings, replacement children receive working watches, and replacing the root remains offline until explicit re-enrollment.

These tiny-fixture timings under concurrent validation are not a large-catalog latency claim. Startup SQL/catalog costs, large inventory application, model-call stalls, common-keyword latency and the remaining storage/runtime audit are still open. The test fixture now supports --stage probe and --restart, using only the private compositor and owned fixture/config/cache paths. Its healthy-query proof is shared between lifecycle scenarios; final architecture comparison adds 254 NLOC and thirteen functions, with no new excessive-complexity function, clone group or dependency cycle (1,470 clone groups, 15 cycles, max CCN 195). Existing modified-file clone warnings keep the aggregate regression label. Final full-workspace/lint results are recorded after their terminal completion below. No commit has been made.
