## Interruptible background model work (2026-10-30)

Moving source reads into the bounded helper pool did not fix coordinator waits inside background inference. An owned real-model fixture reproduces the old release blocking a healthy-folder semantic query for more than ten seconds while an unrelated vector request is held (`/tmp/clip-model-preemption-before-dark-20261030/report.json`). This gate pauses delivery of a synthetic request in user space; it is not a GPU/kernel uninterruptible-hang simulation.

Accepted commands now cancel the current background model load/inference via the existing model's cancellation and kill/reap lifecycle. A small shared admission gate counts successfully queued commands and prevents background cancellation from being cleared before dispatch. Failed queue admission does not interrupt or add phantom work. Shutdown permanently closes the gate, preventing a race that could clear cancellation after service drop. The same coordinator still owns catalog writes and one model; there is no extra resident model, executor, model provider, backend or quantization change. This deliberately trades one interrupted inference/load for interactive responsiveness; repeated edits can postpone background progress until the user stops issuing commands.

Interrupted work retains its existing pending/checkpoint state and is retried rather than marked failed, published as partial or discarded. Text cursors advance only after a complete vector is obtained; media retains its prepared bytes. A cancelled directory model load requeues its directory without completing its scan. Completed checkpoints/segments and all source verification remain in place. Focused tests deliberately stall loading, text vector generation after tokenization, and media inference; each child is reaped before control returns, and retries preserve the initial passage/prepared media. Admission rejection and shutdown-reset fencing are also tested. Genuine failures still follow the normal error path.

Both optimized private native runs pass four observations each (`/tmp/clip-model-preemption-dark-20261030/report.json`, `/tmp/clip-model-preemption-light-20261030/report.json`). The healthy query completes inside the application in 2,129.71 ms / 2,094.85 ms (2.78 / 2.73 seconds including test submission), with the old child reaped and its gate unreleased. The interrupted multi-passage file then finishes with complete coverage, its first canary passage still searchable, and unchanged source bytes. These tiny controlled timings include model restart, not a large-catalog p95 claim. The test-only preload shares its existing reader gate/lifecycle code instead of adding a second library.

Foreground model requests can still occupy the coordinator; source-helper admission, background-model preemption and the remaining SQL/large-inventory/common-keyword/runtime audits are separate obligations. Full Phase 6 is not complete. Final workspace/lint/source-probe regression and architecture evidence follow after terminal completion.

The reviewed production source passes the full workspace again: **2,976 passed, zero failures, 38 ignored across 26 suites**, including **254 search tests** (four ignored benchmarks). Report: `/tmp/phase6-model-preemption-workspace-final-20261030.log`. The release build completes in 37.82 seconds with six existing warnings. The shared preload's existing source gate also passes a real startup/restart regression (`/tmp/clip-model-preemption-probe-restart-light-20261030/report.json`, six observations): the healthy query takes 107.01 ms while the unrelated Probe remains alive and unreleased; removal kills/reaps it. Together the new model-preemption and source-probe regressions supply fourteen native observations.

Architecture against `phase6-model-preemption-20261030` adds 309 NLOC, thirteen functions and one file. Clone groups stay at 1,470, cycles at 15 and max CCN at 195, with no new excessive-complexity function. The scheduler reuses next_work admission; test-only read/model gates share process holding and argument inspection. Existing modified-file clone warnings retain the aggregate regression label. Report: `/tmp/phase6-model-preemption-architecture-20261030.json`. No commit was made.

Final targeted Clippy completes with the same 264 existing warnings (`/tmp/phase6-model-preemption-clippy-20261030.log`). Python acceptance scripts compile and whitespace checks pass. The current release, source, native reports, architecture and Rust logs are hashed in `/tmp/phase6-model-preemption-evidence-20261030.json`; known-profile evidence remains separately recorded in `/tmp/phase6-private-profiles-evidence-20261030.json`.


## Foreground read cancellation and temporary-answer lifetime (2026-10-30)

A real held-answer fixture reproduces a remaining foreground stall: an accepted preferences save cannot complete while the previous answer request is held. The authoritative before report is `/tmp/clip-answer-preemption-before-dark-20261030b/report.json`: the save times out, the child remains alive, and the user-space gate remains closed. The earlier run exposed a harness predicate that indexed an absent `[file_manager]` table before the first asynchronous preferences save; that predicate now tolerates an absent section and waits for the actual persisted value. The earlier harness error remains retained, not counted as product evidence.

The existing Dispatch admission lock now owns the interactive cancellation token as well as background cancellation. After successful queue admission, a new control command cancels the previous read-only search/answer; rejected commands do not cancel it. Token publication and queue admission are ordered under the same lock, eliminating a gap between sending a command and publishing its cancellation token. The separate Service query-token mutex is removed. Backup/restore/clear/recovery and explicit setup retain the independent service-shutdown token; ordinary file writes retain their separate owners. New commands do not cancel accepted mutations.

A cancelled answer completion now invokes the existing Columns::cancel_pending mechanism before taking its pending cell. This requeues that file, retains completed cells and excludes the cancellation result from typed values. New request IDs reject late replies. Answer dispatch waits while accepted index maintenance is pending/in progress. The existing folder-return test now also covers externally interrupted cells and retry; the model fixture additionally covers foreground interruption and kill/reap before returning to an accepted maintenance request. Separate admission coverage proves a rejected control leaves the active interactive token intact. This extends cancellation rather than adding a second coordinator, model or worker pool.

Initial real Q8 dark/light native runs pass preference saving, child reaping with the gate unreleased, automatic retry, typed values, folder return and unchanged sources/no implicit index. Expanded runs additionally close and reopen the real window and check application logs, configuration and non-model search data for the synthetic question, canary and answer strings. Their terminal evidence and full validation results follow below. The source remains a generic question-answer path; neither model/quantization nor prompt/evidence validation changes.

Expanded final dark/light runs each pass eight observations (`/tmp/clip-answer-preemption-{dark,light}-20261030b/report.json`). The held child is reaped with its gate unreleased, the previous completed value stays present, and the interrupted second value retries successfully. Preference-control timings are 7.90/5.68 seconds including menu opening, OCR captures, clicks and closing the settings UI; these are not inference-only latency measurements. Closing/reopening the actual application discards columns. Both old/new application logs, saved config and non-model search data contain none of the synthetic question, canary or exact answer strings. No index is created and source hashes stay unchanged. Observed folder-key reuse does not establish remote server/source lifetime continuity; that remains in the freshness audit.

The current full workspace passes **2,978 tests, zero failures and 38 ignored across 26 suites**, including **256 search tests** (`/tmp/phase6-foreground-admission-workspace-20261030.log`). The preliminary search run passed 257 before consolidating duplicated setup into the existing folder-return test; no scenario was dropped. The accepted-maintenance scenario initially passed backup integrity/content checks but missed its confirmation below the viewport; after scrolling to that confirmation it completed clear/persistent-pause/reindex SQL/source/hash assertions but missed the final vector count above/below the current viewport. ## Bounded large-directory catalog application (2026-10-30)

The prior release processes a whole completed directory inventory before returning to commands. An owned 100,000-file fixture with a catalog-local test trigger adding deterministic CPU work to each membership insertion reproduces the stall: a healthy-folder query times out after five seconds while admission advances from 20 to 9,725 paths (`/tmp/clip-inventory-slices-before-dark-20261030/report.json`, `inventory-responsiveness.json`). The trigger exists only in the private fixture catalog. This is a controlled slow-SQL scheduling test, not a natural-filesystem or full-crawl benchmark.

The existing reader worker now computes the complete inventory metadata reservation off the coordinator. The anonymous-file transport and its 128-MiB cap remain unchanged; the in-process/private helper reply carries the reservation beside the directory. Each acquired inventory becomes an owned continuation. Applying it admits at most 64 entries or eight milliseconds of cooperative work, then yields to commands and queued file work; a single database transaction may exceed that time budget. File work between slices prevents a large flat directory from filling the entire work queue before any files can complete. The remaining inventory bytes are tracked and rechecked against current storage capacity before each later slice, since intervening file/vector work may consume space. The original complete-inventory preflight still occurs before its first row write.

Retirement/completion waits for every entry to be handled; partially applied inventories cannot mark coverage complete. Invalidation uses the existing owned queue rather than a second epoch map: directory notifications mark the exact buffered parent, and watch/source coverage loss marks the root's buffered inventories. Unrelated parents remain usable. Restarting an invalidated continuation uses a fresh catalog generation so entries observed in its abandoned prefix can still become removal candidates. Pending file work for that parent is replaced, preserving already committed checkpoints/canonical inputs and avoiding duplicate pending jobs; other directories' work survives. Final absence and content-publication verification remain unchanged. No schema/model/pipeline-format change or extra executor is introduced.

Component coverage includes a 100,000-entry transport/reservation round trip, a 514-path synthetic catalog application proving cancellation, per-slice cardinality and no premature completion, and directory/root invalidation proving old-buffer rejection, new-generation removal candidates, unaffected-parent isolation and replacement of only the abandoned parent's pending files. Initial testing caught a missing cancellation-to-invalidation connection; it was fixed and the passing rerun retained. The final mechanism marks the owned continuation directly, avoiding the earlier separate epoch bookkeeping. Two new lifecycle tests extend the existing search suite.

Current optimized native dark/light runs each pass five observations (`/tmp/clip-inventory-slices-{dark,light}-20261030/report.json`). Healthy query times are 54.54/38.21 ms inside the application while only 52/49 of 100,000 large-root paths have been admitted. Each run records six incomplete metadata batches, at most nine rows per batch; the largest observed batch times are 9.19/8.74 ms under the synthetic per-row SQL cost. These are small scheduling observations, not p95 or whole-index completion claims. Both runs remove the partial index and preserve all 100,000 source files and the healthy root's vector. The private trigger is dropped before removal.

The same release also passes document/content/RTF/Unicode/office/sparse-PDF/indexed-text freshness regression (eleven observations, `/tmp/clip-inventory-slices-documents-dark-20261030/report.json`) and automatic directory rename/replacement/nested move/root replacement regression (eight observations, `/tmp/clip-inventory-slices-directory-light-20261030/report.json`). These confirm the shared reader reply and continuation paths retain their existing behavior. SQL startup, bulk completion/pruning, remaining runtime/source-lifetime audits and measured common-keyword latency remain separate open work. Full workspace/lint/architecture evidence is recorded after terminal completion below.

Review additionally identified that completed reader replies can remain buffered between metadata slices, so counting active source helpers alone would not bound large retained inventories across many enrolled roots. The existing Readers owner now grants at most two inventory permits (the same fixed admission count as its helpers). A permit travels from the read task to its delivered inventory and queued application, and is released only when that data is dropped or application finishes. Retiring tasks retain it until actual return. It is an in-process Arc ownership token, excluded from helper serialization; helpers cannot supply admission ownership. New directory reads wait when the budget is full, while file reads, pending watch completion, metadata continuations and explicit probe/control requests keep their existing paths. This bounds retained inventory count rather than creating a new thread pool. At most two large inventories may be pending/delivered/applying together; additional roots wait for a buffer, as they already wait for bounded reader admission.

A focused test covers a retired held worker plus a delivered buffer exhausting admission, rejected new inventory requests without launching a source read, continued ordinary-file reader availability, permit release after retirement/data drop, and omission from serialization. The current search suite passes 259 tests (four ignored benchmarks) after this review. Earlier successful native/workspace reports describe the preceding slice implementation; final reruns below cover the added ownership limit.

The reviewed buffer-limited release builds successfully in 40.26 seconds with six existing warnings (`/tmp/phase6-inventory-slices-release-final-20261030.log`). Both final 100,000-file native runs pass five observations (`/tmp/clip-inventory-slices-{dark,light}-20261030b/report.json`): 49.66/71.07-ms healthy queries while only 36/37 large-root paths are admitted. The incomplete batches have at most nine/eight rows, with largest observed batch times 12.88/8.64 ms under concurrent validation and the synthetic per-row SQL cost; the eight-millisecond limit is cooperative, not a deadline that interrupts a transaction or scheduler delay. Partial removal still preserves all sources and the healthy vector. The final automatic directory reconciliation regression passes all eight observations (`/tmp/clip-inventory-slices-directory-light-20261030b/report.json`). The earlier successful document/wire/content run remains applicable to that unchanged path; the follow-up permit field is excluded from serialization.

The final full workspace passes **2,981 tests, zero failures and 38 ignored across 26 suites**, including **259 search tests** (`/tmp/phase6-inventory-slices-workspace-final-20261030.log`). Architecture against `phase6-inventory-slices-20261030` adds 516 NLOC, fifteen functions and one file; clone groups remain 1,470, cycles 15 and max CCN 195. No new excessive-complexity function or clone group remains. The original excessive combined application function was split into continuation lifecycle and bounded row observation, and the native timing probe now reuses measure_ui_queries with an explicit timeout rather than duplicating its query loop. Existing modified-file clone warnings retain the aggregate regression label. Final lint and evidence details follow after completion. No commit was made.

Final targeted Clippy completes with the same 264 existing warnings (`/tmp/phase6-inventory-slices-clippy-20261030.log`); Python compilation and whitespace checks pass. Architecture details are `/tmp/phase6-inventory-slices-architecture-20261030.json`. Current source, release, before/after native reports and validation logs are hashed in `/tmp/phase6-inventory-slices-evidence-20261030.json`. This checkpoint does not close SQL-startup/bulk-completion, general runtime/packaging/source-freshness or common-keyword performance obligations. The original stash and unrelated work remain untouched.


## Name-only content admission and unsupported question states (2026-10-30)

An owned syscall-traced before run proves the indexer reads six MiB across six unsupported fixtures while storing no embeddings for them: 96 payload reads from ZIP, EXE, STL, MIDI, SQLite and unknown files (`/tmp/clip-name-only-before-dark-20261030/name-only-checks.json`). The previous generic fingerprint stage hashes those inputs before extraction reports that no extractor exists. This is unnecessary I/O, not evidence that those bytes reached a model.

A shared positive content-format predicate now reuses the existing text/TypeScript, photo, audio and video classification. Unsupported FileWork starts in its final-publication stage with an explicit name-only reason and no digest, decoder state or segments. It still uses the existing asynchronous final source validation before any record is published; source identity, root/mount/policy and replacement checks are retained. This classification belongs to FileWork construction, avoiding repeated stage decisions during ticking. Supported formats keep their existing fingerprint/reuse/extract paths and byte budgets. No content extractor, model, schema version or quantization changes. The root's existing model-pipeline identification may still load the embedding runtime; the claim is no unsupported file payload/model input, not no model resident in a mixed folder.

Review also exposed that unsupported Q/A could reach header inspection before falling back to an unsupported document result. The UI and service now share admission for readable documents/still images only; archives and unknown formats cannot enter header planning. Supported audio/video/MIDI keep their separate unsupported question state. Explicit user exclusions return Excluded, preserving the distinction from unsupported modality and non-applicability. Tests cover the positive supported-format set, TypeScript versus transport routing, name-only final-validation ownership, no fingerprint replacement, unsupported questions without reader/model admission, and exclusion precedence. The expanded check initially had an incorrect test-module namespace; that compile error was corrected before validation.

Both optimized private dark/light native runs pass four observations (`/tmp/clip-name-only-{dark,light}-20261030/report.json`). The catalog has seven path records and one vector: six explicit unsupported/name-only records plus supported text. The filename remains retrievable, literal search does not invent content, unsupported question cells render Unsupported without launching an answer model, and fixture hashes remain unchanged. Source-reader-only syscall traces record **zero unsupported payload reads or mappings**, with one supported-source body read as a positive control. Ordinary browser previews have their separate source access and are not covered by this no-read claim. A real Q8 YAML/TOML/generic question regression also passes all five observations (`/tmp/clip-name-only-frontmatter-light-20261030/report.json`).

A separate native storage diagnostic finds a concrete remaining defect: supported `a.txt` and unsupported `b.data` hardlinks share the same file-state record, and the unsupported representation overwrites the text result, leaving zero vectors (`/tmp/clip-mixed-alias-dark-20261030/{report,mixed-alias}.json`). The replay script is `/tmp/phase6-mixed-alias-repro.py`. This does not establish when the defect was introduced; the current source and native catalog establish that it exists. S1 is now explicitly Open. The next storage change must preserve physical membership/path identity while separating decoder representation state; canonical input reuse should remain shared where inputs actually match. A single preferred suffix would hide the more general incompatibility between different supported decoders and is not a sufficient final design. Phase 6 remains active.

Final validation passes **2,983 workspace tests, zero failures and 38 ignored across 26 suites**, including **261 search tests** (`/tmp/phase6-name-only-workspace-20261030.log`). The final optimized build completes in 37.83 seconds with six existing warnings; formatting and targeted Clippy complete with the same 264 existing warnings. Python compilation and whitespace checks pass. Architecture against `phase6-name-only-admission-20261030` adds 236 NLOC, seven functions and one file, with unchanged 1,470 clone groups, 15 cycles and max CCN 195. No new excessive-complexity function remains: initial stage selection now belongs to FileWork construction rather than adding another branch to every indexing tick. Architecture report: `/tmp/phase6-name-only-architecture-20261030.json`; source/release/test/native/mixed-alias evidence hashes: `/tmp/phase6-name-only-evidence-20261030.json`. The failing mixed-alias native diagnostic remains authoritative despite the broader green suite. No commit was made.


## Decoder-specific hardlink state and schema 6 (2026-10-30)

The prior native failure is now fixed. Physical identity alone cannot determine decoding: one inode can be reached through supported text, HTML, transport-stream/TypeScript, or unsupported aliases. Choosing one preferred suffix would still conflate incompatible supported routes. The existing source-format key now also identifies each file-state record, alongside device/inode/source scope. It records the folded suffix, positive content-admission class (including extensionless README versus unknown names), a version marker, and the existing TypeScript dispatch flag. Compatible aliases still share publications. Exact canonical input/pipeline/modality vector reuse remains independent of source path and representation. No model or quantization changed.

Schema 6 transactionally rebuilds the file identity constraint for existing v1-v5 catalogs. It preserves old IDs, segment/vector bytes and path memberships, but marks ambiguous legacy file state pending under an empty representation sentinel. Fresh observations bind each path to its actual decoder representation. Legacy canonical vectors remain available for exact prepared-input reuse until reconciliation drains and unreferenced records are pruned. The optional whole-source cache and old checkpoint formats cannot authorize extraction reuse across the key change; some legacy preparation must therefore restart conservatively. Offline ambiguous records cannot be treated as freshly ready before reobservation. This one-time conservatism is preferable to guessing a decoder from the last alias's stored metadata. No filesystem content is read by the SQL migration itself.

Current literal-cache reuse also requires the matching decoder representation. Native backup import accepts versions 4, 5 and 6 using explicit historical column lists; old imports are pending, restored roots remain disabled/unverified, and current nonempty representation bytes must match their metadata. The bounded checkpoint-format allowance is 512 bytes to accommodate the versioned key plus a full filesystem suffix. Tests preserve exact v5 canonical vector bytes/reuse and foreign-key enforcement, check v4/v5 imports, reject mismatched backup representations, isolate TypeScript routes and extensionless content admission, and verify both compatible and mixed-format hardlinks.

The optimized release builds in 37.89 seconds with six existing warnings (`/tmp/phase6-alias-representations-release-20261030.log`). Both private native theme runs pass four observations (`/tmp/clip-alias-representations-dark-20261030c/report.json`, `/tmp/clip-alias-representations-light-20261030/report.json`): four aliases share one inode; two compatible text names share one record; HTML and name-only aliases have independent records; two supported vectors survive name-only publication, a genuine v5-shaped catalog upgrade, and removal of the unsupported alias. Source bytes remain unchanged until that deliberate owned-fixture removal. The first two native attempts passed catalog assertions but expected an off-viewport last row in screenshots; those failed reports remain retained, and the test now explicitly collapses the Shelf after each launch.

A separate replay copies the original failed schema-5 catalog with zero vectors into a new private runner while retaining its original source paths. The new release repairs it to one supported text vector and independent unsupported state, without changing source hashes (`/tmp/clip-alias-original-repaired-20261030/{report,repair-results}.json`). The original failed catalog/report remains untouched; replay is `/tmp/phase6-repair-original-alias.py`. This verifies repair of the actual observed failure rather than only a synthetic downgrade.

The current full workspace passes **2,987 tests, zero failures, 38 ignored across 26 suites**, including **265 search tests** (`/tmp/phase6-alias-representations-workspace-20261030.log`, `/tmp/phase6-alias-representations-search-20261030c.log`). New-schema regressions pass the four-observation syscall-traced name-only scenario and thirteen-observation backup/clear/persistent-pause/explicit-resume scenario (`/tmp/clip-alias-name-only-dark-20261030/report.json`, `/tmp/clip-alias-maintenance-light-20261030/report.json`). Architecture against `phase6-alias-representations-20261030` adds 330 NLOC, eight functions and one native test file, with unchanged 1,470 clone groups, 15 cycles and max CCN 195. An initially duplicated test assertion was strengthened to compare decoded canonical vectors and the added clone was eliminated. The aggregate regression label still includes old modified-file clone warnings and nondeterministically ordered members of an unchanged Python worker cycle. No production dependency or mechanism was added to address metrics. Report: `/tmp/phase6-alias-representations-architecture-20261030.json`. Final targeted Clippy matches the prior counts exactly: 589 library, 648 library-test and 264 binary-test warnings (`/tmp/phase6-alias-representations-clippy-final-20261030.log`). Its first pass found one new collapsible-if warning; collapsing the backup predicate removes it without changing the acceptance condition, and all nine restore tests pass after that cleanup (`/tmp/phase6-alias-representations-restore-final-20261030.log`). The full workspace/native runs above precede only this equivalent lint cleanup. The hardlink defect is resolved for the tested cases; the broader storage, runtime, packaging, source-freshness and common-keyword performance obligations remain open. No commit was made.

The final optimized release after the equivalent validator cleanup also builds successfully (37.98 seconds, six existing warnings; `/tmp/phase6-alias-representations-release-final-20261030.log`). Final formatting, Python compilation and whitespace checks pass. Source/release, architecture/test logs, native reports/screenshots/syscall traces and the retained failed reproductions are hashed in `/tmp/phase6-alias-representations-evidence-20261030.json`; the builder is `/tmp/phase6-alias-evidence.py`. The native release evidence above differs from this final source only by that equivalent validated predicate cleanup.


## Root summary cost and read-only SQL interruption (2026-10-30)

The next responsiveness review found that every root-status refresh scanned all segment records once per root, with a correlated membership check per segment. An isolated probe of the actual exported 100,000-file/150,000-vector schema measures median 229.17 ms for one root and 2,049.43 ms for nine roots (the enclosing root plus eight 1,000-file scopes). Starting from each root's memberships instead measures 213.43/236.97 ms with exactly the same count rows. Adding a derived partial index on segment file IDs where vector is non-NULL, and using the existing covering file-state index, reduces the nine-root median to 126.75 ms and p95 to 128.78 ms across 21 samples. This is isolated root-summary SQL, not retrieval, source verification or native UI latency. The probe checks the measured SQL against the production query text. Artifacts are under `.fitness/evidence/phase6-root-count-20261030/`.

The production query now counts unique root/path memberships directly and deduplicates eligible file IDs before counting their non-NULL vectors through the partial index. Multiple compatible hardlink paths must not multiply vector counts; different decoder representations remain distinct records under schema 6. Unverified memberships and non-ready records cannot supply current vector counts. No cached count authority, bitmap, extra connection/thread or model is introduced. The new index is a derived lookup accelerator recreated by the existing schema/open/restore path, without a schema or backup-format change. It also lets ordinary SQLite non-NULL vector counts avoid vector payload pages when the planner selects it.

Background root summaries now reuse the existing command-admission gate and scoped SQLite cancellation guard. An accepted command interrupts an active read; queued commands defer admission. Failed/superseded reads leave the previous summary intact and retain a dirty flag. Only dirty summaries retry after the existing 250-ms throttle, including when no file work remains. The guard ends before any later write. The main loop's existing reader polling, scheduling and idle-resource housekeeping now has a cohesive background-tick owner, rather than adding another responsibility to command waiting/dispatch. Accepted backup/restore/clear and ordinary file writes keep their prior completion behavior. SQL opening/migration/policy cleanup, scan-completion and pruning still require their own audit; this change does not make every SQL operation interruptible.

All 266 search tests pass before the background-tick extraction, including actual command admission during an owned expensive root read, retained snapshot/no spurious error, a write after cancellation while the token is still set, queued-read deferral and a successful retry. Existing hardlink tests now also check overlapping-root counts, compatible-alias deduplication, NULL-vector exclusion and invalidation/reverification. The derived-index recreation test includes the new index. The production release after the equivalent background-tick extraction builds successfully in 37.88 seconds with six existing warnings. Architecture against `phase6-lexical-query-20261030` adds 148 NLOC and three functions, with no new excessive-complexity function, clone group or cycle. Existing modified-file clone warnings retain the aggregate regression label. Final workspace/lint/native results follow after completion.

The current unchanged retrieval component baseline still misses the initial common-keyword target: 100,000 files/150,000 vectors, 21 samples, warm p95 exact-name 10.71 ms, rare keyword 10.09 ms, common keyword 174.39 ms, default hybrid 248.09 ms and broad hybrid 291.46 ms. Synthetic vectors exclude inference, source verification, explanations and UI publication. Root-summary improvements do not by themselves close that lexical target or the retained native hybrid gap. The report and log are retained beside the SQL probe. Native before/after runs use the private compositor and ordinary owned source fixtures; the first attempted persistent output path exceeded Wayland's 108-byte Unix-socket limit before Files started. That failed runner report/log remains retained; subsequent runner paths are shorter. The original large stash and unrelated work remain untouched. No commit was made.

The actual native baseline fails before producing a warm-latency series. Metadata reconciliation takes 449.207 seconds; immediately after the ready observation, the five-minute backstop starts generation 3 and unverifies all 100,000 memberships. The exact filename appears in the preliminary lexical pass, but the full verified pass returns zero entries. Pipeline and all canonical vectors remain unchanged. The failed runner report, source/binary manifest, ready inventory report, query logs and catalog summary are preserved in `.fitness/evidence/phase6-root-count-native-before-final-20261030/`. The before desktop executable is retained with its manifest-matching SHA-256 under `.fitness/evidence/phase6-root-count-20261030/`. This is a correctness failure under long reconciliation, not a valid native latency comparison.

Review identifies the backstop anchor as the beginning of a full scan. The monitor now tracks a full scan in flight and advances the backstop anchor only on its successful completion. Ordinary directory-delta completions do not postpone the full backstop. Pending events/gaps retain their separate due/full state, and failures retain the prior retry backoff. The new deterministic clock-state test covers a scan lasting longer than the period, delta completion without deadline postponement, the eventual periodic full refresh, and overflow arriving during the scan. It does not sleep for five minutes. The root-read cancellation test additionally marks the end of its expensive SQL computation and requires that the marker remain unset, proving interruption before completion rather than merely discarding a completed read. Final current-source workspace and native validation are in progress.

## Rejected FTS owner cache and model idle release (2026-10-31)

An isolated SQL prototype stored the canonical file ID in an FTS5 `UNINDEXED` column using `contentless_unindexed`, eliminating the explicit segment-ID lookup from the lexical query. On the exported 100,000-file/150,000-segment fixture, its 21 alternating samples returned identical path/file/score/segment rows but were slower: median 187.35 ms versus 170.27 ms, p95 234.05 ms versus 179.42 ms. The callback used a frozen final 500-path threshold and Python's SQLite 3.54.0, rather than the production evolving ranking window and bundled SQLite 3.49.1. These are diagnostic SQL timings, not native acceptance measurements. The experiment does not justify an FTS cache migration or extra stored owner metadata; no production schema/query change was adopted. The report is `.fitness/evidence/phase6-root-count-20261030/fts-owner-probe.json`.

The runtime audit found an independent model-lifetime defect: the five-minute idle check also required an empty work queue, paused indexing or a capacity block. A long metadata-only queue could therefore retain an unused embedding or answer model indefinitely. Idle release now uses the actual model last-use time and the existing keep-loaded preference, independently of queued index metadata. It runs between coordinator steps, after synchronous model work returns, and reuses the existing helper kill/reap destructor. No inference timeout or active request cancellation is introduced.

A new regression fails on the old predicate and passes with the fix. It starts owned lightweight helper processes for both model roles, proves recent-use retention and the keep-loaded override, ages the last-use timestamp past five minutes without sleeping, then verifies model/backend release, child reaping and preservation of queued inventory. All 268 search tests pass (four ignored). This is deterministic coordinator/helper lifecycle evidence, not a five-minute native GPU-memory observation. Remaining R5 backend/OOM/retry and native lifecycle acceptance still require their separate evidence audit.

The complete workspace after this change passes 2,990 tests, zero failures and 38 ignored across 26 suites. Release build, formatting and whitespace checks pass; targeted Clippy matches the previous 589 library, 648 library-test and 264 binary-test warnings. Four Python runtime/provisioning tests pass. The current private light-theme search-settings run passes eleven observations, including persistent pause/keep-loaded preferences, acknowledged reopening, protected-scope rejection, filename fallback and no implicit catalog/enrollment. Its arbitrary form input now uses the existing private clipboard helper, as required by the acceptance runner instructions. The separate idle scenario reuses the scale test's owned-child lookup and canonical-vector fingerprint rather than introducing application instrumentation or changing the production timeout. Durable logs and architecture reports are under `.fitness/evidence/phase6-model-idle-20261031/`; native idle results follow after the real interval finishes.

The same R5 inspection identifies actual remaining implementation work: an unchanged `failed` file is not considered complete by `observe_object`, so every reconciliation resubmits it. There is no per-file one/five/30-minute automatic retry ledger followed by a terminal wait-for-change/manual-retry state. `Model::start_preferred` bounds backend attempts during loading, but inference-time OOM recovery is separate and must not be inferred from those load tests. The audit keeps both obligations open. No commit was made.

The real five-minute native dark-theme idle scenario now passes three observations. The embedding helper is observed on CUDA with a 1,432-MiB allocation (one sample, not a peak guarantee), remains alive during the interval, and is absent after 299.11 seconds of post-capture observation. Capture/OCR consumed the start of the production five-minute interval. A subsequent query starts a different CUDA helper and returns the expected file in 1,026 ms; the prior resident query took 40.86 ms. These are single-query lifecycle observations, not latency percentiles. Source bytes and the canonical vector fingerprint are unchanged, and closing the window reaps the successor. The scenario does not establish native answer-role idle behavior, keep-loaded GPU residency or OOM recovery. Reports/screenshots, lifecycle JSON and progress samples are retained under `.fitness/evidence/phase6-model-idle-20261031/idle-dark/`, alongside the eleven-observation settings run. The source helper tests cover both roles and the keep-loaded override. Architecture against `phase6-model-idle-20261031` adds one test file and no clone group or dependency cycle; the initial duplicated child lookup was replaced by reuse of the existing scale scenario's mechanism. The goal remains active with the explicit retry/OOM, performance, SQL scheduling, Q/A/freshness and packaging obligations still open.

## Bounded per-file failure retries (2026-10-31)

Failed content extraction previously ran again on every reconciliation indefinitely. The index now keeps small persistent operational retry records keyed by the canonical file representation, with guarded source revision, model pipeline and extraction version. An initial failure permits three more attempts after one, five and thirty minutes; the fourth failure waits for a changed source/pipeline or explicit Rebuild index. Duplicate alias/completion failures during the same wait cannot consume another attempt. Existing untyped decoder/model errors receive this finite budget rather than guessing permanent/transient classifications from error-message text. Cancelled/shutdown requests keep their previous checkpoint and do not consume an attempt.

The existing directory monitor supplies retry wakeups. Waiting files hold no worker, FileWork buffer or runnable queue slot. An earlier event/deadline is preserved; a busy root still cannot acquire an overlapping scan. Reconciliation checks each canonical deadline before content work and re-arms later waits as needed, so unrelated directory events do not bypass backoff. The deadlines are persistent wall-clock seconds, translated to bounded monotonic directory waits; they are not promised exact deadlines while paused, busy or offline. Normal source validation and all policy admission remain required at execution.

Partial failures preserve successfully published vectors but retain their retry record and partial root status. A successful complete attempt clears the retry record. Manual rebuild atomically marks affected failed/partial records pending before clearing their retry budget, preventing a ready partial publication from being skipped. Source revision, pipeline or extraction-version changes discard stale retry state; late failures cannot mark a different observed revision/pipeline failed. Orphan retirement cascades retry cleanup with the canonical file. The operational table is created on current-schema opening without changing schema-6 canonical records/backup columns. Backup import creates it empty and does not trust/import historical retry decisions. The shared schema builder now also creates the optional digest-cache table for restored catalogs, fixing the analogous missing-table path before any resumed source reuse.

The initial 273-search-test and 2,995-workspace-test runs pass; the final run includes the manual partial-reset and restored-table checks. Components cover actual SQL deadlines across reopen, all four failure counts, terminal state, manual reset, stale-failure rejection, source/pipeline change, retained partial vectors, canonical-file retirement, directory scheduling/busy-root deferral and command-preemption preservation. Native retry/recovery validation is in progress; longer timers and terminal/reopen behavior are component scope until separately observed natively. Inference-time backend OOM fallback remains separate required work. No commit was made.

The final workspace run passes **2,995 tests, zero failures, 38 ignored across 26 suites**, including **273 search tests**. The current private dark-theme retry scenario passes three observations: initial invalid-PNG failure at generation 1, an unrelated directory event at generation 2 retaining failure count 1, the automatic retry at generation 3 after 60.27 seconds with a 300-second next deadline, manual rebuild resetting the count at generation 4, and valid-image replacement completing generation 5 with one vector and no retry state. The final CUDA query returns the recovered file in 24.39 ms (one lifecycle sample, not a performance percentile). This observes the production one-minute timer without modifying stored retry deadlines; longer tiers and terminal/reopen behavior are component checks. The source fixture is ordinary local tmpfs data under the private runner output; the earlier native idle fixture uses the requested type-testing directory.

The final release builds in 37.94 seconds with the six existing warnings. The first release/lint pass exposed an unused import left by moving failure SQL into the store module; its removal restores the existing warning baseline and does not change behavior. Native validation precedes only that import cleanup, and the workspace run precedes only that cleanup and moving the unchanged test module to its conventional position. Architecture against `phase6-file-retries-20261031` adds 469 NLOC, two files and sixteen functions, with unchanged 1,470 clone groups, fifteen dependency cycles and max CCN 195; no new excessive-complexity function is reported. The initial duplicated native fixture setup was replaced with the existing `media_fixture_directory` mechanism. Final logs/reports, sources and artifact hashes are retained under `.fitness/evidence/phase6-file-retries-20261031/`. Inference-time backend OOM recovery and the broader performance, SQL scheduling, Q/A/freshness and packaging gates remain open. No commit was made.

## Inference backend recovery and orderly shutdown (2026-10-31)

Model loading already tried CUDA, Vulkan and CPU, but a compute failure after loading only retired the helper and failed the file/query. Runtime recovery now owns the same prepared request until success or its original deadline, reaps the failed helper before loading the next backend, and tries each remaining backend at most once. A successful replacement stays resident for later requests; the Index popup displays its actual device and fallback history. Both embedding and answering roles share this path. New helpers must report the identical model pipeline before any replay, preventing a runtime/model upgrade during recovery from mixing representations. Initial backend loading also shares one 120-second deadline instead of granting a fresh 120 seconds to each backend. Recovery does not extend warm-query/indexing/answer request deadlines; when no time remains, existing lexical/error handling applies.

`inference_errors.py` translates fixed statuses/templates from the pinned native ABI into a structured backend category. Fatal `llama_process` statuses below -1, a post-tokenization media encoder failure and answer decode status 2 can request recovery. Unexpected helper exit or pipe failure is also recoverable. Invalid input, malformed/oversized replies, grammar errors, cancellation, timeouts and ordinary context/output abstentions do not initiate backend replay. This adapter is not a heuristic over user/model text. The numerical shims, weights, framing and embedding pipeline are unchanged, so this error-policy change does not invalidate stored embeddings. The answer-worker source fingerprint includes the shared adapter for ephemeral Q/A provenance. Keep the adapter beside the Python helpers in installed bundles.

The first new crash test exposed EOF being converted to an ordinary error inside the reader thread. Typed transport failures now retain their category through delivery, while malformed reply parsing remains a request error. All 276 search tests and 77 Python helper tests then pass, and the first full workspace passes 2,998 tests. Components exercise compute failure, abrupt helper exit, failed intermediate-backend load, sticky success, exact replayed input, one attempt per backend, reaping before replacement, CPU terminal failure, pipeline mismatch, cancellation and original-deadline expiry.

The first private native run successfully recovers embedding and Q/A requests onto real Vulkan helpers after a controlled native status -2, but its test driver then clicks the red Index button in the unindexed Q/A folder while trying to inspect status. That explicitly enrolls the folder and correctly trips the unchanged-catalog assertion. The corrected driver inspects model status from the already enrolled folder and preserves the original failed report. Its second run passes all recovery/catalog/source checks but fails the strict helper-gone-on-close assertion: the coordinator thread was detached, allowing application exit before its answer child was fully reaped. The child is subsequently absent, but parent-death cleanup after exit does not satisfy the planned orderly shutdown ownership.

The service now retains its coordinator join handle. Graceful close closes admission and cancels read tokens, allows already admitted controls/maintenance to settle, then stops the worker. The worker retires its model and drains cancelled source readers before finishing. The application polls completion asynchronously in a short Closing view and only joins an already finished thread; file writes and unsaved-edit guards run before entering this closing state. Forced termination still relies on the existing parent-death signal. Two additional component tests verify accepted-maintenance preservation/new-admission rejection and model reaping before close approval, bringing the search suite to 278 passing tests. Final workspace/native checks after the shutdown change follow below. Architecture reports no new clone group or cycle; the existing app update function gains one necessary branch for the closing state (CCN 21 to 22). That lifecycle decision is required rather than hidden by an unrelated refactor. No commit was made.

The orderly-shutdown release passes the same strict native scenario in dark and light themes (four observations each). Both roles recover actual requests onto Vulkan after the private preload returns native compute status -2 once per CUDA role; the failed helper PIDs are gone before replacement checks, recovered backend status is visible, the successful embedding backend stays resident for the next query, the typed answer is 42 points, source hashes and canonical vectors remain unchanged, and the answer helper is already reaped when the real application exits. This injects a compute-failure status rather than allocating until GPU OOM; unexpected process exit, terminal CPU failure and deadlines remain component evidence. The first two failed native reports are retained alongside the successful runs.

Final review also finds that cancellation during the last initial backend load was previously aggregated into a generic load error, which could consume a file retry instead of preserving interrupted work. The loader now preserves cancellation/deadline identity after its final backend attempt, with an owned three-backend cancellation/reaping regression. The final workspace passes **3,001 tests, zero failures, 38 ignored across 26 suites**, including **279 search tests**; all **77 Python helper tests** and five acceptance/benchmark-driver tests pass. The final release builds in 37.78 seconds with six existing warnings. Targeted Clippy matches the prior 589 library, 648 library-test and 264 binary-test warnings. The final light native run uses this exact release; the passing dark run precedes only the final cancellation-edge correction, whose successful-inference/close path is unchanged. Formatting, Python compilation, strict C-fixture compilation and whitespace checks pass.

Architecture against `phase6-inference-fallback-20261031` adds 605 NLOC, four files and thirty functions, with unchanged 1,470 clone groups, fifteen cycles and max CCN 195. The app update function gains the documented required closing-state branch (21 to 22); there is no new excessive-complexity function elsewhere or added clone group. Current source/release/test/native evidence and hashes are retained under `.fitness/evidence/phase6-inference-fallback-20261031/`. The runtime lifecycle/retry/recovery row is now checked for its stated evidence scope. The goal remains active: retrieval latency, remaining SQL scheduling, Q/A/freshness/action coverage, resource/packaging/quality consolidation and the final requirement audit remain open. No commit was made.

The installed pinned llama.cpp source also confirms the native allocation route directly: graph allocation failure becomes `GGML_STATUS_ALLOC_FAILED`, and both encode/decode map that status to -2; the CUDA fatal-error handler terminates through `GGML_ABORT`. The structured status adapter and unexpected-exit recovery therefore cover those documented allocation-failure routes without forcing GPU exhaustion. The source revision, relevant excerpts and file hashes are retained in `native-failure-routing.json` beside the native reports.

## Interruptible scan completion and bounded idle retirement (2026-10-31)

The completion audit found two more catalog-wide operations running directly between coordinator commands: the root-completion classification embedded in an UPDATE, and full orphan pruning after reconciliation. Completion now uses the existing dispatch admission and scoped SQLite cancellation guard for its read-only classification, then removes the guard before the small accepted root-state update. An interrupted root remains queued, with round-robin retry, and remains busy to the directory monitor until completion is published. Queued controls defer admission without rotating that queue. Explicit error completion skips the classification read.

Normal reconciliation no longer runs global pruning after each directory-completion marker or work batch. Once admitted work and pending root completions drain, an idle cursor seeks through paths and canonical file IDs in pages of 64. Candidate selection is interruptible. Each accepted atomic write rechecks ownership and retires at most 64 orphan paths or 64 segments from one orphan file; committed vector removals update the in-memory count without a global recount. Healthy candidate pages perform no writes, preserving cache epochs. Newly admitted source work cancels this sweep and restarts it only after that batch drains, retaining directory-move reuse. Partial file retirement revokes whole-source digest/checkpoint/retry claims and marks the representation pending before removing its first segments, so a reappearing source must reprepare rather than trust a truncated complete publication. Exact matching passage vectors remain reusable.

Confirmed path/subtree retirement now uses exact-path and slash-delimited binary key ranges instead of whole-catalog substring scans; independent overlapping memberships still retain their ownership. Actual subtree writes remain proportional to the retired subtree and atomic. Explicit root removal, policy changes and maintenance retain their existing accepted write semantics; this change does not claim all startup/maintenance SQL is bounded or cancellable.

Six new regressions cover actual queued-command interruption of completion and prune selection, removal of the read handler before subsequent writes, bounded segment retirement and committed counts, candidate ownership changes, reappearance after partial retirement, and checkpoint replacement revoking an old whole-source digest. The last invariant already existed in `append_segments`; the test guards it rather than introducing a new production fix. `store/publication.rs` is byte-identical to the previous inference-recovery checkpoint after a redundant invalidation was removed during review.

The final full workspace passes **3,007 tests, zero failures and 38 ignored across 26 suites**, including **285 search tests**. Release compilation succeeds in 40.45 seconds with the six existing warnings. Formatting passes; targeted Clippy retains the existing 589 library / 648 library-test / 264 binary-test warnings. Both private dark/light directory scenarios pass eight observations each, including automatic rename, replacement, watched edits, nested import/move/removal, unchanged vectors and generated counts on moves, stale-path search exclusion and explicit re-enrollment for a replaced root. A current 100,000-file / 150,000-vector native scale scenario passes five observations: startup generation 2 verifies every membership in 22.34 seconds, every query completes, and the canonical vector fingerprint is unchanged. Its single warm samples are correctness observations, not p95 acceptance; the prior percentile failures remain open.

Architecture against `phase6-completion-sql-20261031` adds 561 NLOC, one file and eighteen functions, with unchanged 1,470 clone groups, fifteen cycles and maximum CCN 195. Separating path/file/segment retirement eliminates the initial new CCN-26 transaction hotspot; no newly excessive-complexity function remains. The remaining new-file clone match is six lines of standard SQLite row collection and storage-error mapping, not duplicated retirement logic. An extra abstraction for that boilerplate would not improve domain ownership. Other modified-file clone flags match existing broad worktree changes; the aggregate tool still reports REGRESSION. Evidence is retained under `.fitness/evidence/phase6-completion-sql-20261031/`. Startup policy/schema work, explicit large mutations, performance targets and the remaining acceptance audit stay open. No commit was made.

### Renewed cache and backup/restore acceptance

Current-source inspection and operational evidence now close R6 and S16 for their stated boundaries. The release component run at 125,000 files / 187,500 vectors retains 167,751 cached vectors within a conservative 522,736,640-byte reservation, scans every reference and returns the uncached final-file winner. Both default/broad semantic scans cancel at reference 169,984, after uncached rows are reached, and subsequent full scans recover identical ordered results. The three timing samples are resource/correctness evidence, not a percentile gate. Cache epochs, hard filtering, single-matrix allocation and independent idle release also pass the current full workspace.

The private light native scenario at 100,000 files / 350,000 vectors passes nine observations, including startup generation 2 fully verified in 25.492 seconds, visible checked-reference progress, two Escape cancellations and successful replacement requests. Captures show 34,816 and 36,864 checked references; neither cancelled request is accepted afterward. All canonical vectors remain unchanged. The native captures prove progress and cancellation, while the component test separately proves cancellation beyond the cached population. Neither run establishes a whole-process 512-MiB memory bound or a passed latency target.

Native backup/restore passes seventeen observations in each theme on the current release. An invalid backup leaves the active index intact; valid restore preserves exact vector/pipeline bytes and FTS, retains configured enrollment, clears source verification, persists pause and resumes explicitly. Source and backup bytes remain unchanged. Inspected import uses pinned input, a bounded helper and fresh canonical schema instead of executing supplied triggers/views/FTS; component tests prove WAL-aware export, no-overwrite installation, malformed-record rejection, current-root authority, empty-cache non-enrollment, staging cleanup and cancellation/SQLITE_FULL rollback. The restore driver now uses the existing private clipboard helper for destination text instead of physical character injection. Recovery of a corrupt active database remains a distinct explicit action with its own lifecycle audit. Reports and source hashes are retained beside the completion/pruning evidence.

## Restore policy and checkout-independent helpers (2026-10-31)

Further boundary review found that opening an existing index purged currently forbidden cached content, while importing a structurally valid backup did not. Retrieval's live policy still excluded forbidden paths, but imported private-only text/vectors could remain stored until the next reopen. The new regression reproduces a forbidden `.mozilla` reference surviving in the prepared staging catalog. Import now applies the same existing cache-only hard policy to the rebuilt staging catalog, inside the already bounded disposable helper, before integrity validation and active installation. It returns the post-purge vector count. No new policy implementation, source read or schema version is introduced. A shared public owner keeps its canonical file/vector while its forbidden alias is removed; private-only records and FTS content are removed. The supplied backup remains unchanged, and preparation failure still cannot modify the active index.

The regression checks profile, credential and repository references, both private-only and shared with a public owner, in staging and after application. All 76 storage tests pass. The full workspace passes **3,008 tests, zero failures and 38 ignored across 26 suites**, including **286 search tests**. Private native dark/light scenarios each pass ten observations: a fixture-only legacy backup loses its private-only payload and forbidden shared alias, preserves the public vector exactly, retains enrollment and persistent pause, and leaves source/backup bytes unchanged. The forbidden source paths do not exist; policy application does not require opening them. These runs are cached-data retirement checks, not forensic secure-erasure claims.

Provisioning inspection also found that its copied runtime helper set omitted the new `inference_errors.py` module. An isolated subprocess regression reproduces the resulting FileNotFoundError when starting the provisioned worker outside the checkout. The explicit setup copy now includes that dependency. The test mocks compilation and downloads, copies the real helper files, starts the copied worker with Python isolated mode from a separate directory and proves it can parse `--help` without models. All **78 Python helper tests** pass. Numerical shims, weights and embedding pipeline identity are unchanged; neither fix requires re-embedding ordinary existing files.

For native deployment validation, the current release and complete helper tree were copied under `/tmp/clip-phase6-package-20261031/`. Bubblewrap hides the original checkout with a private tmpfs mount, and a shell guard proves its Cargo.toml is absent before executing the copied acceptance runner. Both compositors, the runner, the desktop app and all its helpers run inside that mount namespace, preserving the runner's exact PID/socket ownership. The fourteen-observation light-theme run passes literal/recursive search, protected-path enrollment exclusions, real semantic indexing/retrieval, rejection of a modified cached source, generic true/false Q/A and return to embedding inference. An observed CUDA worker uses the executable-adjacent copied `search_model/worker.py` path. Source checkout files were never renamed or hidden on the host. This proves the search helper layout for these exercised paths, not packaging for every optional studio/codec.

Two failed deployment attempts remain in evidence: wrapping only the application changes its PID relative to the strict private-window owner, so the runner correctly refuses that window; running the whole private runner in the namespace fixes ownership. The next attempt reaches semantic retrieval but exposes an obsolete test assumption that the unrelated bank document must always survive the product cosine floor for a cat query. The driver now allows irrelevant fixtures to be rejected and requires any admitted distractor to rank below the relevant cat document. No production floor, scope or quality fixture was weakened. The corrected run passes all fourteen observations.

## Rejected query optimizations and refreshed precision evidence (2026-10-31)

The latency follow-up retained the exact scope/ranking/source checks and rejected several measured alternatives. On the owned 100,000-file/150,000-vector catalog, a Python SQLite diagnostic returns the identical row set but materializing eligible memberships is slower (median 297.88 ms versus 228.79 ms). Materializing only roots/sessions is effectively flat (237.97 versus 239.72 ms in its alternating run). Larger page caches and mmap are also flat in that diagnostic. These are SQL-only probes with Python's SQLite/callback overhead, not application latency measurements.

A temporary complete-vector-cache shortcut removes repeated SQLite-to-Rust presence callbacks only after every current-epoch segment has a cached vector. It passes its NULL/external-write/epoch regression but produces no consistent improvement across both semantic workloads. The before/candidate/control p95 values are 273.47/262.99/282.09 ms for the default-floor component query and 308.55/312.86/335.81 ms for the broad query. A separate NO_MUTEX experiment also fails to provide a consistent overall improvement. Both changes, including their extra state/tests, are removed; no new count/completeness rule or connection mode is retained.

The larger experiment runs lexical and semantic ranking concurrently on separate read connections, sharing the existing vector matrix and a reference-counted ownership lock. Its read-only/ownership/replaced-catalog tests pass. Native 21-sample results reject the optimization: a fresh reader per request improves the lighter cases but regresses common-keyword p95. Retaining one read-only connection restores page/statement reuse but still does not produce a useful overall win:

| Native hybrid query | Accepted single connection p95 | Parallel, fresh reader p95 | Parallel, retained reader p95 |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| Exact filename | 571.08 ms | 472.56 ms | 492.81 ms |
| Rare keyword | 497.10 ms | 464.22 ms | 506.09 ms |
| Common keyword | 687.54 ms | 773.32 ms | 676.86 ms |
| Broad meaning | 498.77 ms | 504.37 ms | 505.12 ms |

All three private dark-theme scenarios pass their five correctness observations, finish startup generation 2 with every membership verified and preserve all 150,000 canonical vectors. The prototypes retain complete filtering, natural ties, rank fusion and fresh source verification; no sample is removed. They are synthetic-vector scale fixtures with actual model/source/UI work, not quality evaluations. Parallel phase diagnostics show common-query median lexical/semantic wall times of 374.35/519.33 ms; the concurrent scans themselves slow down. This does not isolate a physical CPU/cache/locking cause. The additional connection, resident cache, thread lifecycle and ownership changes are **all removed**. Retained rejected sources/reports are under `.fitness/evidence/phase6-admission-query-20261031/`; accepted retrieval/store/vector sources match their pre-experiment SHA-256 values, and recovery has no worktree diff. The single-connection implementation remains authoritative. The common-keyword and native tail targets remain open.

The fresh accepted baseline component run has p95 12.52 ms exact filename, 11.43 ms rare keyword, 193.27 ms common keyword, 273.47 ms default-floor hybrid and 308.55 ms broad hybrid (21 samples). The later retained-binary control is 10.96/10.32/187.47/282.09/335.81 ms. It executes the original binary after source edits; its separate provenance record distinguishes that binary's original source hashes from the runner's observed newer worktree. The native baseline above is the current accepted desktop checkpoint, using the unchanged fixture exporter from the prototype test harness; exporter input/vector fingerprints are verified. No prototype result is presented as production performance.

Refreshing actual frozen-corpus inference exposes a developer-driver protocol mismatch before any source extraction: Directory replies now wrap their original fields inside `Inventory.directory`. `quality.py` and the existing frontmatter probe now unwrap that envelope and still reject nonzero skipped coverage; missing coverage fails closed. A new regression verifies the actual nested shape and rejection before Extract. The initial zero-segment/zero-query failure is retained. All **79 Python helper tests** pass. These are the only retained source changes from this follow-up; the restored optimized Rust search suite passes its original **286 tests**.

Current real CUDA Q8 preparation and production ranking pass the frozen corpus: 70 combined positive queries, 32 negatives and ten exact names. Defaults retrieve 66/70 positives (audio/image 100%, PDF/video 92.86%, text 85.71%) and return results for 3/32 negatives. The matched canonical-f16/f32 oracle loses zero recall and changes no expected-file rank; one returned order changes. Matching Q8 and BF16 inference additionally verifies the checksum-pinned weights, identical ordered request hashes and identical CUDA device. Both variants retrieve 66/70; BF16 returns 2/32 negatives. Q8 loses zero recall in both canonical and f32 comparisons, and both storage comparisons lose zero recall. Returned orders and one expected audio rank differ between models, so this is not numerical/ranking identity. All recorded precision gates pass.

The primary/validation manifest hashes remain `65cb62116a9beb64d1f082cefed239e48d46812ad818985e9d1bbea36c074061` and `88721eaca98dbfb1b4f268784271c11d7b2cc0a04b5be5c4e405b595b4f9390c`; expectations and defaults are unchanged. The known two-independent-video limitation and 3/12 Q8 validation-only negatives remain disclosed. These results do not establish population accuracy or close broader long-media coverage. Reference BF16 weights are checksum-verified under the owned type-testing reference directory, outside application models; no model default or enrollment changes. Durable reports, request hashes, vectors, manifests and source/artifact hashes are under `.fitness/evidence/phase6-quality-refresh-20261031/`. ## Native header shortcut defect resolved (2026-10-31)

The remaining A11 clipboard failure is now reproduced and fixed. The real pointer injects press and release in a single egui frame; the earlier rendered test split those events across frames. The header restored list focus immediately, then egui's later list-background interaction observed a press outside its rectangle and surrendered that focus. A content-free trace confirms a requested focus followed by no focus, disabled file-key forwarding and a text Copy event. Changing the regression to one coalesced event batch fails before the fix, matching the native behavior.

Both ordinary and answer headers now set one transient focus request. The shared browser UI applies it after dock/list interactions and before its existing focus filters/clipboard-forwarding update. This follows the existing deferred address-focus pattern; it adds no new queue, persistence, global shortcut policy or focusable control. Title editing still keeps text focus because it does not submit a header action. Temporary trace statements are removed.

Current release dark/light private native runs each pass ten observations, including ascending/descending numeric order, copied URI identity for the originally selected file after each header click, true/false columns, retained session answers after folder return and no implicitly created index. All 1,012 affected file-manager tests pass, the optimized desktop builds with six existing warnings, formatting passes, and targeted Clippy completes with the existing 1,237 library/library-test and 264 binary-test warnings. No model/embedding/storage behavior changed; previous model-quality results remain scoped evidence rather than fresh reruns. The final architecture delta is -2 NLOC, no new functions/files/clone groups/cycles, and unchanged maximum complexity; aggregate broad-worktree warnings remain pre-existing. Evidence: `.fitness/evidence/phase6-native-header-20261031/`. This closes A11 for its declared boundaries, not the remaining Phase-6 performance, freshness, resource or full-delivery gates.

## Answer revision and late-completion acceptance (2026-10-31)

Added `--source-freshness` to the existing held-answer acceptance driver, reusing its real-model gate, fixture provisioning and private compositor lifecycle. Both themes pass six observations. The test edits a held input in place while preserving inode/size/mtime (ctime changes), replaces an already answered path while preserving size/mtime (inode changes), and edits a completed source while away from its folder. Current values are recomputed after each event and on folder return; no semantic index is created. Six independent per-frame answer-column crops verify those numeric results separately from source previews; header coordinates are re-read for each frame because layout shifts after folder return. These are final refreshed-value checks, not continuous frame-by-frame nonpublication, remote cache-coherency or native remount claims.

A new Rust regression removes and recreates the same column, or changes its answer pipeline, while a request is pending. In both cases the obsolete token is cancelled, its late result cannot consume the replacement request, and only the new completion advances the count. Together with the existing mount/folder/file/navigation/completed-pipeline tests and inspected final source verification, this closes A12 at its stated identity/version and ordinary-source boundaries; remote/offline/index continuity remains separately audited. Current affected tests pass 1,013 file-manager cases including 289 search tests; Python helpers pass 79. Only acceptance code and this audit changed in this checkpoint, with no model, schema or production behavior change.

Architecture adds three test/driver functions and 101 NLOC, with unchanged files, maximum complexity and dependency cycles. One short additional clone is test-fixture setup shared with existing column scenarios; retained as explicit routing-test setup rather than adding a production abstraction or arbitrary one-line wrappers. Native setup/gating is reused from the existing driver. Evidence: `.fitness/evidence/phase6-answer-freshness-20261031/`.

## Live-result content guards and file-reference boundary (2026-10-31)

Added `--live-replacement` to the existing traced acceptance driver. Unlike the offline scenario, the indexed root remains the same available directory while one selected file is replaced at its address. Current dark/light runs each pass eight observations: the cached result can export a normal desktop file URL without reading payloads, audio-studio opening rejects the changed observation, and query refresh omits the old revision. The replacement canary stays unchanged. The original file is moved away before the trace cutoff and the replacement is created afterward, so old-descriptor reads cannot be mistaken for new-file reads at that address.

File URLs are explicitly ordinary path references, as with the Shelf/reference IPC channel; they do not capture bytes or promise that an external consumer will later resolve the same inode. Captured-content and content-consuming actions retain their own source-validation obligations. This documents the existing protocol boundary rather than claiming that clipboard export creates an immutable file snapshot. The full C16 action/preview/drag/mutation-consumption audit remains open; a single audio entry is not evidence for every studio or external handoff.

The live trace now includes read/pread/readv/preadv and file mappings, retains per-process byte cutoffs, and has an independent successful replay after process shutdown. Its descriptor matcher recognizes exact paths and char/block annotations while excluding pipes and similarly named files; a new regression fails with the former substring matcher and passes afterward. Read-only metadata opens are separate from payload reads/mappings. Four native-driver unit tests pass, Python compilation and whitespace checks pass. The final fixture uses the existing enrollment helper, eliminating a duplicated setup block. Architecture adds one test file/four acceptance-test functions and 78 NLOC, with no new clone groups/cycles or maximum-complexity increase; no application implementation/model/schema changed. Evidence: `.fitness/evidence/phase6-live-result-20261031/`.

## Early lexical rejection feasibility (2026-10-31)

A new read-only SQL probe on the owned 100,000-file/150,000-segment catalog evaluates a dominance hint before segment/path lookups, retaining the same full SQL eligibility and a later path check. Eleven alternating samples retain identical 750 postings and report median 167.85 ms baseline versus 113.92 ms early rejection. Post-path callbacks fall from 150,000 to 750; map construction costs 110.78 ms. This is a fixed tied-score threshold using Python SQLite 3.54.0, not the actual dynamic Rust Top, bundled engine, bounded cache, model or UI. The catalog remains unchanged. No application code, schema or target changed, and R8 remains open. The next bounded experiment must handle unknown IDs/aliases conservatively, share the existing generation checks, preserve matching locations and account for memory alongside the vector cache. Evidence and criteria: `.fitness/evidence/phase6-early-lexical-probe-20261031/`.


## Initial bounded lexical path-hint prototype (2026-10-31, superseded below)

A singleton-only segment-to-path hint cache can apply the existing exact Top comparison before SQLite segment/path joins. It never supplies eligibility: every surviving row still passes the original scope, membership, visibility, source and candidate checks. Unknown IDs, every alias of a multi-path file and entries beyond the memory limit fall through. Local/external catalog generations invalidate it; scoped callbacks are removed on success/error; retained paths continue contributing matching locations. It reserves 32 MiB from the existing 512 MiB working-cache allowance, reducing the vector allocation allowance to 480 MiB, and shares its idle release. No schema/model changes or new persistent data.

Matched 100k-file/150k-vector component control/prototype common-keyword p95 is 181.40/138.62 ms; the repeat prototype measures 132.76 ms. First-query cost increases from 174.00 to 234.65 ms due to hint construction. A 125k-file/187.5k-vector correctness run scans all references, including the uncached final winner, with 157,265 cached vectors and a conservative combined-cache bound below 512 MiB. All 1,016 file-manager tests pass, including ordered result and retained-evidence equality against the real no-hints alias fallback; frozen quality retains the existing gates. These are bounded claims: the corpus replay itself is too small to trigger the hints, and the component timing excludes model/source/UI work. Private dark desktop performance validation passes its five correctness observations with startup generation 2 and unchanged 150k vectors: warm exact/rare/common/broad-hybrid p95 is 483.06/473.78/633.19/501.04 ms over 21 samples each. This is an improvement for common keywords versus the historical 687.54-ms baseline, with some other differences potentially due to run variability; it is not a matched native control and does not close the performance targets.

The architecture comparison flags additional request-planning/allocation branches and two clone groups, with overall duplication percentage unchanged. Its broad diff also reports pre-existing feature WIP; review the actual prototype delta before acceptance, retaining necessary budget/cancellation checks rather than moving code merely to change metrics. The optimization remains provisional and R8 stays open: the 100-ms lexical target is still missed. Evidence, pre-experiment source backups, exact binary/source hashes and follow-up actions are in `.fitness/evidence/phase6-lexical-hints-20261031/`.
