## Smaller conservative lexical hints (2026-10-31, acceptance pending)

The initial full-path cache reserved too much vector capacity: at 125k files/187.5k vectors its longer broad semantic run regressed. It is replaced by at most 16 MiB of escaped basenames, with a 496 MiB vector allowance. The early check reuses the exact tier/score/natural-name comparison but passes equal-name ties and every retained basename; full-path ordering and all source/SQL checks still occur normally. Name-owner counts are request-local and only enabled for hinted lexical Top sets. Shared removal updates path/name ordering together; semantic Top sets do not maintain these counters. Invalid-byte names use the same escaped display representation as the browser, and oversized malformed catalog names bypass hints with bounded conversion temporaries. No source path, question or new persistent data is added.

Current 1,017 file-manager tests pass, including alias-fallback order/evidence equality and distinct-path basename ownership. At 100k files/150k vectors, 21-sample common-keyword p95 is 113.20 ms versus the saved control's 181.40 ms; first-query cost is 200.88 versus 174.00 ms. Default/broad hybrid component p95 is 264.46/306.73 ms. At 125k files/187.5k vectors, matched control/refined common p95 is 230.09/161.25 ms; broad hybrid is 441.87/449.58 ms. Every reference is scanned despite only 162,508 cached vectors, including the uncached final winner. Initial/rejected variants and timing tails remain retained. A read-only microprobe found only about a 1% difference using the FTS rank column instead of explicit bm25; it was not adopted.

These measurements justify the smaller cache as an improvement, while the 100-ms lexical goal remains unmet. They do not stand in for the refined native desktop run or remaining feature acceptance. Sources, before/after tests, fixed-corpus quality replay and reports are in `.fitness/evidence/phase6-lexical-hints-20261031/`.


The refined private dark CUDA run retains warm p95 exact/rare/common/broad-hybrid 598.87/616.35/610.41/503.47 ms (21 samples each), including its exact/rare tail regressions. The separate real CPU-fallback run passes the backend checks with p95 555.61/522.08/572.45/472.17 ms, closing only the two-second CPU gate. Both runs complete startup generation 2, preserve every canonical vector and pass their correctness observations. These do not close the lexical/CUDA hybrid performance targets.


## Answer columns use List presentation (2026-10-31)

The acceptance audit found an undocumented mismatch: question submission already switches to List through the shared `set_view` lifecycle, while the plan said to retain the current view. Retain this behavior deliberately and clarify the plan. A sortable question column needs tabular presentation; leaving a Photos/Music/Videos view active would hide the requested result, while adding a second table inside every media view would duplicate layout and controls. The normal view transition stops media previews and preserves file selection and the current directory/filter; it records List as the location's manual view. Returning to a media view remains available through its normal tab. This changes presentation only, not which files are eligible for answer work.

The acceptance driver now has an optional column-controls scenario using the existing private compositor, clipboard paste, parser submission and OCR helpers. It covers selector-excluded rows without hiding them, renamed duplicate definitions without consuming capacity, four columns and fifth rejection, forbidden recursive/indexed questions, header actions, released capacity and folder return. A component regression separately distinguishes retry from refresh across false, N/A, not-found, ambiguous, unknown, failed and unavailable cells, including cancellation and stale-response fencing. These checks do not substitute for the remaining applicability, extraction, resource, performance and result-action audits.

Both private themes now pass 26 observations for that expanded scenario, including Photos-to-List and preserving an existing name filter. All 294 optimized search tests pass (four ignored); fmt and targeted Clippy complete with unchanged existing warning totals. No production Rust behavior changed in this checkpoint. The architecture comparison reports regression with +139 measured test lines/+4 functions, unchanged maximum complexity/duplication/clone groups/cycle count; its scoped review accepts necessary coverage growth. Durable reports, screenshots, failed driver attempts and source hashes are in `.fitness/evidence/phase6-column-controls-20261031/`. A2 is closed for its declared contract; pending-answer close/accepted-write coexistence and the broader A4 lifecycle audit remain open.

## Bound retained indexing payloads across roots (2026-10-31)

The S4 resource audit found that bounding each reader reply did not bound all retained `FileWork` content: queued files could keep prepared text and completed segments while the two read workers moved on to other roots. The scheduler now admits at most two retained content-file jobs. Each job owns its permit across queueing, model preemption, checkpoint verification and publication; dropping the file releases it. Existing staged jobs and metadata probes remain runnable when content slots are full. The independent reader budget still includes cancelled/retiring helpers until they actually finish. This follows the existing owned inventory-permit pattern without adding a queue/executor or source persistence.

Two conservative 64-MiB reservations leave half the declared 256-MiB staging allowance for bounded reader/inference transport. Prepared replies are checked centrally at the reader handoff (8 MiB text, 16 MiB encoded media, 2 KiB location, 3 KiB coverage, declared modalities). Resumed/full-source cached segments validate borrowed SQLite cell sizes before allocating owned fields: at most 2,048 segments, 8 KiB text/4 KiB location per segment, bounded modality/hash/canonical vector. Their retained decoded data stays below 32 MiB; together with a prepared reply and ordinary allocation overhead it fits the per-file reservation. This bounds staged content, not total application RSS, metadata inventories, the separate vector cache or model weights. It may defer other roots until one retained content file completes; interactive requests continue to bypass background work. No source-format semantics, embedding pipeline, enrollment defaults or stored vectors changed.

The new regressions cover held permit ownership/reuse/release, blocked third-file admission with runnable existing/probe work, exact reply-budget edges and oversized cached fields before copying. All 297 optimized search tests pass. The full workspace passes 3,020 tests (zero failures, 38 ignored in 26 suites); release/fmt/Clippy pass with unchanged existing warning totals. Both native quota themes pass eight observations, retaining search at the one-vector limit and resuming the same generation after increasing it to two. Inventory-budget rejection occurs before payload/model work. Native text restart preserves its eight-segment checkpoint and completes all 392 passages over unchanged source bytes, with exact canonical-prefix and coverage checks. S4 is now checked for declared reader/staging/catalog admission; model/context/whole-process resource, PDF segmentation, media quality and performance gates remain independent. Durable source/report/catalog/checkpoint evidence and the accepted architecture regression are in `.fitness/evidence/phase6-staging-budget-20261031/`.


## PDF sparse-page whitespace and targeted policy revision (2026-10-31)

The S5 audit reproduced a PDF page with 199 non-whitespace characters and 202 trimmed characters after Poppler layout padding. The old `trim().chars().count()` rule incorrectly skipped its visual segment. The reader now counts Unicode non-whitespace characters, stopping at the 200-character threshold. An actual four-page PDF regression preserves existing sparse pages, the failing 199-character case and the dense 200-character boundary in both ordinary and text-only extraction. Useful extracted text remains available even when a visual segment is added; source bytes are unchanged.

Version this decoder choice in the PDF representation rather than changing the entire model pipeline/extraction version. Prior PDF representations are re-prepared, their full-source shortcut/checkpoint cannot bypass the changed choice, and unchanged text/image inputs can still reuse exact canonical vectors. Other formats retain their state. This is a scoped versioning deviation from a single global pipeline bump: the model and its actual input framing/preprocessing are unchanged, while only the PDF text-versus-image selection rule changes. Native older PDF backups remain importable: the recognized prior representation becomes pending with the existing empty-format sentinel, preserving vectors/IDs; unknown representations still fail validation. The import does not rewrite the backup or source.

The actual pinned CUDA tokenizer passes 46 passages across five synthetic short/multilingual/long-paragraph/sentence/unbroken inputs, preserving all 166,215 UTF-8 bytes, valid exact offsets and at most 768 framed/128 overlap tokens. Production framing uses `title: none` rather than a mutable filename, hashes the exact framed text and stores page/part plus UTF-8 locations outside model text. Existing overlap-cache reconstruction and real 392-passage checkpoint evidence retain literal text without duplication or missing ranges. Both new native themes repair a simulated prior PDF catalog: the missing sparse-page image is generated, both canonical text vectors are reused byte-for-byte, the dense page stays text-only and the unrelated text file is not reembedded. The counter is one generated/two reused. These are segmentation and reuse checks, not general retrieval/visual-QA accuracy claims.

All 299 optimized search tests, 79 helper tests and 3,022 workspace tests pass (zero failures; 38 workspace tests ignored). Release/fmt/Clippy complete with unchanged existing warning totals. Test-fixture directory capture is shared after architecture review, removing the first draft’s added clone; final measured clone/cycle/max-complexity counts remain unchanged while necessary policy/test growth retains the tool’s regression classification. S5 is checked for its declared segmentation/page-selection contract. Sources, failing-before regression, actual-tokenizer inputs/offsets, native catalogs, model hashes and scoped review are durable under `.fitness/evidence/phase6-pdf-passages-20261031/`. Media sampling, applicability/evidence, source freshness, general quality and latency remain open.
